    Review pass 10 (2026-11-07)¶
    Date 2026-11-07
    Reviewed 0.15.0
    Findings 10.01–10.95 (pass-9 identifiers kept where a finding was already known)
    Released in 2026.11.0
    Overview Audit history · Changelog

    The tenth review pass ran against 0.15.0. Its change concept was decided on 2026-11-07 and recorded in the [Unreleased] block of the changelog; the work was carried out by parallel work streams, each of which wrote its release notes as a changelog fragment. The release notes of 2026.11.0 condense these fragments; the concept and the fragments are reproduced here verbatim.

    Where this text comes from

    This page was moved verbatim from CHANGELOG.md when the release notes were condensed in 2026.11.0 (source: commit 30a122d). Only the markup was adapted: reST roles are rendered as code or math, relative links point to the current pages, trailing whitespace is removed, and each block stands under a heading that names where it was published. Paths, test names and numbers are those of the time; the code has changed since.

    Change concept (decided 2026-11-07)¶

    Planned — review pass 10 (change concept, decided 2026-11-07)¶

    Not yet implemented. This block records the change concept of the tenth review pass (2026-11-07, against 0.15.0) as decided on 2026-11-07. Findings are referenced as 10.NN; pass-9 identifiers (Fxx) are kept where an item was already known. Each item moves into Added / Changed / Fixed / Security / Removed below once it lands together with its test; whatever is still listed here when the release is cut moves to the Roadmap instead. Items marked ⚠ change numerical results.

    One release, calendar versioning. All items below ship together in the first calendar-versioned release. The version scheme changes from SemVer (0.15.0) to CalVer YYYY.MM.MICRO (e.g. 2026.11.0; month unpadded and no hyphen, because PEP 440 drops leading zeros and reads 2026-11 as a post-release). MICRO counts further releases within the same month. CalVer carries no compatibility promise, so deprecations follow a written policy: a deprecated name keeps working, warns with DeprecationWarning, and is removed no earlier than two releases and six months after its announcement.

    • Versioning and release tooling: scripts/release.py computes the CalVer version from the release date, validates everything before it writes, and runs git in the repository root; tags become vYYYY.MM.MICRO.
    • Security (10.01): evaluate_spec passes impedance_formula from a BusType JSON to sympy.sympify, which evaluates Python code. Replace it by an allow-list parser that builds the SymPy expression from the AST (numbers, f, rho, j, pi, + − * / **, sqrt/exp/log, size and exponent bounds) and validate on load. groundinsight (local 0.6.0) already guards its formulas; its guard is re-verified against the same attack corpus and against every formula groundfield exports.
    • Packaging (10.02, 10.11): no top-level scripts package and no release console script in the wheel (the same collision exists in groundinsight); py.typed; polars dropped (never imported); profile.out untracked; poetry-core bounded.
    • Silent failures become errors (10.06, 10.43, 10.51, 10.66, 10.67): unknown attached_to / return_to names, worlds without a finite current source, magnitude=nan, cross_section <= 0, non-finite resistivities, zero-length geometry, an ideal conductor with discretize_segment_length, and VoltageSource (currently solved to zero) raise with a named parameter and a suggestion; a cross section above 1e-2 m² warns ("mm² given?"); worlds beyond HARD_LIMIT raise before any allocation unless max_segments is raised; inert settings warn.
    • ⚠ Two-terminal sources and measurement (F01/F30, 10.16, 10.17, 10.38): Source.return_to honoured by every backend through one shared injection step; leakage-free junction nodes; measurement leads re-wired through them (replacing the 5 cm pseudo-rods); FieldResult.input_impedance(source).
    • ⚠ Cross-layer electrodes (F02, 10.15, 10.32, 10.50): one current share per electrode and layer in image_2layer, cim and solve_mutual_*; interface slivers merged; Galerkin self-term.
    • ⚠ Concrete encasement (10.12–10.14): one mechanism — the solver always treats the concrete shell as a distributed series resistance; the lumped model only converts its input. Today the lumped shell is dropped by solve_mutual_matrix, solve_mutual_field and every world without a PEN service drop while the inflated wire radius is kept (−3 % to −29 % on a foundation's self-impedance). OrtsnetzLayout honours the shells. An impact study on representative AP1 sites compares the old and new numbers.
    • ⚠ Exact line-source kernel (10.28): default reaction-matrix and field kernel; the point kernel converges 0.9–1.9 % below the exact uniform-current solution. Engine(kernel="point") reproduces earlier numbers.
    • ⚠ Earth return (F32, F50, F52, 10.20, 10.24, 10.35–10.37, 10.45, 10.46): carson_series on the anti-parallel image baseline; Sommerfeld λ-grid resolves distant pairs; exact n >= 3 buried kernel; Neumann inductance also on lumped and single-segment conductors; consistent internal-inductance convention; opt-in skin effect for conductor resistance.
    • ⚠ Post-solve consistency (10.25, 10.26, 10.33, 10.34): FieldResult.potential and solve_mutual_* use the engine's series controls (today capped at 100 terms); length-weighted solve_mutual_matrix diagonal; backend check and an optional reciprocal off-diagonal; line-source evaluation near conductors.
    • ⚠ Safety (F13, F51, 10.21, 10.22, 10.42, 10.52): touch voltage over the electrode footprint; uniform-current caveat; the EN 50522 anchors are confirmed against EN 50522:2022 Table B.4 (DIN EN 50522:2023-10) and pinned as literals in the test; docstrings cite the current edition; optional body-impedance / additional-resistance model and spectrum touch voltage.
    • ⚠ Fits and export (10.18, 10.19, 10.27, 10.40, 10.41): quantity= on sweep and the rho-f helpers with the input impedance as default; passivity check before export; robust vector fitting with a 0 Hz sample; standard form with optional constant term and relative weights; readable exported formulas.
    • ⚠ Engines (10.23, 10.29–10.31, 10.47–10.49): floating passive clusters in the mom family (reciprocal transfer impedances); cim weighted read-back; fem marked experimental with guards; duplicated layers merged; cim, bem and image_nlayer become deprecated aliases; shared engine benchmark suite.
    • ⚠ Generators (F07, F08, F28, F47, F48, 10.39, 10.53–10.55, 10.78): one network plan and one materialiser behind TnNetworkGenerator and the layout builder; reproducible resolved configs; rotated mesh foundations split at crossings; per-footprint foundations; lead depth honoured; bounded and validated Overpass requests with atomic caching; MD5 marked usedforsecurity=False (identical digests — stored masks stay valid).
    • Performance (10.56–10.65): row-chunked kernels (memory from 96 B·N² to O(N)), one reaction-matrix evaluation per solve, direct matrix assembly in solve_mutual_matrix, vectorised two-layer series (optional numba extra), multi-frequency Sommerfeld assembly, chunked field evaluation, sweep(n_jobs=...), performance and memory regression tests.
    • Architecture (10.74–10.77, 10.79–10.81): one kernel module and one solve pipeline shared by all backends; backend registry with a common metadata core and provenance (F22); versioned save_result / load_result (F23); nested backend options.
    • Usability (10.68–10.73): typed electrode factories, consistent parameter names with aliases, English names for German identifiers with aliases, curated namespaces (gf.plot, gf.safety, gf.fit), FieldResult.to_dataframe(), complex-valued compare_engines over all frequencies.
    • Standards (10.80, 10.94, 10.95): ruff format, ruff lint with the NumPy docstring convention and a physics naming allow-list, mypy, pre-commit, CI jobs for lint, types, docs and Python 3.14; tests organised by feature with a finding marker; supply-chain hardening of the workflows (10.03, 10.04).
    • Documentation (10.82–10.93): tutorials / how-to / reference / explanation structure, versioned tutorial notebooks executed in CI, glossary, units and conventions, warnings catalogue, validation page, known limitations, factories in the API reference, corrections to the 0.15.0 release notes (F47, F48 and F53/F54 were listed as fixed but the code was unchanged; cim is not bit-identical to image_2layer on unequal segment lengths; the F16 entry describes the symptom of F08), a concise changelog with a list of releases that changed numbers.

    Release notes of the work streams¶

    The fragments are reproduced as the work streams wrote them (from changelog.d/ of the release branch); only reST roles would be adapted, and none occur. Each stream wrote its fragment against the state of the branch at that time, so a later stream may have changed what an earlier one describes.

    Corrections (first adversarial audit of this release)

    Three statements in the fragments below were found to be wrong; the release notes of 2026.11.0 carry the corrected wording:

    • coup1, references.pollaczek (10.45): R′ moves by up to +3.5 % in band (100/10 Ω·m, h₁ = 1 m, conductor at 3 m, 1 kHz; +1.4 % for 1000/30 Ω·m, h₁ = 5 m, conductor at 3 m in the upper layer), not by “up to +0.5 %”; X′ by up to −0.09 %, not “less than 0.02 %”.
    • coup1, exact removal of the additive image (10.46): only kernel-level numbers are quoted. At solver level (default sommerfeld earth return, point kernel; two 1 m rods in 100 Ω·m joined by a 95 mm² conductor) Im Z moves by +6.0 % for a conductor inclined by 30° and by +18.4 % (50 Hz) to +18.5 % (1 kHz) for a steep riser; Re Z is unchanged.
    • core1a, solver structure (10.75, 10.79): “every previously importable name of groundfield.solver.image remains importable” is false for private names and re-imports: the _discretize_* helpers, _grid_segments, the electrode classes and PointSource that the module re-imported, _galerkin_solve of bem and mom_sommerfeld, private helpers re-imported by the other solver modules, and the private Pollaczek kernels of coupling.sommerfeld_inductance are gone.
    • Several fragments of the first waves describe intermediate states that later streams changed: the default kernel became "line" (core2b, after core1a), touching="exact" and the anti-parallel carson_series baseline became the solver default (coup2, after coup1), cim, bem and image_nlayer became deprecated aliases (eng2), the 180° counter-source of the layout builder was replaced by the junction-based measurement loop (gen2, after core2a), the two passivity checks were merged (fitx, after post and secio), and touch_voltage_envelope replaced mesh_centres= by interior= (fitx).

    Corrections (second adversarial audit of this release)

    The second audit found these statements of the fragments below to be false at the time they were written; the fragments of the fix2* streams at the end of the page record the fixes, and the release notes carry the corrected wording:

    • core2b, interface slivers (10.32): dropping an interface cut that would leave a part shorter than the wire diameter ignored the contact with the other layer (a 4.5 m rod in 1000/30 Ω·m: 195.0 Ω with its tip 15.9 mm into the lower layer, 128.6 Ω at 16.1 mm); every cut is now kept (fix2solver).
    • core2b, “a world without a source stays solvable through Engine.solve (all results zero)”: only fem did; every backend now raises (fix2solver).
    • The segment budget (10.06): conductor sub-pieces were not counted (a 100 m PEN in 2000 pieces was predicted at 12 segments); the guard now counts the nodal system (fix2solver).
    • Floating clusters in solve_mutual_* (10.29): not only an accuracy change; the cost grew with the square of the number of bonded nodes (256 nodes: 24.3 s → 199.9 s); one factorisation now serves all excitations (fix2solver).
    • carson_series on the anti-parallel image (10.20): wrong for a pair on opposite sides of the surface (an overhead lead 1 m above a PEN at 0.7 m: mutual reactance 35.9 % low at 1 kHz) and outside Carson's per-metre domain (fix2coup).
    • Lumped inductive conductors (10.24): under sommerfeld a km-long lumped conductor was one 16 × 16 quadrature (3 km at 1 kHz: +13.4 % earth-return resistance, +11 % at solver level, no warning) (fix2coup).
    • fixsec, “parse cost bounded” (10.01): SymPy still expanded symbolic exp and fractional powers of large powers (67- to 75-character formulas ran longer than 60 s), and a literal of 320 nines raised OverflowError (fix2sec).
    • gen2, a shell with ρ_c = ρ_soil (10.12): a shell of any size switched a mesh foundation from one current owner to one per wire (largest touch voltage 232.3 → 221.2 V at 100 A) (fix2gen).
    • eng2, deprecated cim “bit-identical on every world compared” (10.76): it is bit-identical to image_2layer of the same release; through the alias it uses the new default line kernel (core2b), so a ring of r = 2 m in 100 Ω·m moves by +0.85 % against 0.15.0 (13.31207 → 13.42534 Ω).

    Superseded by the third adversarial audit and the user's decisions

    These statements of the fragments below no longer describe the release; the fragments at the end of the page and the release notes do:

    • core2b, solve_mutual_matrix: offdiag=None selected "probe" when probe points were given. The galerkin off-diagonal is the default of every call; probe points need offdiag="probe" (ddefaults, user decision Q15).
    • coup2 and fix1, Sommerfeld assembly: “the default stays the per-pair assembly”, “"exact" (default)”. The default is "tabulated"; "exact" is the per-pair rule (ddefaults, user decision Q16).
    • fitx and fix3solver, touch_voltage_envelope(interior=None) sampled mesh cells and ring centres (and the cells of generated mesh foundations) by default. The default samples no interior; interior="meshes" does (dsafety, user decision Q3).
    • post, long_duration=True for 80 V beyond 10 s. 80 V applies to every fault duration above 10 s; the keyword is gone (dsafety, user decision Q4).
    • secio / fitx, rho_at_fit = ρ₁ for layered soil. The convention is the effective resistivity effective_resistivity(soil) (drhoeff, user decision Q6).
    • fix2gen, the nearest-point tree of PEN cables (chained cabinets, drops hung on other drops). Every cabinet has its own cable and a drop taps the cable that feeds its building (fix3gen).
    • fix2solver, “the step at zero penetration is the onset of the contact”. It is a limit of the thin-wire model; a rod end within one wire radius of an interface warns (InterfaceContactWarning), and the interface diagonal no longer clamps at h₁/200 (fix3solver).
    • fix2coup, wires lying side by side were switched to touching wires as whole pairs, from one segment's view (order dependent); the rule acts pointwise and order-free, and the accuracy statements “5e-11”, “1e-9” and “≤ 1e-4” hold only where they were measured (fix3coup).

    Stream infra: Release tooling, packaging and CI¶

    Changed¶

    • Calendar versioning: releases are numbered YYYY.MM.MICRO in PEP 440 form (2026.11.0; month without leading zero, no hyphen; MICRO counts the earlier releases of the month; tags v2026.11.0). The deprecation policy is stated at the top of this changelog. Test: tests/test_release_script.py::test_calver_version_from_date_and_tags.
    • Release tool (10.09): python scripts/release.py calver [--date YYYY-MM-DD] [--dry-run] derives the version from the date and the existing tags; python scripts/release.py set X.Y.Z takes an explicit canonical PEP 440 calendar version (pre-releases such as 2026.11.0rc1 included). The tool updates pyproject.toml, __version__, CITATION.cff (version, date-released) and turns [Unreleased] into ## [X] - YYYY-MM-DD, then commits and tags; it never pushes. The major / minor / patch bumps and the options --no-push, --allow-dirty and --allow-empty-changelog are gone. Test: tests/test_release_script.py.
    • Package metadata (10.11): metadata moved from the legacy [tool.poetry] tables to [project] (PEP 621; license as SPDX expression MIT, PEP 639); explicit classifiers for Python 3.12, 3.13 and 3.14 (the auto-generated 3.15 classifier is gone), OS independent, Scientific/Engineering, Typing :: Typed; build backend bounded to poetry-core>=2.2.0,<3.0.0. Test: tests/test_packaging.py::test_metadata_uses_the_project_table.
    • Typed package (10.02): groundfield ships a py.typed marker, so type checkers use its annotations. Test: tests/test_packaging.py::test_wheel_contents.
    • Development standards (10.94, 10.80): ruff lint configuration (F, E, W, I, B, UP, D with NumPy convention, N with a physics naming allow-list, C901 with complexity 15), mypy configuration with the pydantic plugin, pre-commit hooks (ruff format, ruff check, nbstripout, file hygiene), ruff 0.17.4 and mypy 2.4.1 pinned in the dev group, black removed. CI runs lint, types (non-blocking), tests on Python 3.12, 3.13 and 3.14 with pytest -n auto and coverage, a strict docs build on pull requests, and a non-blocking weekly dependency and workflow audit.

    Removed¶

    • polars dependency (10.11): it was never imported. It is still installed with the groundinsight extra, because groundinsight 0.3.0 requires it. Test: tests/test_packaging.py::test_polars_is_neither_a_dependency_nor_imported.
    • Console scripts and the top-level scripts package (10.02): the wheel no longer installs release, generate-third-party-licenses or a scripts package. Run the tools from a checkout: python scripts/release.py ..., python scripts/generate_third_party_licenses.py. Test: tests/test_packaging.py::test_no_scripts_package_and_no_console_scripts.

    Security¶

    • Package collision with groundinsight (10.02): the v0.15.0 wheel installed the same scripts/release.py and release command as groundinsight 0.3.0; installing both overwrote one with the other and uninstalling one broke the other. The wheel now contains only groundfield/ and its metadata. Test: tests/test_packaging.py::test_wheel_contents.
    • CI supply chain (10.03): every workflow starts with permissions: {} and grants each job only what it needs; every action is pinned to a full commit SHA with the tag in a comment; checkouts do not persist credentials (except the docs deployment, which pushes gh-pages); snok/install-poetry and softprops/action-gh-release are replaced by a pinned pip install poetry and the GitHub CLI; tag names reach shell code only through environment variables. The release workflow checks that the tag equals the version in all three version files, runs the full CI as a gate before building, and keeps PyPI trusted publishing. zizmor 1.32.0 reports 26 findings on the v0.15.0 workflows and none on the new ones.
    • Locked dependencies (10.04): pip-audit on the exported lock (all groups and extras) found 100 known advisories in 8 packages; after updating pillow (13.0.0), tornado (6.6), urllib3 (2.8.0), pymdown-extensions (12.3), certifi (2026.10.23), idna (3.20), pytest (9.2.0) and mkdocs-material (9.8.1) it finds none. numpy, scipy, sympy, pydantic, matplotlib and pandas keep their locked versions.
    • MathJax (10.08): the documentation loaded MathJax from a floating cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/mathjax@3 URL without integrity check. MathJax 3.2.2 is now vendored with the site (60 files byte-identical to the verified npm release, pinned by SHA256SUMS); the site loads no third-party JavaScript. Test: tests/test_docs_assets.py.
    • Release tool (10.09): the v0.15.0 tool ran git in the caller's working directory, rewrote the version files before it validated the changelog (an aborted run left bumped files behind), committed on a detached HEAD or onto an existing tag, and could not finalise a pre-release. It now runs git in the repository root, validates everything before it writes (clean tree, branch, tag, agreeing version files, non-empty [Unreleased]) and restores the files if the commit fails. Tests: tests/test_release_script.py (marked finding("10.09")).

    Docs¶

    • Formula rendering: the MathJax configuration started a second typesetting pass concurrently with MathJax's own; every formula was typeset twice (the copy lands in the hidden assistive-MathML region), and pages that autoload a TeX extension (\boldsymbol in ADR-0003 and ADR-0004) failed with "Cannot read properties of null (reading 'replaceChild')". Re-typesetting after instant navigation is now serialised through MathJax.startup.promise.

    Stream core1a: Exact line-source kernel, solver structure and memory¶

    Added¶

    • Exact line-source kernel (10.28): Engine(kernel="line") treats every segment as a uniform line current (Galerkin form of the exact line potential ln((R1+R2+L)/(R1+R2-L))/L, self term (2/L)[asinh(L/a) - sqrt(1+(a/L)^2) + a/L], images with mirrored end points) for the reaction matrix, FieldResult.potential and solve_mutual_matrix / solve_mutual_field. Against the exact uniform-current resistance (100 Ω·m) a 3 m rod, a 10 m strip, a 10 × 10 m foundation and a 20 × 20 m grid agree within 0.0004 % at every segment length from 2 m to 0.125 m (point kernel: −0.4 … −1.7 %); a ring of 5 m radius converges monotonically as O(ds²) (−0.60 / −0.17 / −0.044 / −0.011 % at ds = 2 / 1 / 0.5 / 0.25 m; point kernel −0.95 % at every ds). The default stays kernel="point", which reproduces earlier numbers; backends outside the image family reject kernel="line". Results record metadata["kernel"]; PointSource gains an optional direction (results written before this release load unchanged and are evaluated with the point kernel). For 16 house grids (1 920 segments) a solve with the line kernel takes about 2.5× the time of the point kernel with numba installed and about 5× without. Test: tests/test_line_kernel.py.
    • Geometry queries on the electrode models (10.79): every electrode kind provides segments(ds, layer_interfaces=None), segment_count(ds), wire_polylines(), bounds(), total_length() and min_feature_length(); groundfield.geometry.WireSegments holds midpoints, lengths and tangents. The solver discretises through them (midpoints, lengths and tangents bit-identical to the previous discretiser), so diagnostics, plots and exports can use the same conventions instead of their own isinstance ladders. Test: tests/test_electrode_geometry.py.
    • Optional compiled kernels (10.58, 10.28): when numba is installed the Tagg/Sunde series matrix and the far-field term of the line kernel are evaluated by compiled kernels that give the same matrices bit for bit: the series about 3× faster than the numpy path on one core (1 200 segments, 148 images), the far-field term about 5× (1 920 segments). Tests: tests/test_kernel_performance.py::test_numba_series_kernel_is_bit_identical, tests/test_line_kernel.py::test_numba_far_field_block_is_bit_identical.

    Changed¶

    • ⚠ Two-layer image series summed before the product (10.58): the series is accumulated image by image into one matrix and then multiplied with the currents, instead of one matrix product per image. Results of image_2layer (electrodes inside the upper layer) and cim move by floating-point round-off only: at most 7.3e-16 relative on the golden cases cim_rod_two_layer, cond_isolated_pen_sommerfeld_two_layer, defaults_two_layer_ring_auto, img2_grid_upper_resistive and img2_ring_rods_within_layer (all within_tol). The reaction matrices of solve_mutual_* and of the mom family are unchanged bit for bit. A 30-house two-layer network (3 669 segments, 11 frequencies) solved 10–15× faster in two back-to-back comparisons on the same machine (memory 1 236 → 219 MiB).
    • Solver structure (10.75, 10.79): solver/image.py (2 526 lines) is split into private modules (discretisation, guards, kernels, inductive assembly, topology, pipeline); solve_image and solve_image_2layer run through one pipeline and differ only in the Green's-function object; the Tagg/Sunde series is one ImageSeries object used by the solver, FieldResult.potential and solve_mutual_*. Every previously importable name of groundfield.solver.image remains importable. Numbers are unchanged (golden values bit-identical). Tests: tests/test_solver_structure.py, tests/test_image_pipeline.py.
    • The warning for ignored non-current sources of image and image_2layer uses the common text of the other backends; FieldResult.clusters lists the electrodes in world order.

    Fixed¶

    • Memory of the image-family kernels (10.56): the kernels no longer build (N, N, 3) difference tensors (96 bytes per segment pair). A 30-house network (3 687 segments) peaks at 115 MiB instead of 1 248 MiB and solved about 10× faster in back-to-back runs on the same machine; an 80-house network (9 712 segments) solves in about 2 s with 773 MiB (before: about 8.4 GiB needed). With rods reaching the lower layer of a two-layer soil (ADR-0007 path) the 30-house network peaks at 510 MiB instead of 1 250 MiB. Results are bit-identical. Above 1 GiB per reaction matrix the kernels switch to row blocks (results then differ by round-off only). Tests: tests/test_kernel_performance.py::test_solve_memory_peak_is_bounded, tests/test_kernel_performance.py::test_cross_layer_solve_memory_peak_is_bounded.
    • Reaction matrix evaluated twice per solve (10.57): the segment potentials after the solve reuse the assembled matrix. Bit-identical. Test: tests/test_kernel_performance.py::test_one_reaction_matrix_assembly_per_solve.
    • solve_mutual_matrix built the reaction matrix as kernel(eye(N)) (10.59): the matrix is assembled directly; Z_G is bit-identical and a 20-house two-layer catalogue matrix (2 466 segments) was 23–31× faster in two back-to-back comparisons (memory 604 → 146 MiB). Test: tests/test_kernel_performance.py::test_mutual_matrix_without_identity_product.
    • FieldResult.potential unchunked (10.62): large evaluations run in row blocks; a 200 × 200 grid over 3 687 sources peaks at 17 MiB (v0.15.0 needs about 96 bytes per point·source pair, 13 GiB here). Two-layer evaluations share every 1/r block between real and imaginary part and skip an all-zero imaginary part. Evaluations up to 16 MiB of kernel are bit-identical. Test: tests/test_kernel_performance.py::test_potential_on_a_large_grid_is_memory_bounded.
    • Point-source arrays rebuilt on every potential() call (10.65): cached privately on the result (not serialised). Test: tests/test_kernel_performance.py::test_point_source_arrays_are_cached_and_invalidated.

    Removed¶

    • Dead code (10.81): solver.image._potential_kernel, solver.image._segment_z_extent, solver.image_2layer._two_layer_potential_kernel and the empty module solver.field_study. Test: tests/test_solver_structure.py::test_dead_helpers_are_removed.

    Stream core1b: Backend registry, metadata, provenance and result files¶

    Added¶

    • Backend registry (10.74): groundfield.solver.registry with register_backend(name, solve_fn, *, capabilities, options_model=None, aliases=(), deprecated_alias_of=None, experimental=False, forward=None), get_backend, available_backends, unregister_backend, BackendCapabilities and BackendSpec. Engine.solve dispatches through the registry instead of an if-chain, so a backend is added without editing Engine.solve; the automatic forwarding of image to image_2layer / image_nlayer by soil model is kept. A deprecated alias warns with its replacement and the earliest removal (2027.5); an experimental backend warns once per process (ExperimentalBackendWarning). Tests: tests/test_backend_registry.py::test_new_backend_runs_without_editing_engine_solve, ::test_deprecated_alias_warns_with_replacement_and_removal_version, ::test_experimental_backend_warns_once_per_process, ::test_image_is_auto_forwarded_by_soil_model.
    • Capability check before the solve (10.74): a world the backend cannot represent (soil layer count, concrete-shell electrodes, an explicitly requested source kernel) raises BackendCapabilityError before any backend code runs and names the registered backends that can solve it; a stack of equal layers counts as the merged stack. A backend that solves the world with a simplification warns with BackendCapabilityWarning: fem reduces distributed and inductive conductors to lumped resistive branches, and every backend still returns the current of a source with return_to through remote earth. Tests: tests/test_backend_registry.py::test_capability_error_names_a_backend_that_can_solve_the_world, ::test_capability_check_runs_before_the_backend, ::test_two_terminal_source_warns_when_the_backend_ignores_return_to, ::test_fem_warns_when_it_reduces_a_distributed_conductor.
    • Metadata core (10.74): every result carries backend, effective_backend, kernel, groundfield_version, frequencies, n_segments, soil_kind, earth_inductive_model, solve_time_s, series and provenance (groundfield.solver.engine.CORE_METADATA_KEYS) next to the backend-specific keys, which are unchanged. series records the engine's image-series controls for the post-solve field evaluation. Test: tests/test_backend_registry.py::test_every_backend_reports_the_metadata_core.
    • Backend options (10.74): Engine.options, keyed by backend name and validated by MomSommerfeldOptions (mom_sommerfeld) and FemOptions (fem), with a did-you-mean message for misspelled names; Engine.backend_options(name) returns the options a backend runs with. Tests: tests/test_engine_options.py::test_nested_sommerfeld_options_reach_the_backend, ::test_misspelled_option_is_rejected_with_a_suggestion.
    • Provenance (10.77, F22): metadata["provenance"] of every solve holds the groundfield, Python, numpy, scipy and pydantic versions, the platform, the engine configuration, world_fingerprint (SHA-256 of the canonical world JSON, groundfield.io.world_fingerprint) and the UTC time of the solve. Tests: tests/test_result_persistence.py::test_every_solve_records_provenance, ::test_world_fingerprint_is_canonical_and_sensitive, ::test_fingerprint_describes_the_world_as_built.
    • Versioned result files (10.77, F23): groundfield.io.save_result / load_result write and read one standard-JSON file with the envelope {"format": "groundfield.result", "schema_version": 1, "written_by", "provenance", "result"} (no pickle, atomic write, no silent overwrite); a newer schema raises ResultFormatError. Tests: tests/test_result_persistence.py::test_save_and_load_result_round_trip, ::test_load_result_rejects_a_newer_schema.
    • FieldResult.to_dataframe() (10.69, 10.72): tidy table with one row per frequency and electrode (frequency_hz, electrode, cluster, potential_v, current_a, cluster_impedance_ohm); to_dataframe("sources") gives one row per frequency and source with the impressed current and the input impedance. pandas is imported on first use. Tests: tests/test_result_dataframe.py::test_electrode_table_is_tidy_and_matches_the_accessors, ::test_source_table_carries_the_input_impedance.

    Changed¶

    • Engine.backend is validated against the registry (a plain str annotation; Backend stays the Literal of the built-in names for type hints). An unknown name raises with the closest registered name as suggestion. Test: tests/test_backend_registry.py::test_unknown_backend_name_suggests_the_closest_registered_one.
    • Unsupported worlds fail earlier: cim, bem, mom and image_nlayer on soils with three or more distinct layers, and every non-image backend on concrete-shell electrodes, now raise BackendCapabilityError from Engine.solve and list the backends that can. It subclasses ValueError and NotImplementedError, the types image_nlayer, cim, bem and the concrete-shell guard raised before; mom raised TypeError for three or more layers.
    • One default for the image-series cap (10.74): DEFAULT_IMAGE_MAX_TERMS = 300 and DEFAULT_IMAGE_SERIES_TOL = 1e-6 (in groundfield.solver.registry) are the engine defaults and are recorded on every result as metadata["series"]. The image-series controls stay common Engine fields because six backends and the post-solve layer read them. Test: tests/test_engine_options.py::test_series_default_is_one_documented_value.
    • Engine JSON: options replaces the four flat sommerfeld_* keys; engine JSON written by 0.15.0 still loads, silently when it carries the default values. Test: tests/test_engine_options.py::test_v0150_engine_json_loads_without_warnings.
    • Result JSON writes non-finite floats of typed fields as "Infinity" / "NaN" strings instead of null, and tagged objects in metadata (see the groundfield.solver.result module docstring), so it is standard JSON and round-trips exactly.
    • Warnings of the pre-flight resolution check of Engine.solve are attributed to the caller's line instead of a line inside groundfield.

    Fixed¶

    • Lossy result JSON (10.77): metadata["penetration_depth"] lost its float keys and its inf, and numpy values in the metadata could not be serialised at all. Tests: tests/test_result_persistence.py::test_result_json_round_trip_is_lossless, ::test_numpy_and_non_json_metadata_survive_the_round_trip.
    • Backend options silently accepted for another backend (10.74): the sommerfeld_* fields were accepted by every backend without effect; options for a backend that will not run now warn (BackendOptionsWarning). Test: tests/test_engine_options.py::test_options_for_another_backend_warn.
    • fem knobs unreachable (10.74): n_radial, n_axial, r_far_factor, z_far_factor and far_field can now be set through Engine(options={"fem": {...}}). Tests: tests/test_engine_options.py::test_fem_knobs_are_reachable_through_engine_options, ::test_fem_far_field_option_reaches_the_solver.
    • Engine.solve let a backend rewrite the caller's sources (10.72): World.solve, Engine.solve and run_simulation now share one identity-preserving snapshot and restore of World.sources. Test: tests/test_solve_entry_points.py::test_every_entry_point_restores_the_sources.
    • Inert engine settings accepted silently (10.51): Engine.tolerance and Engine.max_iterations are read by no backend; a non-default value now warns. Test: tests/test_engine_options.py::test_tolerance_and_max_iterations_warn_that_they_have_no_effect.

    Deprecated¶

    • Engine.tolerance and Engine.max_iterations (no effect): a non-default value or attribute access emits DeprecationWarning; removal no earlier than 2027.5.
    • The flat fields Engine.sommerfeld_lambda_max_factor, sommerfeld_epsabs, sommerfeld_epsrel and sommerfeld_max_osc_panels: use Engine(options={"mom_sommerfeld": {...}}) or MomSommerfeldOptions. They are still accepted (constructor, JSON, attribute read and write); removal no earlier than 2027.5. Test: tests/test_engine_options.py::test_flat_sommerfeld_fields_are_mapped_and_deprecated.
    • World.solve(snapshot_sources=False): every entry point takes the snapshot now, so the opt-out saves nothing; removal no earlier than 2027.5. Test: tests/test_solve_entry_points.py::test_snapshot_opt_out_is_deprecated.

    Removed¶

    • The private, unreachable Engine._stub_result and Engine._dispatch (replaced by the registry; part of 10.81).

    Stream secio: Formula security and the groundinsight export¶

    Security¶

    • BusType impedance formulas are parsed, never executed (10.01): evaluate_spec passed impedance_formula to sympy.sympify, which evaluates the text as Python code, so a crafted BusType JSON file ran arbitrary code or exhausted memory (7**7**7*0+rho builds an integer of about 696 000 digits, 9**9**9**9 never finishes). A new allow-list parser (groundfield.io._formula) parses the text with ast and builds the SymPy expression itself: numbers, f, rho, j / I, pi, E, + - * /, ** with a literal exponent (at most 64 in magnitude) and sqrt / exp / log, after Unicode NFKC normalisation, ASCII only, at most 10 000 characters, 2 000 syntax nodes and nesting depth 100. Every number stays within 1e-300 to 1e300 and powers are checked before they are computed, which also stops towers such as (((10**60*f)**64)**64)**64 (the review prototype accepted this one after 24 to 46 s with a 15.7-million-digit integer). BusTypeSpec.from_dict and load_bustype_json validate the formula on load and raise EvaluateSpecError naming the offending element (a missing formula raises EvaluateSpecError instead of KeyError). All 384 formulas the 0.15.0 exporter wrote parse to identical values, so existing BusType files keep loading. The corpus tests/data/formula_corpus.json (115 accepted formulas with reference values, 79 rejected strings) is shared with groundinsight's guard. Tests: tests/test_formula_security.py::test_evaluate_spec_does_not_run_code_from_a_formula, tests/test_formula_security.py::test_every_rejected_formula_fails_on_load_within_bounds, tests/test_formula_security.py::test_every_rejected_formula_fails_in_evaluate_spec_within_bounds.

    Added¶

    • Passivity check before BusType export (10.40): to_bustype_dict, save_bustype_json, to_bustype and save_bustype_to_db evaluate the exported formula from 0 Hz to twice the highest fitted frequency (dense linear and logarithmic grid, the samples and the midpoints between them, points around every pole; for the standard form over the fitted rho range) and refine the minima. metadata["passivity"] records passed, min_re_Z_Ohm, f_at_min_Hz, rho_at_min_Ohm_m and the checked ranges. Re Z < 0 emits NonPassiveFitWarning; the new keyword require_passive=True raises NonPassiveFitError (a ValueError) and writes nothing. Example from the review: a 4-pole fit of passive data (rms 4.4 mOhm) has Re Z = -0.0194 Ohm at 5.507 Hz between the 0 Hz and 50 Hz samples and is now flagged. Test: tests/test_io_groundinsight.py::test_in_band_non_passive_vector_fit_is_flagged_on_export.

    Changed¶

    • ⚠ Readable exported formulas (10.73): vector fits are written as partial fractions with float coefficients, each rounded once to decimals significant digits with Python's correctly rounded formatting (identical on every platform), e.g. 28.4652054274 - 54.0434542796/(2*pi*f*j + 27.5749267026). The exporter no longer calls nsimplify (exact fractions with integers of up to 254 digits) and no longer merges the partial fractions into one rational function (sympy.simplify produced terms up to f**4). Formulas of 2 to 10 poles shrink from 273-622 to 126-469 characters, and the export takes 0.04-0.14 s instead of 0.39-2.23 s (same session, passivity check included). decimals must be a positive integer; values above 17 count as 17. Numbers move only at the rounding level, at most half a unit in the last printed digit per coefficient (round trip below 1e-10 relative at the default 12 digits). Affected: anyone comparing formula text, e.g. diffs of BusType JSON files. Golden cases: none moved (fit_rho_f_standard_sweep bustype/eval is bit-identical). Tests: tests/test_io_groundinsight.py::test_vector_fit_formula_prints_floats_not_long_rationals, tests/test_io_groundinsight.py::test_round_trip_accuracy_follows_decimals.

    Fixed¶

    • Actionable rho_at_fit error for vector-fit exports (10.84): the documented save_bustype_json(fit, ...) call for a vector fit raised "rho_at_fit is required" without saying what to pass. rho_at_fit stays required for a VectorFitResult (the BusType records the soil resistivity it belongs to); all four export functions now raise the same ValueError naming the function, an example call and the layered-soil convention, before a file is written or groundinsight is imported, and rho_at_fit must be positive and finite. Test: tests/test_io_groundinsight.py::test_vector_fit_without_rho_at_fit_raises_an_actionable_error.
    • BusType provenance stamp: metadata["source"] read groundfield unknown when the package ran from a source checkout (and went stale under an editable install); it now uses groundfield.__version__ (review item R5-12). Test: tests/test_io_groundinsight.py::test_bustype_metadata_records_the_running_groundfield_version.

    Internal¶

    • The groundinsight round-trip test for vector fits asserts 1e-9 (ADR-0008) instead of 1e-3; a new test compares evaluate_spec with groundinsight's compute_impedance to 1e-9 and skips without groundinsight (tests/test_io_groundinsight.py::test_evaluate_spec_matches_groundinsight_evaluation). The bridge tests no longer skip the whole module when groundinsight is missing.

    Stream post: Safety, sweeps, fits and plots¶

    Added¶

    • Prospective touch voltage (F51, 10.52): permissible_prospective_touch_voltage_en50522(t_f, *, R_H=0, R_F1=0, rho_s=None, HF=1, long_duration=False) implements EN 50522:2022, Annex B.4 / Equation (A.3): U_vTp = U_Tp(t_f) + I_B(t_f)/HF · R_A with R_A = R_H + R_F1 + 1.5 m⁻¹ · ρ_s and the body current of Table B.1. Test: tests/test_safety.py::test_prospective_touch_voltage_figure_b3_parameter_sets.
    • True-RMS touch voltage over a fault spectrum (10.52): touch_voltage_spectrum(result_or_results, world, *, electrode, currents, ...) scales the touch voltage of every harmonic with its fault current and returns sqrt(Σ|U_T,k|²) at one foot position (AP2 harmonic cases). Test: tests/test_safety.py::test_touch_voltage_spectrum_is_the_rms_of_the_scaled_harmonics.
    • 80 V for long-duration faults (10.42): permissible_touch_voltage_en50522(t_f, long_duration=True) returns the 80 V that EN 50522:2022 allows for current-flow durations considerably longer than 10 s (requires t_f > 10 s; math.inf accepted). The default keeps the 85 V end value of Table B.4. Test: tests/test_safety.py::test_en50522_long_duration_allows_80_volt.
    • sweep(n_jobs=...) (10.64): process pool (spawn, BLAS pinned to one thread per worker); rows in serial order, worker warnings re-issued, bit-identical to n_jobs=1 when the caller also runs a single-threaded BLAS. Callables must be importable (not lambdas or notebook functions; a clear TypeError says so). Test: tests/test_sweep.py::test_parallel_sweep_is_bit_identical_to_the_serial_sweep.
    • check_passivity(fit, f_max, n=...) (10.41, 10.40): returns a PassivityReport (passed, min_re_Z, f_at_min, as_dict()); dense grid plus pole frequencies plus bounded refinement for vector fits, exact corner check for standard-form fits. It finds the −0.019 Ω dip at 5.5 Hz of the review's 4-pole fit. The export bridge has its own check (SEC/IO); the two are to be unified. Test: tests/test_vector_fitting.py::test_check_passivity_finds_an_in_band_violation_between_samples.
    • Plot components (10.73): plot_potential_contour, plot_potential_profile, plot_potential_radial and plot_surface_potential take component="real" | "imag" | "abs" | "phase". Test: tests/test_plotting.py::test_profile_plots_and_labels_the_requested_component.
    • read_csv_with_provenance(path) (10.73): reads the CSV files of groundfield.io.csv and returns the table plus the provenance header. Test: tests/test_io_csv.py::test_csv_files_carry_a_provenance_header.
    • New names: UniformCurrentFieldWarning, NEAR_FIELD_DISTANCE_M, RhoFStandardQualityWarning, VectorFitQualityWarning, PassivityReport; fields RhoFStandardFit.k0, .max_relative, .weights, .quantity and VectorFitResult.rms_relative, .quantity; keywords touch_voltage(anchor=), touch_voltage_envelope(anchor=, mesh_centres=, return_points=), step_voltage(world=), vector_fit(rms_tol=).

    Changed¶

    • ⚠ Input impedance is the default of sweep, convergence_study and the rho-f exporters (10.18, 10.19): all take quantity="input" | "cluster" | "electrode" and source=. "input" (default) is the driving-point impedance (φ_attached − φ_return)/I_source; the v0.15.0 behaviour is quantity="cluster" for sweep / convergence_study and quantity="electrode" for rho_f_from_field_result / rho_f_standard_from_results (electrode_name is now optional). Numbers move wherever conductors carry current out of the feeding cluster: the cluster impedance of a station was 1.26 / 1.79 / 3.09 times the input impedance with 1 / 3 / 8 houses on a PEN, and the exporters fitted a ring with two bonded rods 17 % too high. Affected: groundinsight BusTypes exported with the rho-f helpers and sweeps of TN worlds; the AP1 catalogue is not affected (it computes its own driving-point impedance). With several current sources source= is now required (v0.15.0 silently used the first). Golden cases: fit_rho_f_standard_sweep (model halves: the two rods act in parallel through the PEN). Test: tests/test_impedance_quantity.py::test_sweep_default_reports_the_input_impedance.
    • ⚠ Standard rho-f form with constant term and relative weights (10.27): fit_rho_f_standard fits Z = k0 + k1 ρ + (k2 + j k3) f + (k4 + j k5) f ρ with k0=True and weights="relative" (1/|Z|) by default, reports max_relative (worst pointwise relative error) next to rms_relative and warns with RhoFStandardQualityWarning above 5 %. In ρ₁ sweeps of two-layer soil the worst pointwise error drops from 15–27 % (ring) and 43 % (AP1-like station) to 0.8–3.3 % and 6.2 %; homogeneous single-electrode sweeps are unchanged (k0 → 0). Exported formulas gain the constant term. k0=False, weights="uniform" reproduces v0.15.0 bit for bit. Affected: rho-f formulas exported to groundinsight, including AP1 exports (the stored samples are unchanged). Golden cases: fit_rho_f_standard_sweep. Test: tests/test_rho_f_standard.py::test_two_layer_ring_sweep_is_fitted_within_five_percent.
    • CSV files (10.73): the electrode and cluster tables gain a frequency_Hz column, every file starts with # key: value provenance lines (groundfield version, UTC time, backend, frequency, solve provenance when present), and the writers raise FileExistsError instead of overwriting unless overwrite=True. Read with read_csv_with_provenance or pandas.read_csv(path, comment="#"); provenance=False writes a plain CSV. Test: tests/test_io_csv.py::test_csv_writers_refuse_to_overwrite.
    • Plot labels (10.73): potential plots label the drawn component (Re φ in V for the default) instead of Potential φ in V. Test: tests/test_plotting.py::test_default_component_is_the_real_part_with_an_honest_label.
    • split_factor (10.18) divides by the source current of the solve (snapshot in FieldResult.sources) instead of the world's current source; the current-balance tables document that their impedances are cluster / electrode impedances, related to the input impedance by Z_in = s · Z_cluster. Test: tests/test_impedance_quantity.py::test_split_factor_uses_the_solved_source_current.

    Fixed¶

    • ⚠ Touch voltage anchored at the electrode centre (F13, 10.21): touch_voltage and touch_voltage_envelope measured the 1 m from the connection point, so for a 20 m × 20 m grid (100 A, 100 Ω·m) the feet stood above the grid: 9.92 V instead of 69.6 V at +x, envelope 9.92 V instead of 111.4 V; a 4-arm star 275 V instead of 818 V beyond the arm tip. The feet now stand distance from the nearest wire of the electrode's footprint, on the side given by direction; the envelope walks that offset curve and adds the mesh centres of mesh / grid electrodes. anchor="connection_point" reproduces v0.15.0; rods are unchanged. Affected: touch-voltage assessments of grids, meshes, stars, strips, polylines and rings (rings at +x unchanged). Golden cases: img_mixed_cluster_hom (safety/touch_1m: strip ×3.2, star ×2.1). Test: tests/test_touch_voltage_footprint.py::test_grid_envelope_covers_the_one_metre_offset_curve.
    • Uniform-current field caveat (10.22): touch and step voltages from the image family (one uniform current per electrode) near grids, meshes, stars, strips, open polylines and bonded clusters deviate from mom by up to +19 % (touch) and −21 % (step, non-conservative) 1 m outside a 20 m grid corner; rings, rods and closed loops agree within 1 %. touch_voltage*, step_voltage and touch_voltage_spectrum now emit UniformCurrentFieldWarning once per call when a uniform-current result is evaluated within 5 m of such an electrode. Test: tests/test_touch_voltage_footprint.py::test_uniform_current_backend_warns_once_per_call_near_a_grid.
    • EN 50522 permissible touch voltage (F44, 10.42): the test compared the interpolant with the constant it interpolates; it now checks literals from EN 50522:2022, Table B.4 (DIN EN 50522 (VDE 0101-2):2023-10). The module values were confirmed against the standard and do not change; the deviations quoted in the review came from a secondary reproduction. Docstrings cite the 2022 edition (Table B.4, Figure 8) instead of "EN 50522:2010, Figure B.3" and no longer call it a low-voltage curve. Test: tests/test_safety.py::test_en50522_docstrings_cite_the_current_edition.
    • Vector fit collapse with a 0 Hz sample (10.41): a 0 Hz sample with an imaginary part (measured or averaged data) made the pole relocation move a pole onto the origin; the fit collapsed to Z ≈ 0 (relative rms 0.99 in 20 of 20 noisy data sets, no warning) or numpy raised LinAlgError. 0 Hz samples are now projected onto their real part (bit-neutral for real DC samples), an imaginary DC part above 0.1 % warns, a relative rms above rms_tol (1 %) warns with VectorFitQualityWarning, and solver failures raise ValueError. Test: tests/test_vector_fitting.py::test_dc_sample_with_imaginary_part_does_not_collapse_the_fit.
    • VTK header injection and float precision (10.10): a newline in world.name corrupted the legacy VTK header, and float declarations quantised UTM northings (5.2e6–6.2e6 m) by up to 0.25 m. The header line is sanitised and limited to 256 bytes; POINTS and SCALARS are double; the z-down convention is documented. Tests: tests/test_io_vtk.py::test_world_name_cannot_break_the_header, tests/test_io_vtk.py::test_points_are_double_precision_at_utm_scale.

    Stream api: Factories, namespaces and soil parameter names¶

    Added¶

    • Typed factories (10.68): create_electrode has one @overload per electrode kind and returns the concrete class (create_electrode(world, "ring", ...) is a RingElectrode), so IDEs show the parameters of the chosen kind and mypy reports a misspelled kind or keyword. Every string option of the factories is a Literal type: ElectrodeKind, SourceKind and KernelKind (new, in groundfield.api), and Backend, EarthInductiveModel, ConductorType, SoilCoupling, InductanceModel where they are defined. mypy now finds all four typos of the review example (wrong kind, wrong keyword, coupling_to_soil="galvanik", kind="Current"); it found none before. Test: tests/test_api_factories.py::test_mypy_finds_the_four_typos.
    • "Did you mean …?" errors (10.68): an unknown electrode kind (ValueError), an unknown keyword of a kind (TypeError, naming the kinds that do have it), an unknown source kind (ValueError) and an unknown electrode name as a conductor end (KeyError) name the closest valid names. Tests: tests/test_api_factories.py::test_unknown_electrode_kind_suggests_the_closest, ::test_unknown_electrode_keyword_suggests_the_closest, ::test_keyword_of_another_kind_is_explained, ::test_unknown_source_kind_suggests_the_closest, ::test_unknown_conductor_end_suggests_the_closest.
    • Curated namespaces (10.72): gf.plot (all plot helpers), gf.safety (touch and step voltages, EN 50522 limit) and gf.fit (vector fitting and the rho-f standard form) are new modules (groundfield.plot, groundfield.safety, groundfield.fit); gf.io groups the export. They hold the same objects as the flat names, which keep working; dir(gf) lists the namespaces. Test: tests/test_package_namespace.py::test_namespace_is_an_importable_module.
    • create_engine(kernel=...): chooses the field / reaction-matrix kernel ("point" or "line", KernelKind). Test: tests/test_api_factories.py::test_create_engine_accepts_the_point_kernel.
    • groundfield.utils: suggest, did_you_mean, warn_deprecated, warn_inert, InertConfigurationWarning (a UserWarning), REMOVAL_VERSION ("2027.5"). Deprecation and inert-configuration warnings point at the caller's line and are not emitted when groundfield's own code is the caller. Test: tests/test_utils_helpers.py::test_helpers_stay_silent_for_groundfield_callers.

    Changed¶

    • Faster import groundfield (10.72): post-processing, IO, generators, the geo ingest, references, cross-engine validation and diagnostics are imported on first use (PEP 562); pandas, matplotlib, sympy and scipy are no longer loaded at startup. Median import time 798 ms → 223 ms and 1169 → 346 loaded modules (15 fresh interpreters, shared 2-core sandbox). __all__ is unchanged. Test: tests/test_package_namespace.py::test_import_does_not_load_heavy_dependencies.
    • Soil parameter names (10.69): the model layer uses the literature symbols — HomogeneousSoil.rho, SoilLayer.rho, SoilLayer.h (TwoLayerSoil.rho_1, rho_2, h_1 unchanged). The canonical world JSON writes rho / h; JSON written by 0.15.0 still loads. No numerical change. Golden: gen_layout_builder (world/json only). Test: tests/test_soil_models.py::test_world_json_written_by_v0150_loads.
    • create_electrode with an unknown keyword raises TypeError (was a pydantic ValidationError), like the other factories, whose explicit signatures always raised TypeError.
    • create_engine forwards only the arguments you pass; everything else takes the Engine default (single source of the defaults). create_engine() builds the same engine as Engine(). Test: tests/test_api_factories.py::test_create_engine_forwards_only_explicit_arguments.
    • Soil models validate assignment, and SoilModel is a union discriminated by kind (inferred from the keys when a mapping omits it), so a soil is validated once and errors name the selected model.
    • model_copy(update=...) of a soil model rejects unknown keys (a misspelled key was silently stored next to the fields and the copy kept the old value). Test: tests/test_soil_models.py::test_model_copy_rejects_unknown_fields.
    • run_simulation(world, engine) is documented and tested as world.solve(engine), with the same source-snapshot semantics. Test: tests/test_api_factories.py::test_run_simulation_has_world_solve_semantics.

    Fixed¶

    • Non-finite soil parameters (10.67): resistivity = inf, rho_1 = inf, rho_2 = inf, h_1 = inf, an infinite layer thickness or relative_permittivity were accepted; an infinite resistivity then failed inside the solver with LinAlgError: Singular matrix. They are now rejected at construction and on assignment, naming the parameter. Tests: tests/test_soil_models.py::test_soil_parameters_must_be_finite_and_positive, ::test_assignment_is_validated.
    • MultiLayerSoil structure errors (10.67): an empty stack, a single layer with a finite thickness, a half-space above the bottom and a finite bottom layer each raise a message that names the layer and the fix; a one-layer stack with h=None stays a valid homogeneous half-space. Test: tests/test_soil_models.py::test_multilayer_structure_errors_are_clear.
    • Inert settings were silent (10.51 part, F57): a non-default BoundaryConditions field set through the constructor, create_world(boundary=...) or a world JSON, and a non-default soil relative_permittivity, now warn with InertConfigurationWarning. No backend reads them: with every boundary field changed, all eight backends solve bit-identically, and ε_r = 1 and 81 give identical impedances (below 1 kHz the displacement current is at most ωε₀ε_rρ = 4.5·10⁻³ of the conduction current for ρ = 1000 Ω·m, ε_r = 81). World.set_boundary_conditions keeps its single warning. Tests: tests/test_boundary_conditions.py::test_non_default_field_warns_on_construction, ::test_boundary_conditions_are_inert_in_every_backend, tests/test_soil_models.py::test_non_default_relative_permittivity_warns.
    • annotations leaked into the top-level namespace (10.72): gf.annotations (the __future__ feature object) is gone. Test: tests/test_package_namespace.py::test_annotations_does_not_leak.

    Deprecated¶

    Old soil parameter names (10.69). They keep working as keyword, JSON key, attribute and model_copy(update=...) key, warn with DeprecationWarning, and will be removed in 2027.5 at the earliest. Passing an old and its new name together raises. Type checkers already report the old keywords (only the canonical names are part of the typed signature); at run time they keep working.

    Deprecated Replacement
    HomogeneousSoil(resistivity=...), HomogeneousSoil.resistivity rho
    SoilLayer(resistivity=...), SoilLayer.resistivity rho
    SoilLayer(thickness=...), SoilLayer.thickness h

    Test: tests/test_soil_models.py::test_old_keyword_warns_and_maps_to_canonical_field.

    Stream gen1: Generators: network plan, footprints, reproducibility¶

    Added¶

    • Network plan (10.78): NetworkPlan with PlannedSite, PlannedElectrode, PlannedJunction, PlannedBranch, ConductorParameters, PlannedMeasurement and PlannedSource is the pure-data description of one realisation (sites with concrete electrodes, PEN junctions, cables, measurement set-up, sources, soil). TnNetworkGenerator.plan() and LvNetworkLayout.plan() produce it, materialise_plan(plan) turns it into a World; build() / to_world() are plan + materialise. A plan round-trips through JSON and rebuilds the same world. GroundingSystemSpec.resolve_electrodes, plan_site and with_footprint expose the steps of build_at. Test: tests/test_network_plan.py.
    • Foundation outline of the layout builder (10.54): LvNetworkLayout.to_world and plan take foundation_outline: "ombr" (default, oriented minimum bounding rectangle of the footprint, the rule of the OSM placement of TnNetworkGenerator), "polygon" (ring along the real outline, DIN 18014, ring-style specs only) or "spec" (the fixed spec geometry of v0.15.0). Test: tests/test_footprint_foundations.py.
    • Explicit OSM building-tag filter: every building=* value is a footprint by default (also garage, shed and building=no), as before. parse_overpass_payload, query_and_project and LvNetworkLayout.from_osm take exclude_building_tags; groundfield.geo.NON_SERVICE_BUILDING_TAGS (carport, container, garage, garages, greenhouse, hut, no, roof, ruins, shed, shelter, tent) is the documented opt-in set. Test: tests/test_overpass_ingest.py::test_building_tag_filter_drops_non_service_buildings.
    • Reproducible generator draws (10.53): TnNetworkGenerator.build() and plan() accept rng= (seed or numpy.random.Generator).

    Changed¶

    • One network plan and one materialiser (10.78): both front-ends (TnNetworkGenerator, LvNetworkLayout) produce a NetworkPlan; one module (groundfield.generators.materialise) builds foundations and their concrete shells, PEN junctions, PEN and service cables, the measurement loop and the sources. The layout module (ortsnetz_builder.py, about 2700 lines) is split into the package groundfield.generators.layout (model, routing, measurement, penetration, plot). Worlds are unchanged: 18 of 20 reference worlds are byte-identical to v0.15.0, the other two list the same conductors in another order and solve bit-identically; the refactor alone passes all golden values in strict mode. Test: tests/test_network_plan.py::test_tn_build_is_materialised_plan.
    • English names in the generator layer (10.69): see Deprecated for the full table. Generated element names (trafo_*, kvs_*, house_*) are data and stay unchanged, so stored worlds and results keep their keys.
    • ⚠ sample_world collapses nested distributions once per realisation (10.53): as documented, cfg.sample(rng) now replaces every distribution of the config tree, also those inside the electrode specs of a building type, so all buildings of a type share the drawn value in sample_world. v0.15.0 left these to per-building draws while reporting the config as resolved. build() on an unresolved config still draws per building; plain presence_prob and jitter_m still draw per site. Affected: sample_world realisations of configs with distributions inside specs. Golden cases: none moved.
    • Overpass retries: also on HTTP 503, honouring Retry-After (capped at 60 s); max_retries is limited to 5. Non-integer timeout_s raises TypeError (it is part of the query text). Test: tests/test_overpass_ingest.py::test_retries_honour_retry_after_and_are_bounded.

    Fixed¶

    • Resolved configs replay their realisation (10.53, F07): cfg.sample() left distributions in nested specs and in building_counts; replays of the "resolved" config with seeds 0/1/2 gave 15/15/9 electrodes at 337/280/168 Ohm m instead of 15 electrodes at 331 Ohm m. Every distribution is now resolved (dicts in sorted key order), and sample_world(rng) draws the whole realisation from rng (the construction seed gave 5 vs 7 houses for the same rng). Test: tests/test_generator_reproducibility.py::test_resolved_config_replays_the_world_for_any_seed.
    • ⚠ Stable penetration mask without OSM ids (10.53): LvNetworkLayout.foundation_mask keyed footprints without osm_id by their list index; reversing the list changed the equipped houses and Z by 2.8 %. They are now keyed by their outline; footprints with osm_id keep their keys, so stored masks of OSM layouts stay valid. Affected: masks of footprints without osm_id at penetration below 1. Golden cases: none moved. Test: tests/test_generator_reproducibility.py::test_mask_without_osm_id_ignores_footprint_order.
    • ⚠ Layout foundations follow the building footprints (10.54, F28): LvNetworkLayout.to_world gave every house the same 10 x 10 m foundation at the footprint centroid. Foundations now take size, orientation and centre from the footprint (oriented minimum bounding rectangle), like the OSM placement of TnNetworkGenerator. In three test streets (8 houses, 100 Ohm m, 50 Hz, image backend) the substation impedance moves by 0 % (10 x 10 m squares), +0.6 % (12 x 9 m houses rotated by 0 to 35 deg) and +5.2 % (6 x 4 m garages alternating with 30 x 15 m blocks). Affected: every layout world whose footprints are not 10 x 10 m axis-aligned squares; foundation_outline="spec" reproduces v0.15.0. Golden cases: gen_layout_builder unchanged (10 x 10 m squares). Test: tests/test_footprint_foundations.py::test_foundation_size_follows_the_footprint.
    • ⚠ Rotated mesh foundations without the singularity clamp (10.39): rotated mesh foundations were built from full-length strips whose segment midpoints could coincide at the crossings; the solver's 1 mm clamp then made a 10 x 10 m, 2 x 2 mesh at 30 deg 11.8 % too high at a segment length of 2.0 m and 11.0 % at 1.5 m (no error at 2.5 m or 1.0 m). Crossed wires are now polylines with a vertex at every crossing, so crossings fall on segment ends as in GridMeshElectrode; ring-style foundations keep their four strips. The oriented bounding rectangle of a square reports the axis closest to +x, and a foundation at a multiple of 90 deg is built on the axis-aligned path: mesh foundations of squares and north-south rectangles change by +1.0 to +1.2 % (or -9.5 % where the clamp fired), ring foundations by less than 0.002 %. Note: with the uniform-current image backend a rotated mesh stays about 1.1 % below the axis-aligned mesh of the same rectangle (six wires with one current each instead of one); mom and bem give the same value for both, closer to the rotated one. Affected: mesh-style foundations on rotated or right-angle footprints (OSM placement, layout builder). Golden cases: none moved. Test: tests/test_rotated_foundations.py::test_rotated_mesh_has_no_clamp_and_converges_smoothly.
    • Jitter distribution (10.55, F47): ManhattanGridPlacement(jitter_m=Uniform(...)) raised TypeError ("Unable to apply constraint ge"); the bound now applies to the float only, a distribution is drawn once per site and a draw of 0 or less leaves the site on its grid point. Test: tests/test_placement.py::test_manhattan_grid_jitter_accepts_a_distribution.
    • ⚠ Measurement leads at their configured depth (10.55, F48): MeasurementLeadConfig.depth_m was drawn and discarded: buried and overhead leads ran on the same diagonal from the substation ring (z = 0.6 m) to the auxiliary rod head (z = 0). A lead now runs horizontally at z = depth_m (z > 0 below the surface) between the anchors and stays connected to both; depth_m = 0 is placed 0.1 m above ground (SURFACE_LEAD_HEIGHT_M; the surface plane is singular for the image kernels and the Sommerfeld kernel degrades within about ten wire radii of it), a galvanic lead above ground is rejected. In the golden geometry (perfect-mirror earth) the probe potential moves by 2.5e-4 at 50 Hz and its coupled imaginary part by +37 % (overhead) and +21 % (buried) at 1 kHz; v0.15.0 gave identical results for both lead types. Affected: TnNetworkGenerator worlds with measurement leads. Golden cases: gen_tn_measurement_overhead_leads (potentials up to 2.5e-4, currents up to 2.8e-4, lead end points). Test: tests/test_measurement.py::test_buried_lead_runs_at_its_depth, tests/test_measurement.py::test_overhead_lead_lies_above_ground.

    Deprecated¶

    • English names replace the German identifiers and capitalised unit suffixes of the generator layer (10.69). Every old name keeps working, emits a DeprecationWarning naming the replacement, and is removed no earlier than 2027.5. Configurations written by v0.15.0 (JSON keys kvs, source_magnitude_A, star_kvs, resistivity, thickness_m, ...) still load.
