    LvNetworkLayout.kvs_placements LvNetworkLayout.cabinet_placements
    from_osm(kvs_lat_lons=, kvs_xys=) from_osm(cabinet_lat_lons=, cabinet_xys=)
    to_world(kvs_grounding=) to_world(cabinet_grounding=) (also on the new plan)
    source_magnitude_A= of to_world, measured_grounding_impedance, verify_current_balance source_magnitude_a=
    result keys positive_side_A, auxiliary_side_A, expected_*_A, abs_error_*_A, tolerance_A of verify_current_balance positive_side_a, auxiliary_side_a, expected_*_a, abs_error_*_a, tolerance_a
    HomogeneousSoilSpec.resistivity HomogeneousSoilSpec.rho
    SoilLayerSpec.resistivity, SoilLayerSpec.thickness_m SoilLayerSpec.rho, SoilLayerSpec.h

    The module alias groundfield.generators.tn_ortsnetz keeps working as before. Test: tests/test_generator_naming.py.

    Security¶

    • Bounded, validated Overpass requests (10.05): timeout_s was put into the query text unchecked (a string could inject Overpass QL); NaN or out-of-range coordinates and radii reached the server; the response was read into memory without limit; an HTTP-200 payload with a runtime-error remark was cached as an empty result; a writer killed mid-way left a truncated cache file that broke every later call. Now timeout_s must be an integer in [1, 900] s, coordinates finite WGS84 values and the radius at most 10 km; endpoints must use https (http only for localhost); the body is streamed and capped (max_response_bytes, default 64 MiB); error remarks and malformed payloads raise OverpassError and are not cached; cache files are written atomically and an unreadable one is fetched again with a warning; a non-default endpoint is part of the cache key (the default endpoint keeps the old key, existing caches stay valid); the query text of valid requests is unchanged. Test: tests/test_overpass_ingest.py (mocked transport; one real request behind the network marker).
    • MD5 on FIPS systems (10.07): the penetration-mask hash called hashlib.md5() without usedforsecurity=False and failed on FIPS builds of Python. The digest is unchanged, so stored masks stay valid. Test: tests/test_generator_reproducibility.py::test_mask_works_where_md5_is_blocked_for_security.

    Stream eng1: Engines: floating clusters, cim, layer stacks, fem, benchmarks¶

    Added¶

    • Engine benchmark suite B1–B13 (10.76, R2 §3.3): tests/benchmarks/ holds reference worlds with analytic or independently computed reference values (hemisphere, thin-wire Galerkin rod, closed-form thin ring, Tagg/Sunde series, mpmath three-layer kernel, floating-port two-port) and one explicit tolerance table per backend (tests/benchmarks/tolerances.py; python -m tests.benchmarks.tolerances prints it as Markdown for the validation page). Skipped by default; run with pytest tests/benchmarks --run-benchmark. The image-family cross-layer rod (B7) is a strict xfail until 10.15 lands.
    • FemReductionWarning (10.23): fem names every reason why its equivalent-hemisphere reduction does not model the world (several electrodes in a cluster, several clusters in a driven world, a layer interface between the electrode and its hemisphere), once per solve. Exported from groundfield.solver.fem. Test: tests/test_fem.py::test_fem_warns_when_electrode_and_hemisphere_straddle_an_interface.
    • compare_engines tolerance table (10.71): EngineComparison.rows (one ComparisonRow per quantity, frequency and real / imaginary part, with values, reference, deviation, tolerance and verdict), frequencies, impedances (complex values per engine and frequency), input_deviations; new keyword component_floor (default 1e-3). summary() prints the table. Test: tests/test_compare_engines.py::test_compare_engines_tolerance_table_is_explicit.
    • mom_sommerfeld convergence metadata (10.47): converged (bool, true only if every frequency converged), converged_per_frequency, n_unconverged_entries and, when the two-layer diagonal is the Tagg/Sunde series (every segment in the upper layer), n_terms_used. Test: tests/test_mom_sommerfeld.py::test_solve_reports_convergence_in_the_metadata.
    • ComplexImageFit.max_error (10.49): maximum error of the complex-image fit on the verification grid including λ = 0. Test: tests/test_cim.py::test_fit_converged_flag_bounds_the_maximum_error.

    Changed¶

    • ⚠ mom, bem, mom_sommerfeld: source-free clusters float (10.29). A cluster without a source is now an unknown with zero net current (a floating equipotential) instead of being dropped and read back as the mean of the incident field. Numbers move for every world with a passive cluster in these backends: potentials read at a passive electrode by −59 % (rod through h₁ = 2 m in 1000/30 Ω·m: 3.548 → 1.440 Ω transfer impedance) to +1.5 % (100/1000 Ω·m), −0.4 % in homogeneous soil; the driven electrode's impedance by −0.009 % to −0.49 % because the floating neighbour now perturbs the field (R2 two-port worlds, 4 m apart). Passive networks joined by finite conductors carry their equalising current. Affected: probe and auxiliary electrodes, unbonded neighbours and passive conductor networks solved with the MoM family; the image family and the AP1 catalogue path are unaffected. Golden cases: none of the stored cases has a passive cluster in these backends (bit-identical); new case mom_two_port_passive_ring_two_layer.
    • ⚠ cim reports the length-weighted node potential (10.30), as image_2layer has since 0.15.0 (F10). Numbers move on electrodes with unequal segment lengths: −3.9 % (rod split unevenly at h₁ = 2 m: 71.060 → 68.262 Ω), −1.1 % (the F10 rod shape); uniform segments are unchanged. Golden cases: cim_rod_two_layer bit-identical; new case cim_grid_unequal_segments.
    • ⚠ Canonical layer stack (10.48). The layered backends (mom, bem, cim, mom_sommerfeld, and image_nlayer through as_layer_stack) merge adjacent layers of equal resistivity and drop zero-thickness layers before choosing a kernel: MultiLayerSoil([ρ, ρ, ρ]) is homogeneous and [ρ₁, ρ₂, ρ₂] is the two-layer soil it describes. Stacks that raised before now solve; mom accepts a MultiLayerSoil with at most two distinct layers; FieldResult.soil carries the canonical soil model (with the relative permittivity of the upper layer of each merged group), so potential() works for such stacks. Numbers move only for equal-resistivity declarations whose electrodes cross the declared interface (no longer split there): +0.07 % (mom, bem, mom_sommerfeld) and +0.42 % (cim) for a 3 m rod through h = 2 m in TwoLayerSoil(100, 100, 2). fem keeps the declared stack. Golden cases: none moved; new case mom_sommerfeld_duplicated_stack.
    • ⚠ fem: the truncation sphere no longer costs radial resolution (10.49). For a layer stack deeper than 1 m the radial line count grows with ln(r_far / a_eq), so the radial step ratio stays that of the homogeneous mesh. Layered fem results move up by +0.16 % (h₁ = 5 m), +0.41 % (30 m), +1.07 % (1 km) and +1.64 % (10 km) for a 1.5 m rod with ρ₂ = ρ₁ (from −0.16 % … −1.62 % to within ±0.005 % of the homogeneous value); homogeneous soil and stacks with Σh ≤ 1 m are unchanged. metadata["fem_mesh"][cluster]["n_radial"] reports the count used. Affected: every layered fem result. Golden cases: fem_rod_hom bit-identical; new case fem_rod_two_layer_deep.
    • fem is experimental (10.23). Module and solve_fem docstrings say what the equivalent-hemisphere reduction is and is not (no longer "the third independent cross-check" and "better than 5 %"); the registry flag follows with the backend registry.
    • compare_engines compares complex impedances at every frequency (10.71): cluster impedances and the input impedance of every current source, real and imaginary part separately, at all frequencies (up to 0.15.0: Re Z of the clusters at the first frequency). Engines with different frequency lists raise ValueError; a value that is non-finite for some engines only is a failure. Comparisons that passed before may now be reported inconsistent; deviations per cluster is the worst over all frequencies and both parts.
    • metadata["solver"] is "point_matching" for mom, bem and mom_sommerfeld (10.31) (was "galerkin" / "collocation" / "galerkin" for one and the same scheme).

    Fixed¶

    • Transfer impedances of the MoM family are reciprocal (10.29). Z_AB and Z_BA from two solves differed by 4.0e-3 (homogeneous) to 1.46 (rod through the interface); now below 1e-8 (cross-layer quadrature) and 7e-16 (closed form). Test: tests/test_mom_family.py::test_two_port_transfer_impedance_is_reciprocal.
    • cim is bit-identical to image_2layer and reciprocal (10.30), as its reduces_to metadata and the 0.15.0 notes claimed. Test: tests/test_cim.py::test_cim_is_bit_identical_to_image_2layer_for_unequal_segments.
    • Physically two-layer or homogeneous MultiLayerSoil stacks no longer raise (10.48) in cim, bem, mom, mom_sommerfeld and image_nlayer. Test: tests/test_layer_stack.py::test_duplicated_layer_stack_is_solved_like_two_layers.
    • mom_sommerfeld: a capped oscillation budget is no longer silent (10.47). When max_osc_panels binds, every rung of the refinement ladder shares the capped J₀ grid, so two rungs could agree while the absolute floor epsabs dominated: kernels 5 km apart over a 5 cm layer were up to 3.1e-3 wrong without a SommerfeldConvergenceWarning (7 of 1050 stress points). A binding budget now counts as non-convergence; the largest silent error on the stress grid is 2.0e-10. The truncated Tagg/Sunde series of the two-layer diagonal warns like in mom. Test: tests/test_mom_sommerfeld.py::test_kernel_warns_or_converges_when_the_oscillation_budget_binds.
    • fit_complex_images no longer reports converged=True for a fit that misses Γ₁ by more than rms_tol (10.49). The flag was RMS-gated: 26 of 300 random 3–6-layer stacks passed with a maximum error of up to 3.5e-3 between the fitting samples; the maximum error on the verification grid including λ = 0 is now part of the gate (0 of 300 silent). No solver path uses the fit. Test: tests/test_cim.py::test_fit_converged_flag_bounds_the_maximum_error.
    • compare_engines sees the reactance, every frequency and non-finite results (10.71). Perfect-mirror and Sommerfeld earth return (input reactance 0.600 vs 0.376 Ω at 1 kHz) were reported consistent. Test: tests/test_compare_engines.py::test_compare_engines_sees_the_reactance_at_every_frequency.
    • fem warns where its reduction is not a valid cross-check (10.23): +1092 % for a 3 m rod through h₁ = 2 m, −92 % for a 10 m ring above it, −35 % for a ring with three bonded rods, transfer impedance 0 between clusters — all silent before. Test: tests/test_fem.py::test_fem_warns_for_a_multi_electrode_cluster.
    • fem truncation (10.49): see Changed. Test: tests/test_fem.py::test_fem_deep_interface_does_not_degrade_the_resolution.

    Documentation¶

    • mom, bem and mom_sommerfeld are documented as one point-matching scheme (10.31): pulse basis, delta test at the segment midpoints, point-source near field. mom is not Galerkin and its bordered system is not symmetric; it lies 1.0 % (ds = 0.5 m) to 1.7 % (ds = 0.0625 m) below the exact thin-wire Galerkin solution of a 3 m rod and drifts by −0.96 % over that ladder, while image is closer at every ds; bem assembles the identical matrix; mom_sommerfeld shares the discretisation and is a kernel reference, not an independent scheme. The stale advice to lower lambda_max_factor on the delegated cross-layer path is removed (factors 30 … 20 000 change that kernel by ≤ 1.3e-8). No physics change. Test: tests/test_mom_family.py::test_mom_family_reports_one_point_matching_scheme.

    Removed¶

    • The unused private helpers cylindrical_radius and _split_layer_thicknesses of groundfield.solver._layered (10.81).

    Stream coup1: Coupling: image conventions, Sommerfeld, Pollaczek, Carson¶

    Added¶

    • Image conventions for the inductive stack (10.20 preparation, F32): build_inductance_matrix(..., image="none" | "additive" | "antiparallel") (the legacy use_image=True/False maps to "additive"/"none"), and the same image= argument on build_carson_correction_matrix, build_sommerfeld_correction_matrix and the Sommerfeld pair integrals: a correction states which image the inductance matrix it is added to carries, so j·ω·L(image) + ΔZ(image) is the same physical matrix for every convention. Carson's correction is documented as defined against the anti-parallel image; the defaults reproduce the numbers of 0.15.0 for carson_series and perfect_mirror. The solver switches carson_series to the anti-parallel baseline in a later step of this release. Tests: tests/test_sommerfeld_accuracy.py::test_total_impedance_does_not_depend_on_the_image_convention, tests/test_sommerfeld_accuracy.py::test_carson_on_the_antiparallel_baseline_matches_carson.
    • Multi-frequency earth-return builders (10.60, 10.63): build_sommerfeld_correction_matrices(seg_endpoints, radii, omegas=..., earth=...) and build_carson_correction_matrices(...) return all frequencies at once; geometry, λ grid and the Bessel table are built once per segment pair. Each frequency is bit-identical to the single-frequency builder. On the inductive PEN branches of a 2-house street network (34 branches, 11 frequencies) the Sommerfeld assembly takes 1.5 s CPU against 7.6 s for eleven single-frequency calls and 31.8 s in 0.15.0. Carson's P, Q are memoised on (a, θ) (bit-identical; 9130 → 2321 evaluations and 3.9× less CPU time on a 10-house network). Tests: tests/test_sommerfeld_accuracy.py::test_multifrequency_matrices_are_bit_identical, tests/test_carson_pq.py::test_multifrequency_carson_matrices_are_bit_identical_and_cached.
    • Exact touching segment pairs, opt-in (10.46): touching="exact" on neumann_mutual and build_inductance_matrix uses Grover's closed forms for segments that share an end point (chain neighbours, bends) or lie on one axis; the 16×16 Gauss–Legendre rule is −0.3 % … +0.8 % off for such pairs, and a straight wire split into n segments now keeps the self inductance of the unsplit wire exactly. The default stays "quadrature" until the solver switches. Test: tests/test_neumann_touching.py::test_chain_sum_is_refinement_invariant.
    • Per-segment internal inductance (10.46): include_internal of build_inductance_matrix accepts a boolean array. The package-wide convention (DC internal inductance μ₀/8π per metre included; compare with references at GMR = a·e^(−1/4)) is documented in groundfield.coupling.inductance, the Carson and Pollaczek references. Test: tests/test_coupling_conventions.py::test_internal_inductance_convention_matches_references.
    • Opt-in skin effect (10.37): Conductor(resistance_model="skin") with Conductor.internal_impedance_per_length(f) and Conductor.skin_correction_per_length(f) (the correction relative to the DC convention), based on the new groundfield.conductors.solid_round_internal_impedance (exact Bessel solution): 150 mm² Al has R_ac/R_dc = 1.231 at 550 Hz and 1.550 at 1 kHz, internal inductance 0.740× at 1 kHz. The default "dc" changes nothing; the solver uses the correction in a later step of this release. Test: tests/test_skin_effect.py::test_150mm2_aluminium_resistance_ratio.
    • Close, interpenetrating and crossing wires (10.44): groundfield.conductors.find_close_wires(endpoints, radii, names, ...), find_close_conductors(conductors, ...) and segment_distance report wire pairs of different owners closer than rel_threshold·(a_i + a_j), classified as crossing / interpenetrating / close (junctions at shared end points are skipped by default). Test: tests/test_conductor_proximity.py::test_parallel_rods.
    • LayeredEarth.interface_depths and LayeredEarth.layer_of(depth).

    Changed¶

    • ⚠ references.pollaczek is exact for two layers (10.45): the self and mutual impedances use the exact buried-conductor amplitude (lower-interface term included) and the K0(γ·GMR) head instead of ln(2h/GMR). R′ moves by +0.15 % (1 m) and +1.0 % (3 m) at 30 Ω·m, 1 kHz (homogeneous), by up to +0.5 % in band near a layer interface, and by +40 % at 1 MHz (Tsiamitros Case V); X′ by less than 0.02 % in band. Conductors below the first interface and pairs in different layers are now supported exactly. Affects users of pollaczek_self_impedance, pollaczek_mutual_impedance, loop_impedance_conductor_earth (the AP1 catalogue builds its own z′ table from these). Golden cases: none.
    • Carson P, Q (10.46): the asymptote is used only for a > 15 (was 5), where it is accurate to 1e-7; moves carson_series only for pairs with a > 5 (e.g. more than 560 m apart at 1 kHz in 100 Ω·m). Golden cases: none.

    Fixed¶

    • ⚠ Sommerfeld λ grid resolves distant pairs (10.36): the logarithmic region ended at max(p_skin, 1/z) whatever the pair distance, so J0 was unresolved there, and the linear region was capped at 4096 panels without a check: pair entries were off by −24 % (Re, 200 m) and +274 % (Re, 400 m), +2.35 % (Im, 400 m). The grid now follows the F14 policy of layered_green (log region below π/(4ρ_max), 16 nodes per J0 period, graded panels at the skin and layer scales) and raises SommerfeldResolutionWarning instead of truncating; entries agree with an independent quadrature to 1.2e-5 (R′ part) and 2.7e-6 (X′ part) for pairs 50–400 m apart. Golden cases: cond_galvanic_neumann_sommerfeld, defaults_mixed_cluster_inductive (≤ 1.8e-9), cond_isolated_pen_sommerfeld_two_layer (≤ 1.9e-5). Test: tests/test_sommerfeld_accuracy.py::test_distant_pair_entry_matches_independent_quadrature.
    • ⚠ Sommerfeld kernel exact for n ≥ 3 layers and below the first interface (10.35): the buried kernel used the single air-image form outside the two-layer/layer-1 case; a three-layer earth with ρ₂ = ρ₃ gave +17 … +25 % X′ against the identical two-layer earth. The kernel now uses generalised reflection coefficients (any number of layers, any layer) and the transmitted amplitude for pairs in different media. Affects sommerfeld worlds on MultiLayerSoil and conductors deeper than h_1. Golden cases: none. Tests: tests/test_sommerfeld_accuracy.py::test_three_layer_duplicate_equals_two_layer, tests/test_sommerfeld_accuracy.py::test_conductor_below_first_interface.
    • ⚠ earth_return_correction_layered had the wrong sign (10.46, F50): the reflected term was +8.739e-3 + 6.484e-3j instead of −6.346e-3 − 4.580e-3j (earth 100/10 Ω·m, h₁ = 3 m, 1 kHz, z = z′ = 0.6 m); it now uses the exact layered kernel. Test: tests/test_sommerfeld_accuracy.py::test_layered_point_kernel_has_the_exact_sign.
    • ⚠ Exact removal of the additive image in the Sommerfeld correction (10.46): the image was removed as −1/R′ integrated by the 16×16 outer rule with the direct orientation, so vertical segments kept twice the image in the σ → 0 limit and 50 m segments at 0.7 m lost 2 % of it. The correction now subtracts the very image block the inductance matrix added. Golden cases: see the λ-grid entry. Test: tests/test_sommerfeld_accuracy.py::test_transparent_earth_cancels_the_additive_mirror.
    • Segments crossing the soil surface (10.46, F52): an inductive segment whose end points lie on both sides of z = 0 was dispatched by its midpoint (World.solve solved silently) or rejected as "lying in the soil-surface plane". With an image convention it now raises a ValueError that says the segment crosses the surface (its image intersects it); with image="none" the Sommerfeld pair integrals split such segments at the surface and at layer interfaces. Tests: tests/test_sommerfeld_accuracy.py::test_surface_crossing_segment_is_rejected_with_the_right_message, tests/test_sommerfeld_accuracy.py::test_world_solve_rejects_surface_crossing_inductive_conductor.
    • Carson's Q asymptote had the wrong sign of its a⁻⁷ term (10.46): +45, not −45; together with the boundary move the large-a regime is continuous to 1e-7 (it jumped by up to 9.3e-4 at a = 5). Tests: tests/test_carson_pq.py::test_q_asymptote_a7_term_has_positive_sign, tests/test_carson_pq.py::test_large_a_regime_is_continuous.
    • Conductor input validation (10.43, 10.67): cross_section <= 0 or non-finite, non-finite resistivity / wire_radius / end points raise a ValueError naming the parameter (they failed late with ZeroDivisionError or LinAlgError, or gave an active element); a cross section above 1e-2 m² warns with CrossSectionUnitWarning (cross_section=50 meant as mm² was a silent 6.7 nΩ short); an ideal conductor with discretize_segment_length raises (it was LinAlgError: Singular matrix at solve time). Tests: tests/test_conductor_validation.py::test_nonpositive_or_nonfinite_cross_section_raises, tests/test_conductor_validation.py::test_cross_section_in_mm2_warns, tests/test_conductor_validation.py::test_ideal_distributed_conductor_raises_with_parameter_name.
    • Documentation in the coupling package: English prose in coupling/layered_green.py (10.95); the Carson layered-soil warning points to earth_inductive_model="sommerfeld" (it named backend='mom_sommerfeld'); the internal-inductance statement of references.earth_return ("external inductance only") is corrected. Test: tests/test_coupling_conventions.py::test_layered_green_prose_is_english.

    Stream core2a: Two-terminal sources, junctions and diagnostics¶

    Added¶

    • Junction nodes (10.38, prerequisite of 10.17): groundfield.api.create_junction(world, position=..., name=None) creates a leakage-free network node (groundfield.geometry.junction.Junction, World.junctions, World.add_junction / get_junction). Conductors start or end at junctions (create_conductor accepts junctions and their names) and sources attach to or return at them; a junction has no soil contact, takes part in Kirchhoff's current law and reports its potential per frequency in FieldResult.metadata["junction_potentials"]. Electrodes and junctions share one node namespace. A junction subnetwork without soil contact (no path to an electrode or a galvanic conductor) is rejected before the solve. Measurement leads that end on junctions at the instrument leave the soil problem exactly unchanged (fall-of-potential Z_meas equal to the lead-free value to 5.4e-16), where a probe lead ending on the station short-circuits the voltmeter (0.46 % of the true value); a junction leaks nothing where a 5 cm joint rod leaked 0.35 % of the test current in the test world. Worlds without junctions serialise exactly as before. Supported by the image family (BackendCapabilities.junctions); other backends reject such worlds with BackendCapabilityError. Tests: tests/test_junction_nodes.py.
    • World.check() (10.66): validates source terminals and conductor ends (with the closest names), the excitation, voltage sources and short-circuited two-terminal sources; called by every solve. Test: tests/test_source_validation.py.
    • Aggregated diagnostics (10.70): groundfield.diagnostics.diagnose(world, engine=None, *, result=None, proximity=True) returns a DiagnosticsReport of Diagnostic findings (category, severity, subjects, message, data) for thin wires, resolution, segment budget, close or interpenetrating leakage wires (groundfield.conductors.find_close_wires, 10.44), inert settings (conductors not attached at both ends, the deprecated shell registry, non-default boundary conditions or permittivity) and, with a result, series truncation. str() prints one line per cause, .warn() emits one DiagnosticsWarning. Close wires are a warning with the point kernel (two bonded 3 m rods 2 mm apart solve to 5.7 times the one-rod impedance) and information with the exact line kernel (0.998 times). Test: tests/test_diagnostics_report.py.
    • diagnostics.check_segment_budget and ResourceLimitError (10.06): the hard segment limit of the solver pipeline (see Fixed).

    Changed¶

    • ⚠ Source.return_to is honoured by the image family (10.16, F01/F30). image, image_2layer and image_nlayer inject +I at attached_to and -I at return_to through one shared injection step; the leakage currents of a world of two-terminal sources sum to zero and input_impedance() is (φ_a − φ_r)/I. Up to 0.15.0 the auxiliary electrode of a simulated fall-of-potential measurement carried 0 A and the station potential did not depend on its distance (13.107017 V for d_aux = 20 … 1600 m in the test world); now 12.310 V at 20 m rising to 13.097 V at 1600 m, and the probe at 61.8 % reads the remote impedance to −1.6e-3 (20 m), −3.5e-6 (200 m), as Tagg's rule says (0.15.0: −9.9 % and −0.98 %). Who is affected: every world whose sources set return_to — the measurement worlds of TnNetworkGenerator and LvNetworkLayout. The generators still wire the feed lead from the station straight to the auxiliary electrode, so that lead now closes the loop metallically (station potential 1.5008 V → 4.15e-3 V in the golden case); they are re-wired through junctions in the generator work (10.17). LvNetworkLayout still adds its 180° counter-source until then, which now counts the return current twice; the solve warns about such a counter-source. Golden case: gen_tn_measurement_overhead_leads.
    • ⚠ Source-free clusters float in the image family (10.29, image half). A bonded group of passive electrodes and a passive network joined by finite conductors now enter the nodal system with zero net impressed current instead of being dropped and read back as the incident field: one potential per cluster, equalising currents, and members of an isolated floating cluster report currents that sum to exactly zero (cluster impedance nan). Passive ring + rod bonded pair beside a driven rod: 2.5735 V / 1.8055 V and 0 A became one 2.4119 V with ±14.3 mA (mom: 2.4189 V, ±13.1 mA). A lone passive electrode is unchanged (exactly equivalent under the uniform-current ansatz), so worlds without bonded passive groups or passive networks are bit-identical. Who is affected: probe and auxiliary electrode groups, unbonded neighbours with several electrodes, passive measurement leads. Golden case: cond_coupled_leads_open_circuit (the grounded measurement lead beside the source lead: φ(m1) +42.5 %, driven side −0.89 % / −3.8 %).
    • One warning per cause instead of per electrode (10.70): check_segment_resolution aggregates its findings (docs example 07: 81 resolution warnings became 9, one per solve); the return_to capability warning disappears for the image family.
    • expected_segments follows the solver's discretisation (10.79), including wires split at soil-layer interfaces (a 3 m rod across h₁ = 1.3 m: 7 segments, the 0.15.0 prediction said 6). Test: tests/test_diagnostics_report.py::test_expected_segments_is_exact_for_rods_split_at_the_interface.

    Fixed¶

    • Invalid source references solved silently (10.66). A misspelled attached_to / return_to, a source on a conductor (documented as allowed, injected nothing), a source returning to its own node, a world without a current source, a source of magnitude 0, and a conductor end naming no node (the conductor dropped out of the network) now raise — at create_source / World.add_source / World.add_conductor, or when an edited or loaded world is solved — with the closest names. magnitude and phase_deg must be finite, also when assigned later. Tests: tests/test_source_validation.py.
    • VoltageSource solved to all-zero potentials (10.51). Solving a world with a voltage source raised NotImplementedError in the release preparation, naming the alternative (a 1 A current source scaled by U / Z_in); voltage sources are solved since 2026.11 (stream w2src, audit W2A-11). Test: tests/test_source_validation.py::test_voltage_source_solves_and_fixes_the_feeding_potential.
    • HARD_LIMIT only warned (10.06). A world whose predicted segment count exceeds groundfield.diagnostics.HARD_LIMIT (20 000, read at solve time) is rejected with ResourceLimitError before anything of the problem size is allocated, also in solve_mutual_*: a 5 m ring entered in millimetres (62 832 segments) fails after 0.06 s with a 0.67 MiB allocation peak; the wave-1 code solved the full problem (173 s, 140 MiB peak on the review machine) and returned 0.0096 Ω. Test: tests/test_diagnostics_report.py::test_world_beyond_the_hard_limit_is_rejected_before_any_allocation.
    • Polyline and star electrodes skipped by the resolution check (F53, F54). Listed as fixed in 0.15.0, but diagnostics.py was unchanged since 0.13.0; every electrode kind is now checked through Electrode.min_feature_length(). Test: tests/test_diagnostics_report.py::test_resolution_check_covers_star_and_polyline.
    • Close wires went unreported (10.44, diagnostics part): see diagnose above. Test: tests/test_diagnostics_report.py::test_close_and_interpenetrating_wires_are_reported.

    Deprecated¶

    • World.concrete_shell_corrections (the lumped ADR-0012 V1 registry, 10.12–10.14): the solver consumes concrete shells only as the per-length coefficient concrete_shell_coefficient_ohm_m of the electrodes and never read this registry (nor did solve_mutual_*). Solving a world with entries emits a DeprecationWarning naming the coefficient; the field still loads from JSON and will be removed no earlier than 2027.5. Test: tests/test_concrete_shell_registry.py.

    Stream core2b: Default line kernel, cross-layer electrodes, concrete shells, mutual matrix¶

    Added¶

    • Concrete shell on every electrode kind (10.12–10.14): rods, rings, meshes and grid meshes carry concrete_shell_coefficient_ohm_m like strips, polylines and stars. It is the one mechanism through which the solver sees a concrete encasement (ADR-0012): every segment of length Δs adds C/Δs to its reaction-matrix diagonal, in the image family and in solve_mutual_matrix / solve_mutual_field; with the uniform current per electrode this is exactly the lumped shell resistance R = C/L in series with the electrode. Conversion helpers in groundfield.geometry.electrodes: concrete_shell_coefficient(rho_c, wire_radius, thickness) (C = ρ_c/(2π)·ln((r_a + t)/r_a), 38.16 Ω·m for 100 Ω·m and 5 cm around a 5 mm wire), shell_coefficient_from_resistance(electrode, R) (C = R·L) and shell_resistance_from_coefficient(electrode, C=None) (R = C/L), which the generators use for concrete_model="lumped". Rods, rings and meshes without a shell serialise exactly as before. Tests: tests/test_concrete_shell_coefficient.py.
    • solve_mutual_matrix(..., offdiag="galerkin") (10.34): the off-diagonal entry Z_ij is the reaction T_iᵀ(K + S)T_j of the two leakage distributions — the potential of cluster i under excitation j, reciprocal to round-off without symmetrisation, and the matrix with which (Y_L + Z_G⁻¹)u = i reproduces the solve of the clusters joined by branches. probe_points is optional; offdiag=None (default) selects "galerkin" without probe points and "probe" with them, so existing calls keep their numbers apart from the diagonal (see Changed). On an AP1-like site (station ring + three rods, 12 polygon foundations, ds = 2 m, five soils) the galerkin matrix reproduces a network solve with PEN-like branches to ≤ 2e-5, while the symmetrised probe matrix misses the input impedance by −0.15 … −0.88 % and single entries by up to 46 %; for Z_G as a network element offdiag="galerkin" is recommended. Tests: tests/test_mutual_matrix.py::test_galerkin_offdiagonal_is_reciprocal, ::test_galerkin_matrix_reproduces_the_joined_network, ::test_offdiag_selection_and_validation.

    Changed¶

    • ⚠ Exact line-source kernel is the default of the image family (10.28): Engine.kernel defaults to None, the backend's preferred kernel: "line" for image, image_2layer and image_nlayer (also used by FieldResult.potential and solve_mutual_*), the backend's own kernel elsewhere; metadata["kernel"] records the kernel that ran and kernel="point" reproduces releases up to 0.15.0. The request is carried in the value, so engines rebuilt from JSON, with_frequencies or a provenance block run like the original. Numbers move for every default solve of the image family: rod 3 m +0.60 % (surface potential up to 1.9 %), two-layer ring +1.23 %, two rods joined by a galvanic conductor of 5 m segments: currents 10.9 %, cluster impedance 8.4 %. Cost: a 16-house solve (N = 1920) takes 2.7 times as long as with the point kernel with numba installed and 5.7 times without. In two-layer soil a straight 10 m strip stays within 0.05 % of its exact uniform-current resistance for ds = 0.125–2 m (point kernel: up to 22 %). Golden cases: defaults_rod_hom, defaults_two_layer_ring_auto, defaults_mixed_cluster_inductive.
    • ⚠ Electrodes through a soil-layer interface (10.15, F02): in image_2layer and solve_mutual_* an electrode that crosses h₁ (where the resistivity changes) has one uniform-current owner per layer, all in its cluster, so the current density may differ above and below the interface. A 12 m rod through h₁ = 5 m now equals the manual split into two bonded rods and lies +0.11 … +0.31 % from mom with the point kernel (+0.7 … +1.3 % with the line kernel, its offset from point-matching methods; 0.15.0: +566 % in 1000/30 Ω·m, +148 % in 1000/100, +123 % in 100/1000). TwoLayerSoil(ρ, ρ, h) stays bit-identical to the homogeneous backend. Who is affected: rods through h₁ — not the AP1 station rods (4 m from 0.6 m, h₁ ≥ 5 m). Golden cases: img2_rod_crosslayer (−46.5 %), line_img2_rod_crosslayer (−48.2 %), mutual_three_rods_crosslayer.
    • ⚠ Interface slivers and segment counts (10.32): an interface cut that would leave a wire part shorter than the wire diameter (geometry.electrodes.SLIVER_RADII = 2 radii) is dropped; the point kernel takes the Galerkin self term for segments shorter than 2.99 wire radii (identical above); segment counts ignore floating-point noise (ceil(L/ds − 1e-9)): a star arm of 3.000000000000001 m is cut into 6, not 7 segments at ds = 0.5 m (≤ 6.9e-5 point kernel, ≤ 8.3e-8 line kernel). Golden cases: img_mixed_cluster_hom, line_img_mixed_cluster_hom.
    • ⚠ Post-solve series controls follow the engine (10.25): FieldResult.potential, solve_mutual_matrix and solve_mutual_field sum the Tagg/Sunde series with the controls recorded with the solve (metadata["series"], the engine defaults for results without the record) instead of a hard-coded 100 terms / 1e-6, and warn with SeriesTruncationWarning when the series is truncated. For K = −0.94 and image_max_terms=2000 the potential 100 m from a ring moved by 0.8 %. The backend entry points solve_image_2layer / solve_image_nlayer take the engine's controls instead of private defaults 100 / 200 (10.74). Golden cases: none (the cases use the default 300 terms).
    • ⚠ solve_mutual_matrix diagonal (10.26): Z[j, j] is the cluster impedance reduced like FieldResult.cluster_impedance — length-weighted over every member and every layer owner — instead of the unweighted mean over the alphabetically first member; equal to the per-excitation solve to ≤ 2.2e-16. 0.15.0 deviated by +0.150 % for an L-shaped footprint at ds = 2 m and by −3.8 / −9.1 % for a rod split at h₁. AP1-like site (ds = 2 m): house foundations up to 0.10 %, station 0.000 %. Golden cases: mutual_* (round-off; mutual_three_rods_crosslayer see 10.15).
    • solve_mutual_matrix / solve_mutual_field check the backend (10.34): the engine's backend is resolved and checked like in Engine.solve (aliases, capability check, kernel); a backend outside the image family raises BackendCapabilityError (a ValueError) instead of returning image numbers (an 8-arm star: 7.854 Ω returned for a mom engine whose own solve gives 7.128 Ω). Test: tests/test_mutual_matrix.py::test_non_image_backend_is_rejected.
    • ⚠ solve_mutual_matrix / solve_mutual_field: the other clusters float (10.29): each excitation is solved with the source-free clusters floating (zero net current, equalising currents in bonded groups and between the layer owners of a rod through h₁), as Engine.solve does since 10.29; the galerkin off-diagonal is then the exact potential of the passive cluster. AP1-like site: diagonal 3.3e-5, galerkin off-diagonal up to 0.09 %. Golden case: mutual_foundations_distributed_concrete (Z_G 5.2e-4, R 1.6 %).
    • Engine.solve validates the world (10.66): it runs World.check(require_source=False) for every backend and entry point (dangling references, short-circuited sources, zero or non-finite magnitudes); a world without a source stays solvable through Engine.solve (all results zero), as before.
    • Cross-layer solves report no image series (10.50, F43): a solve on the ADR-0007 path (an electrode reaches h₁) no longer reports the term count or the truncation of a series it never evaluates; metadata gains series_used and series. Test: tests/test_series_controls.py::test_cross_layer_solve_reports_no_series.

    Fixed¶

    • Degenerate electrode geometry (10.67): zero-length strips, non-positive mesh or grid extents, non-finite coordinates or lengths and dimensions not longer than the wire radius (typically a radius typed in mm) are rejected when the electrode is built; up to 0.15.0 they failed in the solver as a thin-wire violation, an OverflowError, or solved to Z = nan. Test: tests/test_electrode_validation.py.
    • Reciprocity of the line kernel across the layer interface (10.28): the cross-layer reaction matrix scaled the line corrections of the direct and surface-image terms with the source-layer resistivity while the layered correction kept the compensating terms in point form: 3.8 % asymmetric, and a two-port of a rod through h₁ and a ring non-reciprocal by 1.1–4.7 % (point kernel: 1e-8). Pairs in different layers now carry the transmitted resistivity 2ρ₁ρ₂/(ρ₁ + ρ₂), and the near-singular interface image of the layered correction is a Galerkin integral: symmetric to ~1e-8, and a rod through h₁ changes by less than 0.01 % per halving of ds (was up to 0.22 %). The interface image matters most for horizontal wires just below h₁: a ring (r = 3 m) 5 cm below the interface of 100 over 1000 Ω·m now gives 55.6–55.7 Ω at every ds from 0.5 to 0.025 m; the point kernel (and mom, and the line kernel before) gave 29.7 Ω at ds = 0.5 m (−46 %), converging to 54.7 Ω only at ds = 0.025 m. Keep ds well below 2|z − h₁| with kernel="point". Tests: tests/test_reciprocity.py, tests/test_cross_layer_line_kernel.py.
    • image_2layer with a layer stack (10.48): a MultiLayerSoil with two distinct layers raised a bare TypeError after passing the capability check; it now solves its canonical two-layer form, three distinct layers raise BackendCapabilityError. Test: tests/test_layer_stack_dispatch.py.
    • Interface slivers (10.32): a rod whose tip lay 1 mm below h₁ raised a ValueError, one 9 mm below acted as a short in mom (196.6 → 144.2 → 166.8 Ω for tips at 4.99 / 5.009 / 5.02 m). Test: tests/test_interface_slivers.py.
    • Junction terminals in FieldResult.input_impedance (10.38): a source attached to or returning at a junction reads the junction potential. Test: tests/test_junction_nodes.py::test_input_impedance_of_a_source_on_a_junction.
    • Line-kernel cost with one long segment (10.28): the near-pair search used the longest segment of the whole set as radius for every observer; one conductor discretised at 50 m turned a 16-house line solve from 0.8 s into 29 s and 1.7 GiB. Each segment now searches with its own radius (identical results); the reaction corrections of 3000 short plus three 35 m segments peak below 40 MiB (940.9 MiB before). Test: tests/test_line_kernel_near_pairs.py.
    • Two-layer mom assembled its series matrix twice per solve: the matrix is kept from the reaction-matrix assembly (bit-identical; 20 × 20 m grid, N = 400: 0.294 s → 0.154 s).

    Stream eng2: Shared point-matching pipeline, Sommerfeld tabulation, fem guards¶

    Added¶

    • mom_sommerfeld kernel tabulation report (10.61): metadata["kernel_tabulation"] (enabled, groups_tabulated, groups_pointwise, table_evaluations, max_check_error) and the keyword solve_mom_sommerfeld(..., tabulate=True); through the engine as options={"mom_sommerfeld": {"tabulate": False}} once the registry entry MomSommerfeldOptions.tabulate is in place. converged and converged_per_frequency (10.47) are kept. Test: tests/test_mom_sommerfeld.py::test_solve_reports_the_kernel_tabulation.
    • Benchmarks B12 and B13 (10.76, R2 §3.3): B12 checks the depth-group table of mom_sommerfeld against the pointwise quadrature on a 40 m grid in 1000/30/200 Ω·m (bound 1e-6; measured 0 at ds = 2.5 m, 6.9e-9 for single entries and 1.5e-10 for the grid impedance at ds = 1.25 m). B13 compares the per-metre series impedance of an isolated line (difference of a 1600 m and an 800 m line at 1 kHz, which cancels the end effects) with the Pollaczek conductor–earth loop impedance (reactance bound 5e-3, measured 4.1e-6; resistance bound 2e-2, measured −1.04 %). B7 keeps the strict xfail of image_2layer until 10.15 and records that fem raises. python -m tests.benchmarks.tolerances prints the table.
    • compare_engines names engines that ran the same computation (10.76): a deprecated name and its replacement with identical settings (cim and image_2layer, bem and mom) get a note that their agreement is not an independent cross-check. Test: tests/test_engine_aliases.py::test_compare_engines_notes_that_an_alias_is_no_independent_check.

    Changed¶

    • mom, mom_sommerfeld and bem run the shared solve pipeline (10.75, 10.59). Discretisation, injection, topology and branch assembly are the steps of groundfield.solver._pipeline; the family keeps one shared point-matching solve and read-back, each backend contributes only its Green's function, and the reaction matrix is assembled directly instead of as the product of the kernel with the identity. The numbers are unchanged: 41 of 41 stored golden cases are bit-identical to the wave-2 base, and so are the numeric fields of 47 further results except bem (≤ 3.5e-16, it now runs mom) and the tabulated three-layer case of 10.61 (1.1e-9). The direct assembly makes mom_sommerfeld on two-layer soil faster (40 m grid, ds = 2.5 m: 15.2 → 0.41 s).
    • ⚠ mom and mom_sommerfeld honour Source.return_to and junction nodes (10.75 with 10.16 / 10.38). Because they now use the shared injection and topology steps, the return terminal of a two-terminal source draws the current back through its electrode (0.15.0: ignored, the current returned through remote earth), and metadata["junction_potentials"] reports junction nodes as the image family does. Numbers change for every world with a return_to source solved with these backends; no stored golden case contains one. Needs the registry flags two_terminal_sources and junctions for both backends. Tests: tests/test_point_matching_pipeline.py::test_mom_family_honours_return_to, tests/test_point_matching_pipeline.py::test_mom_family_reports_the_junction_potential.
    • ⚠ mom_sommerfeld tabulates the reflected kernel for n ≥ 3 layers (10.61). Segment pairs of one depth class in the top layer use a cubic spline in ln s (64 nodes per decade) instead of one quadrature per pair; eight midpoints per table are checked against the quadrature (tolerance 1e-7 of |G|, one refinement), and a group whose check fails or whose nodes do not converge falls back to the quadrature with the same convergence report. Every other pair is evaluated once per distinct (s, |Δz|, z + z') instead of once per pair, bit-identically. Results move where a table is used, by ≤ 1.1e-9 relative for the impedances measured and ≤ 6.9e-9 for single matrix entries (40 m grid at ds = 1.25 m: 4.8703316096 → 4.8703316089 Ω). Three-layer 40 m grid: 19.0 → 0.25 s (ds = 2.5 m, deduplication only, bit-identical) and 66.6 → 0.30 s (ds = 1.25 m, tabulated); a village of eleven rings at irregular positions (N = 273) takes 1.25 s instead of 34.1 s with the deduplication alone (impedance 1.8e-11 apart). Golden cases: none of the stored ones moves; new case mom_sommerfeld_three_layer_grid_tabulated. Test: tests/test_mom_sommerfeld.py::test_tabulated_reaction_matrix_is_within_1e_6_of_the_quadrature.
    • ⚠ fem raises when an electrode reaches a layer its hemisphere does not (10.23). An electrode below a layer interface that lies above the equivalent-hemisphere radius (a rod through or below the top layer) now raises BackendCapabilityError (a ValueError and NotImplementedError) before meshing. 0.15.0 returned +633 … +3138 % over a conductive lower layer (1000/30 Ω·m) and −10 … −85 % over a resistive one silently. Affected: rods in layered soil solved with fem; golden cases: none. Test: tests/test_fem.py::test_fem_rejects_an_electrode_below_its_hemisphere.
    • The mom family shares the depth advisories of the image family (10.75): ShallowSegmentWarning for segments long against their burial depth.

    Fixed¶

    • mom, mom_sommerfeld, bem solved a segment that crosses the soil surface as if it were buried (10.75): a 1 m rod from z = −0.1 m gave 94.27 Ω; the shared depth guard now raises as it has for image since 0.15.0. Test: tests/test_point_matching_pipeline.py::test_mom_family_rejects_a_segment_partly_above_the_surface.
    • FemReductionWarning missed interfaces above the electrode (10.23, audit F01): the hemisphere spans the soil from the surface, but only interfaces between the electrode and the hemisphere radius were checked; a 10 m strip and a ring r = 5 m at 0.8 m below h₁ = 0.5 m (30/1000 Ω·m) came out −70 % and −47 % against mom without a warning. Every interface above the deeper of the electrode and its hemisphere is now reported, and so is an interface below both but within the electrode's horizontal half-extent (ring r = 10 m over h₁ = 5 m in 1000/30 Ω·m: −46 %, silent before). Where no rule applies, rings and strips measured in 1000/30, 100/1000 and 100/400 Ω·m stay within −7.4 … +4.3 % of mom. Tests: tests/test_fem.py::test_fem_warns_when_the_electrode_lies_below_a_thin_top_layer, tests/test_fem.py::test_fem_warns_when_an_interface_lies_within_the_electrode_extent.
    • compare_engines reported 34.3 Ω against 0 Ω as consistent (10.71, audit F03): a quantity was skipped as "undefined for every engine" as soon as one engine returned zero. Only a quantity that is zero for every engine is skipped now; a zero for some engines fails with an infinite deviation and a note. Test: tests/test_compare_engines.py::test_compare_engines_counts_a_zero_of_one_engine_as_failure.

    Deprecated¶

    • Backend names cim, bem and image_nlayer (10.76). They keep working, emit a DeprecationWarning naming the replacement and are removed no earlier than 2027.5: cim runs the image auto-dispatch (image, image_2layer; bit-identical on every world compared), bem runs mom (it assembled the identical point-matching system; results move by ≤ 3.5e-16), image_nlayer is the name of the auto-dispatch target of image for MultiLayerSoil and keeps its own solve function, so Engine(backend="image") stays silent. Three or more distinct layers raise and name mom_sommerfeld. As a pure alias, cim has the capabilities of image: concrete-shell electrodes and kernel="line", which it rejected before, now run. The modules groundfield.solver.cim (with fit_complex_images) and groundfield.solver.bem stay importable; solve_cim and solve_bem warn and delegate. Their results carry the metadata of the backend that ran: cim_fit_used, cim_n_images, cim_rms, reduces_to, n_layers, h and rhos are gone, a direct call adds dispatched_to. fem is registered as experimental (ExperimentalBackendWarning, once per process). The registry entries are applied by the release coordinator. Tests: tests/test_engine_aliases.py.

    Removed¶

    • Private helpers without a remaining caller: groundfield.solver.mom._carson_skin_metadata, groundfield.solver.bem._build_Z_collocation, groundfield.solver.cim._cim_self_kernel_factory; the re-imports of image-family helpers from mom, mom_sommerfeld, bem and cim.

    Stream coup2: Carson baseline, skin effect, touching pairs, earth-return assembly¶

    Added¶

    • Skin effect in the solve (10.37, opt-in): a conductor with resistance_model="skin" now changes the branch impedance per frequency: each of its branches (lumped branches and the half stubs of galvanic chains included) adds l_b · Conductor.skin_correction_per_length(f) to its diagonal, so the resistance becomes R_ac(f) and the internal inductance L_int(f) (exact solid-round Bessel solution). A conductor without an inductance model gets the resistance part only and makes the world frequency dependent. Through the solver, a 150 mm² Al PEN has R_ac/R_dc = 1.0556 / 1.2308 / 1.5496 at 250 / 550 / 1000 Hz. The default resistance_model="dc" changes nothing. Tests: tests/test_inductive_solver_integration.py::test_skin_model_raises_the_pen_resistance_at_1khz, ::test_skin_correction_of_an_inductive_conductor, ::test_skin_correction_covers_the_half_stubs_of_a_galvanic_chain, ::test_skin_effect_without_inductance_model_changes_the_resistance_only.
    • Tabulated Sommerfeld assembly (10.60, opt-in): build_sommerfeld_correction_matrices(..., method="tabulated") (and the single-frequency builder) groups the segments by depth and shares one table of the spectral integral S(ρ) per pair of depth groups and frequency (100 nodes, cubic spline in ln(ρ + z)); the outer quadrature of all segment pairs runs vectorised. Against a converged reference (200 instead of 24 ρ nodes per pair) it is closer than the per-pair rule: 1.7e-6 against 1.4e-5 of the largest entry on a 10-house street network (two-layer earth: 2.7e-6 against 3.6e-6; lines 1 cm below the surface: 8.0e-7 against 6.1e-5); the substation impedance agrees with the converged reference to 5e-10 (per-pair rule 1.1e-8). On R8's W3 network a full solve with 11 frequencies takes 2.6 s instead of 27 s CPU (10 houses) and 7.8–9.1 s instead of 242 s (30 houses, 249 inductive branches; impedances equal to 3e-8). Blocks whose λ grid cannot be resolved (conductors in the surface plane across a large network) keep the per-pair rule. The solver selects it through the engine attribute sommerfeld_assembly ("exact" when absent); the default stays the per-pair assembly. Tests: tests/test_sommerfeld_tabulated.py.

    Changed¶

    • ⚠ Exact touching segment pairs by default (10.46): chain neighbours and bends share an end point where the Neumann integrand is log-singular; the 16×16 Gauss–Legendre rule is −0.3 % … +0.8 % off for such pairs. The solver and build_inductance_matrix, neumann_mutual, perfect_mirror_self_pair_inductance now use the closed forms (touching="exact"); touching="quadrature" reproduces 0.15.0. A straight conductor keeps the inductance of the unsplit wire for every discretisation. Every result with an inductive conductor moves: 1.2e-6 (50 Hz) and 2.3e-5 (1 kHz) on the substation impedance of R8's 2-house street network (1.2e-4 at 1 kHz on the 30-house network, together with the lumped-branch fix below), ≤ 3.2e-6 in the golden cases cond_galvanic_neumann_perfect_mirror, cond_galvanic_neumann_carson_series, cond_galvanic_neumann_sommerfeld, defaults_mixed_cluster_inductive, line_cond_galvanic_neumann, cond_coupled_leads_open_circuit, cond_isolated_pen_sommerfeld_two_layer, gen_tn_measurement_overhead_leads, fit_rho_f_standard_sweep.
    • sommerfeld on the image-free baseline (10.46): the solver assembles the Neumann matrix and the Sommerfeld correction against free space (image="none") instead of adding the additive image and removing it again: exact, cheaper, the same numbers to round-off (≤ 4e-18 relative). Golden cases: the three sommerfeld cases, round-off only.
    • One earth-return assembly for all frequencies (10.60): the solver builds the Sommerfeld or Carson correction for the engine's whole frequency set at once on first use (geometry, λ grid and Bessel tables once per segment pair); every frequency is bit-identical to a single-frequency assembly. Sommerfeld matrices of R8's W3 network for 11 frequencies: 7.4–8.2 s → 1.5–1.9 s CPU (2 houses), 137–141 s → 27–28 s (10 houses); Carson matrices of W3c (30 houses): 4.4 s → 2.4 s. Full solves (with the wave-1 gains): W3, 2 houses 35.3 s in 0.15.0 → 2.1 s; W3, 30 houses about 3 600 s (review estimate) → 242 s; W3c, 30 houses 16.4 s → 3.3 s. The correction keeps at most 256 MiB of matrices (longer sweeps are assembled in chunks). Golden cases: none. Tests: tests/test_inductive_solver_integration.py::test_sommerfeld_pair_work_does_not_grow_with_the_number_of_frequencies, ::test_branch_correction_is_built_once_for_all_frequencies, ::test_branch_correction_memory_bound_keeps_the_numbers.

    Fixed¶

    • ⚠ carson_series on the wrong image baseline (10.20, F32): Carson's correction (ωμ₀/π)(P + jQ) is defined against the anti-parallel image, but the solver added it on top of the additive image of ADR-0004, which counts the image twice: the earth-return reactance of a 1 km PEN was 2.04× (50 Hz) and 2.19× (1 kHz) Carson's value. The solver now builds the inductance matrix with image="antiparallel"; the 1 km PEN matches l·X′_Carson(GMR = a·e^(−1/4)) to 7e-6 (50 Hz) and 4.2e-4 (1 kHz). On R8's W3c network (30 houses, PEN Neumann) the substation impedance at 1 kHz drops from 0.4288 + 0.3439j Ω to 0.3964 + 0.2301j Ω (|Z| −16.6 %). Affects every carson_series result with an inductive conductor (all backends that share the inductive assembly). Golden case: cond_galvanic_neumann_carson_series (6.0e-3). Test: tests/test_inductive_solver_integration.py::test_carson_series_reactance_matches_carson.
    • ⚠ Carson's small-a series (10.46): for a ≤ 0.25 only the leading terms were used (no a² term −(π/64)a²cos2θ of Q, constants rounded): P and Q were up to 2.4e-4 and 3.0e-3 off at a = 0.25, and the θ = 0 quadrature above it 6.4e-6. The series is now summed to a¹² with the exact constants and the θ = 0 quadrature uses a graded composite rule; both are at round-off against mpmath and the regime boundary is continuous. Moves carson_series results (included in the bound of the entry above). Tests: tests/test_carson_pq.py::test_small_a_regime_is_continuous, ::test_small_a_series_matches_an_independent_quadrature, ::test_theta_zero_quadrature_is_at_round_off.
    • ⚠ Neumann inductance of lumped and single-segment conductors (10.24): a conductor with inductance_model="neumann" that the topology keeps as one lumped branch (discretize_segment_length=None, or a single sub-segment) lost its inductance silently: a 300 m PEN was 0.0564 + 0j Ω at 1 kHz. It is now one inductive branch from start to end, coupled to every other inductive branch (0.2101 + 3.7839j Ω with sommerfeld; a 10 m chain gives 0.2096 + 3.7840j Ω; with perfect_mirror and carson_series the single branch equals the refined chain to 1e-9). Affects PEN service drops shorter than the PEN segment length (5 m by default) and lumped leads when the conductor is inductive; on R8's 2-house street network the substation impedance moves by 1.3e-6 at 1 kHz. Golden cases: none. Tests: tests/test_inductive_solver_integration.py::test_neumann_is_honoured_on_lumped_and_single_segment_conductors, ::test_lumped_branch_equals_the_distributed_chain, ::test_lumped_inductive_conductor_couples_to_a_parallel_chain.
    • Conductors that leave the soil under sommerfeld (10.46, F52): a conductor crossing z = 0, or lying in the surface plane, is solved with the default earth model (the pair integrals split it at the surface; a PEN in the plane agrees with one 1 mm below it to 1e-7); with perfect_mirror and carson_series its image is singular and the solve raises as before. Tests: tests/test_inductive_solver_integration.py::test_sommerfeld_accepts_a_surface_crossing_conductor, tests/test_audit_guards.py::test_surface_pen_with_neumann_is_continuous_under_sommerfeld.
    • Invalid escape sequences in coupling.inductance docstrings (audit A1-12, 10.95): three docstrings raised SyntaxWarning on compilation (an error under python -W error), and \text in the module docstring turned into TAB characters; they are raw strings now. Tests: tests/test_coupling_conventions.py::test_modules_compile_without_invalid_escape_sequences, ::test_docstrings_contain_no_control_characters.
    • segment_distance with a zero-length segment (audit F04, 10.44): a point segment was measured from the start of the other segment (5.099 m instead of 1.0 m for a point 1 m beside the middle of a 10 m cable), so find_close_wires / find_close_conductors missed a point-like bond 2 cm from a cable; points now take the clamped orthogonal projection. Tests: tests/test_conductor_proximity.py::test_segment_distance_with_point_segments, ::test_point_like_bond_next_to_a_cable_is_reported.

    Stream fitx: Touch-voltage envelope, formula renderer, passivity¶

    Security¶

    • Formula parser cost is bounded for sums, products and powers (10.01; audit 1, H1, H2, L1, L2): formulas inside every documented limit could still cost minutes (times below: fresh process on a shared machine). A sum of fractions with distinct 15-digit prime denominators, ((b+2)/b)**64 per term, took 22 s (120 terms) and 105 s (222 terms) before it was rejected, because the bound was checked after SymPy had built the common denominator; the least common multiple of the fraction coefficients is now accumulated term by term before the sum is built, and the formula is rejected at the second term (0.05 s). Accepted products such as 332 logarithms (13 s) or 111 vanishing log sums (55 s) re-evaluated the growing product with evalf after every factor (2 802 and 2 857 evalf calls); values now come from a numeric shadow evaluation (mpmath, 128 bits) computed once per node, and chains of 32 or more factors are multiplied pairwise (2.1 s and 0.8 s; 176 and 110 evalf calls, all SymPy's own). (exp(600)*f)**64 was accepted as f**64*exp(38400): the power bound now includes the symbol-free factor of a product base, and every symbol-free sub-expression of the result is bounded (f*exp(600)*exp(600) is rejected). The documented exponent (-3/2) (Python reads (-3)/2) is accepted, so format_impedance_formula output such as f**(-3/2) parses. A deterministic budget, MAX_WORK = 200 000 bookkeeping steps, caps every parse. Exported formulas parse to the same values as before. tests/data/formula_corpus.json gains 4 accepted and 6 rejected audit strings for groundinsight's guard. Tests: tests/test_formula_security.py::test_sum_of_fractions_with_distinct_denominators_is_rejected_quickly, tests/test_formula_security.py::test_long_product_chains_parse_in_bounded_time, tests/test_formula_security.py::test_symbol_free_factor_of_a_power_base_is_bounded, tests/test_formula_security.py::test_negative_rational_literal_exponents_are_accepted.

    Added¶

    • touch_voltage_envelope(..., interior=...) (10.21): samples the inside of closed loops, the analogue of the IEEE Std 80 mesh voltage (the worst touch voltage inside a mesh, near its centre). interior=None (default): the mesh centres of mesh / grid_mesh electrodes and the centre of a ring, i.e. loops in open ground; a closed polyline, the generators' building-outline foundation, is not sampled because the building stands inside. interior=True adds the point inside a closed polyline farthest from its wires (non-convex outlines stay inside); interior=False gives the offset curve only. The default cannot see buildings: pass interior=False for an axis-aligned generated foundation (grid_mesh) or a ring around a station housing. It replaces the mesh_centres= keyword added earlier in this release; anchor="connection_point", interior=False reproduces v0.15.0. Test: tests/test_touch_voltage_envelope.py::test_ring_envelope_samples_the_ring_centre_by_default.
    • Fit metadata in BusType documents (10.19, 10.27): standard-form exports record all coefficients k0 ... k5, fit_quality.max_relative, the weights and the fitted impedance quantity ("input", "cluster", "electrode", or null for raw samples); vector-fit exports record fit_quality.rms_relative and quantity. fit_quality_summary names them. Test: tests/test_io_groundinsight.py::test_fit_metadata_records_which_impedance_was_fitted.
    • MAX_WORK in groundfield.io._formula (see Security).

    Changed¶

    • One formula renderer (10.73): fit_to_sympy keeps its signature but returns the partial fractions of the fit (one term per real pole or conjugate pole pair) instead of passing them through sympy.simplify, which merged them into one rational function: 6 of reviewer R5's 98 vector fits had a term above second order (s**4 over s**3 for a pole pair, a real pole and L_inf), now none. fit_to_sympy, fit_to_sympy_standard and the groundinsight exporter share one builder (one pole-pairing rule) and one rounding rule: every coefficient is rounded once to decimals significant digits (decimals must be a positive integer, also a NumPy integer, and every other value raises ValueError; above 17 counts as 17). Accuracy is unchanged: worst deviation from fit.evaluate over R5's fits 4.0e-6 at 6 digits and 4.8e-12 at 12. Exported vector-fit formulas are unchanged character for character; exported standard-form formulas keep every coefficient and value, but a complex coefficient now keeps its own sign (- f*(12.5592 - 1338.31*j) is written + f*(-12.5592 + 1338.31*j); 23 of R5's 31 standard fits). Test: tests/test_fit_formula_renderer.py::test_fit_to_sympy_keeps_one_term_per_pole_or_pole_pair.
    • ⚠ One passivity check (10.40): the BusType export runs check_passivity (on the rounded formula it writes) instead of its own implementation, so both use one band, 0 Hz to twice the highest fitted frequency (the default of check_passivity was the highest fitted frequency: a fit with Re Z = -1.0 Ohm at 1.5 kHz, passive at every sample up to 1 kHz, passed check_passivity but failed the export), the pole-adapted grid with the midpoints between samples, an exact corner check for the standard form, a tolerance of 1e-9 max|Z|, and non-finite values fail (a pole at s = 0 passed check_passivity). PassivityReport.as_dict() is the strict-JSON metadata["passivity"] block (passed, min_re_Z_Ohm or null, f_at_min_Hz, rho_at_min_Ohm_m, f_checked_Hz, rho_checked_Ohm_m, n_points); PassivityReport gains n_points. Affected: callers of check_passivity without f_max, whose verdict now covers twice the band (fits that passed may fail); impedances do not move. Golden cases: none. Test: tests/test_passivity.py::test_violation_between_the_highest_sample_and_twice_it_is_found.
    • rho_at_fit for layered soil (10.84): the error message and the export docstrings state one convention, the resistivity of the upper layer, rho_1 (the parameter of the standard form's rho_1 sweep), so vector-fit and standard-form BusTypes pair with the same groundinsight Bus. Test: tests/test_io_groundinsight.py::test_rho_at_fit_convention_for_layered_soil_is_the_upper_layer.
    • CSV frequency column frequency_hz (10.69): the column the electrode and cluster tables gain in this release, and the header keys frequency_hz / frequencies_hz, use the lower-case unit suffix of the spec layer and of FieldResult.to_dataframe. The potential path keeps its v0.15.0 column frequency_Hz. Test: tests/test_io_csv.py::test_new_frequency_column_uses_the_lower_case_unit_suffix.
    • Footprint from the electrode geometry (10.79): the touch-voltage footprint reads the wires from Electrode.wire_polylines() instead of a per-kind re-implementation (a ring keeps its exact circle). Foot positions are unchanged up to round-off (at most 1.4e-14 relative in the touch voltage, 2.3e-10 at UTM coordinates). Test: tests/test_touch_voltage_footprint.py::test_mesh_centres_lie_midway_between_the_discretiser_wires.

    Fixed¶

    • ⚠ Touch voltage of rotated foundations (10.21; audit 1, H4): the generators build a foundation that is not axis-aligned from bonded wires <name>, <name>_w1, ... (10.39), and with 10.54 every OSM house foundation is rotated. The footprint came from the named edge wire only: for a 10 m x 10 m 2 x 2 mesh at 30 deg (100 Ohm m, 100 A) touch_voltage read 214.4 V on +x / -x and 93.9 V on +y (feet 1 m inside the building), 31 of 72 envelope feet lay inside the foundation and no cell centre was sampled. The footprint is now every wire of the foundation (from any of its names, within its galvanic cluster) and the envelope samples the cells of the rotated grid: 161.5 V on every side (167.5 V at 0 deg; one uniform current per wire instead of per grid), no foot inside. Affected: touch voltages at rotated foundations, i.e. OSM-based layouts. Golden cases: none. Test: tests/test_touch_voltage_footprint.py::test_touch_voltage_of_a_rotated_foundation_matches_the_unrotated_one.
    • touch_voltage_spectrum counted a frequency twice (10.52; audit 1, L4): a frequency carried by two results entered the RMS twice (701.6 V instead of 496.1 V); it now raises ValueError naming the frequency. Test: tests/test_safety.py::test_touch_voltage_spectrum_rejects_a_frequency_given_twice.
    • Strict-JSON fit metadata (10.27): hand-built standard fits wrote max_relative as NaN, which is not JSON; non-finite quality figures are now null. Test: tests/test_io_groundinsight.py::test_metadata_of_hand_built_fits_is_strict_json.
    • ⚠ Envelope of a ring includes its centre (10.21): a consequence of interior=None above; where the centre dominates, the envelope maximum rises (ring of 10 m radius at 0.8 m, 100 Ohm m, 100 A: 139.4 V on the offset curve, 182.6 V in the centre; a 4 m ring's centre, 346.0 V against 360.2 V, does not). Affected: envelopes of ring electrodes. Golden cases: none. Test: tests/test_touch_voltage_envelope.py::test_ring_envelope_samples_the_ring_centre_by_default.

    Deprecated¶

    • Nothing.

    Stream fixsec: Formula parser: resource bounds¶

    Security¶

    • Formula parser: SymPy no longer factorises numbers or rewrites exp(c*log(x)) while parsing (10.01): strings inside the allow-list grammar still kept SymPy busy for minutes. SymPy simplifies a fractional power of an exact number by factorising the number and keeps a radicand of up to q - 1 times its digits: (N)**(63/64) with the 300-digit N = 10**300 - 1 (also written (f*N)**(63/64), (-N*f*rho)**(63/64) or (N/8)**(63/64)) keeps a radicand of about 18 800 digits and tests a 16 280-digit factor of it for primality, and 18**(10**30/(10**30 + 1)) needs 2**(10**30). It also rewrites exp(c*log(x)) as x**c, whose exponent is no literal: exp(log(N)*63/64) factorises like the power, exp(log(2*f)*10**12) builds 2**(10**12). Of 19 such strings (four reported by the groundinsight stream), 15 did not finish within 20 s, three took 1.8 to 2.6 s and one was accepted. Now no exact number is left under a fractional power: a fractional power (x**(p/q), sqrt) of a symbol-free base is evaluated numerically, and the rational coefficient c of a product base is taken out as the float |c|**(p/q) (17 significant digits, principal branch); a powered sum with a float coefficient gets float coefficients throughout (SymPy 1.13, which the dependency range allows, takes the largest coefficient of such a sum out of the power: (2.0*f + N)**(63/64) did not finish within 10 s there); an exact number under a fractional power needs q <= 64 and digits times q <= 300 (MAX_ROOT_DENOMINATOR, MAX_RADICAND_DIGITS in groundfield.io._formula, the limits of groundinsight's guard); log() inside exp() is rejected before anything is built; log of a symbol-free argument other than a positive rational or a float is evaluated numerically (SymPy's log(r + r*j) and the square root of r + r*j/3 factorised numbers of about 1 800 digits: 0.7 to 1.4 s for 900-digit fractions r). Each of the 19 strings is now rejected in at most 3 ms. Exported formulas parse to identical expressions (all 119 accepted corpus formulas and 387 exporter outputs of reviewer R5's generator). tests/data/formula_corpus.json gains 18 resource-exhaustion strings and one out-of-grammar string, all rejected by groundinsight's guard as well; the corpus test now parses every rejected string first in a worker process with a 20 s deadline and a 1 GiB address-space limit, so a regression can neither hang the test run nor exhaust the machine. Tests: tests/test_formula_security.py::test_every_rejected_formula_is_rejected_quickly_in_a_worker_process, tests/test_formula_security.py::test_fractional_powers_of_large_exact_numbers_are_rejected_before_sympy, tests/test_formula_security.py::test_fractional_powers_of_exact_numbers_are_evaluated_in_floating_point, tests/test_formula_security.py::test_log_inside_exp_is_rejected, tests/test_formula_security.py::test_sum_with_a_float_coefficient_keeps_no_exact_coefficient_under_a_root, tests/test_formula_security.py::test_complex_numbers_with_large_fractions_are_evaluated_quickly.

    Added¶

    • MAX_ROOT_DENOMINATOR (64) and MAX_RADICAND_DIGITS (300) in groundfield.io._formula (see Security).

    Changed¶

    • ⚠ Hand-written formulas with roots or logarithms of numbers (10.01): sqrt(2), 3**(1/3), (2*pi*f)**(1/2), log(-2) or log(2*pi) now parse to floats of 17 significant digits instead of exact radicals and logarithms (log of a positive rational such as log(2) stays exact); evaluated values move by at most 3.6e-16 relative (2 units in the last place) in 17 sample formulas. Formulas that loaded before and are now rejected: log anywhere inside exp (e.g. exp(log(f)*3), which SymPy turned into f**3), and fractional powers of exact numbers beyond the new limits, e.g. 2**(1/65), (2*f)**(1/1000), a 5-digit integer under **(63/64) or the square root of a 151-digit integer; writing the number as a float (2.0**(1/65)) keeps such a formula valid. Affects hand-written BusType formulas only: groundfield's exporters write neither roots nor exp/log (checked for every exporter formula of the corpus). Golden cases: none (bit-identical). Test: tests/test_formula_security.py::test_fractional_power_limits_for_exact_numbers.

    Stream gen2: Measurement loop, one concrete-shell mechanism, PEN junctions¶

    Added¶

    • Reading of a simulated earth tester (10.17): groundfield.generators.measured_impedance(result, probe=None, source=None) returns the three-pole reading Z_meas = (phi_E - phi_S) / I per frequency: E is the earthing system the measurement current is fed into (the source's attached_to cluster), S the voltage terminal (meas_S of a network built with a probe lead, else the probe electrode). The two-pole reading (E-H) is result.input_impedance(). New constants MEASUREMENT_H_TERMINAL = "meas_H" and MEASUREMENT_S_TERMINAL = "meas_S"; PlannedMeasurement.terminals and NetworkPlan.all_junctions carry the instrument terminals of a plan. Test: tests/test_measurement_loop.py.
    • GroundingSystemSpec.plan_site(..., outline=): shapes the resolved foundations after the footprint once the random draws are made ("spec" by default). Test: tests/test_footprint_foundations.py::test_footprint_outline_does_not_shift_the_random_draws.
    • groundfield.generators.grounding.MIN_FOUNDATION_SIDE_M (1 m): shortest side of a foundation taken from a footprint's oriented bounding rectangle.

    Changed¶

    • ⚠ Measurement leads end on the instrument (10.17): in TnNetworkGenerator measurement set-ups the feed lead runs from the junction meas_H to the auxiliary electrode and the probe lead from meas_S to the probe (both junctions at the substation position, at the lead height); the source drives the test current between the substation and meas_H (return_to; the auxiliary electrode itself without a feed lead). With both leads, meas_S lies the touching distance (sum of the two wire radii) beside meas_H, so an in-line probe lead runs beside the feed lead instead of through it. Numbers of every measurement world with leads change completely: the old probe lead bonded the probe to the substation (reading 2.8e-4 instead of 1.6096 ohm in a 3-house network) and the old feed lead closed the loop metallically (99.93 % of the current never reached the soil). Golden cases: gen_tn_measurement_overhead_leads (auxiliary rod -1 A instead of +0.024 A, network potentials -1.8 %).
    • ⚠ One concrete-shell mechanism (10.12, 10.13, 10.14): both concrete_model values put the Sunde coefficient C = rho_c / (2 pi) ln(r_b / r_a) and the radius r_b = r_a + t on every encased (perimeter) wire; nothing is written to World.concrete_shell_corrections any more and PEN service drops keep their geometric resistance. "lumped" keeps one grid-mesh owner for an axis-aligned ring foundation (exactly the lumped resistance C / L_perim), "distributed" uses one owner per wire; polygon, rotated and mesh foundations are the same for both. Mesh braces are not encased (bare radius, C = 0); this also moves "distributed" mesh foundations, whose braces had the inflated radius (4-house network, 2 x 2 mesh: +2.6 / +7.8 / +12 % at rho_c = 150 / 1000 / 5000 Ohm m). The shell now reaches solve_mutual_matrix / solve_mutual_field (the AP1 Z_G path), Engine.solve of build_at worlds, the layout builder and substation / cabinet foundations. AP1-like site (rho_c = 100 Ohm m, 5 cm, 12 polygon foundations): house Z_G diagonal +42 / +19 / +2.8 % at rho_1 = 30 / 100 / 1000 Ohm m against v0.15.0, network input impedance +13.6 / +8.8 / +2.2 %; with rho_c = rho_soil the shell is transparent (0.0 %). Affected: every world with concrete_rho_ohm_m set. Golden cases: mutual_foundations_lumped_concrete (Z_G diagonal +42.2 %).
    • ⚠ PEN cable joints are junction nodes (10.38): the layout builder's cable corners and tap points are leakage-free Junction nodes (reported in result.metadata["junction_potentials"]) instead of 5 cm driven rods, which leaked 0.24 to 0.53 % of the test current at full foundation penetration and 3.7 % at 5 % penetration (substation potential +0.07 ... +0.33 % and +3.5 %). The joints are no electrodes any more. Golden cases: gen_layout_builder (+0.33 % potentials, +0.43 ... +0.57 % currents).
    • ⚠ PenConfig.depth_m is honoured: every PEN section and service drop of TnNetworkGenerator runs horizontally at z = depth_m between its anchors (it ran straight between the electrode connection points, e.g. from the substation ring at 0.6 m to a cabinet rod head at 0 m); depth_m must be
      1. 10-house network: resistive PEN -2.7e-7, bare galvanic PEN -1.4 %, Neumann PEN at 1 kHz X 0.736 -> 0.167 ohm (the old sections ended in the surface plane, where the perfect-mirror image meets the wire). Golden cases: gen_tn_default (<= 3.5e-7).
    • Layout reading uses the solved test current: LvNetworkLayout.measured_grounding_impedance and verify_current_balance take source_magnitude_a=None by default and read the current of the solved substation source (was 1 A unless repeated by the caller). Test: tests/test_measurement_loop.py::test_layout_reading_uses_the_solved_source_current.
    • Overpass response cap 64 -> 16 MiB: DEFAULT_MAX_RESPONSE_BYTES also bounds the decoding memory (6x the body for building payloads, 25x for a body of empty objects); the largest AP1 payload is 3.0 MiB. Pass max_response_bytes for wider areas. Test: tests/test_overpass_ingest.py::test_default_response_cap_bounds_the_decoded_size.
    • Naming rule of the spec layer (10.69): spec fields carry lower-case unit suffixes; the soil specs are the deliberate exception and mirror the literature symbols of their soil model (rho, rho_1, rho_2, h_1, h). Test: tests/test_generator_naming.py::test_soil_specs_mirror_their_soil_models.

    Fixed¶

    • Measurement leads short-circuited the instrument (10.17): resistive leads now leave the reading unchanged (1.609633 ohm with and without leads; the feed lead adds its resistance to the two-pole reading only). Tests: tests/test_measurement_loop.py::test_resistive_leads_do_not_change_the_reading, ::test_feed_lead_adds_its_resistance_to_the_loop_impedance, ::test_two_terminal_reading_is_the_superposition_of_one_terminal_solves, ::test_in_line_leads_lie_side_by_side.

    Stream fix1: Fixes from the first adversarial audit¶

    Added¶

    • Engine.sommerfeld_assembly (10.60): "exact" (default) or "tabulated" assembly of the Sommerfeld earth-return correction of inductive conductors, a validated engine field (also a create_engine parameter, recorded in the provenance). The solver read it through getattr on a field that did not exist. Tests: tests/test_sommerfeld_tabulated.py::test_the_assembly_is_a_validated_engine_field, ::test_solve_with_an_engine_that_selects_the_tabulated_assembly.

    • create_conductor(resistance_model=...) (10.37): the opt-in skin effect ("skin"; default "dc") is reachable through the factory; it was accepted by Conductor(...) only. Test: tests/test_api_factories.py::test_create_conductor_reaches_the_skin_effect_model.

    Changed¶

    • One error type when a backend cannot take the soil model (audit A1-08, F09): the backend functions called directly raise BackendCapabilityError like Engine.solve does: solve_image with a layered soil, solve_image_2layer with a homogeneous soil, solve_image_nlayer and solve_mom with three or more distinct layers, and the concrete-shell guard of the mom family. BackendCapabilityError subclasses ValueError and NotImplementedError (the types of image_nlayer, cim, bem and the shell guard in 0.15.0), but not TypeError: code that caught TypeError from image_2layer (homogeneous soil or layer stack) or mom (three or more layers) must catch BackendCapabilityError or ValueError. TypeError stays for a world.soil that is no soil model. Tests: tests/test_soil_capability_errors.py, tests/test_api_smoke.py::test_solve_image_directly_rejects_non_homogeneous.
    • Engine copies and assignments are validated (audit A1-02, A1-04): Engine.model_copy(update=...) validates the update like the constructor (unknown keys raise with a suggestion; only warnings that the update introduces are emitted), every field is validated on assignment (engine.frequencies = [] raises), and entries put into Engine.options in place are validated when they are read (backend_options, solve). Engine.options and BackendCapabilities.soils / conductors / kernels are annotated with the accepted input types (mappings, sets), so the documented calls type-check (audit A1-06).
    • Soil keyword typos are type errors again (audit A1-09): the deprecated soil names resistivity / thickness are translated in a model validator instead of validation_alias=AliasChoices(...), which made the pydantic mypy plugin accept any keyword (HomogeneousSoil(rhoo=100.0) passed). Runtime behaviour is unchanged (deprecation warning, 0.15.0 JSON loads, a rejected value is reported under the name it was given); type checkers now also report the deprecated keywords. Test: tests/test_typing_contract.py.
    • W605 joins the blocking lint subset (audit A1-12): pre-commit and CI run ruff check --select F,E9,B,W605; an invalid escape sequence (a SyntaxWarning today, a SyntaxError under python -W error and in a future Python) no longer passes. The 35 findings of the blocking subset in tests/ were cleared outside the generator / geo test modules.
    • Release tool checks the remote (audit A1-11, 10.09): before anything is written, scripts/release.py reads the upstream branch and the remote tags with git ls-remote (no fetch, --dry-run changes nothing): a clone that is behind or has diverged from its upstream is refused, remote tags count for the MICRO of calver and for "the tag exists", an unreadable remote or a branch without upstream (in a repository with a remote) aborts; a repository without any remote is released with a note. A stale clone used to create a second, different v2026.11.0 that only the push rejected. Tests: tests/test_release_script.py.
    • solve_mutual_matrix / solve_mutual_field assemble the inductive branch correction for the solved frequency only (10.60): an engine with four frequencies built the correction four times per call, now once; results are bit-identical. Test: tests/test_mutual_matrix.py::test_one_call_assembles_the_branch_correction_for_its_frequency_only.

    Fixed¶

    • ⚠ A galvanic conductor with one sub-piece leaks (10.24, galvanic half): a distributed conductor with coupling_to_soil="galvanic" whose discretize_segment_length is at least its length fell back to the leakage-free lumped branch.
