Two 2 m rods 30 m apart joined by a bare 50 mm² conductor in 100 Ω·m: input impedance 22.74 Ω with one sub-piece against 5.54 Ω with two (line kernel; point kernel 22.36 Ω against 6.91 Ω); now 5.54 Ω (point kernel 8.59 Ω, the coarsest member of its convergence sequence towards 5.48 Ω). The sub-piece is one midpoint pseudo-node between two R/2 stubs and also carries its inductance with inductance_model="neumann". Affects only galvanic distributed conductors shorter than discretize_segment_length (no golden case; isolated ones keep the lumped branch). A single sub-piece is as accurate as the kernel is for a long segment: with the line kernel it is converged in homogeneous soil and in two-layer soil without an electrode through the interface (12.134 Ω against 12.136 Ω with 32 sub-pieces, 300 over 50 Ω·m, h₁ = 2 m); with an electrode through the interface the cross-layer correction needs sub-pieces of the order of h₁ (one: 7.18 Ω, four: 9.41 Ω, 32: 9.50 Ω). Tests: tests/test_conductors_distributed.py::test_a_single_segment_galvanic_conductor_leaks, ::test_a_single_segment_galvanic_conductor_keeps_its_inductance.
    • Deprecated flat names in Engine.model_copy (audit A1-02): engine.model_copy(update={"sommerfeld_epsrel": 1e-2}) was silently ignored (the copy ran with 1e-7; the name is a deprecated property that shadowed the stored value). The flat sommerfeld_* names are now mapped onto options["mom_sommerfeld"] with a DeprecationWarning, also back to a default value. Tests: tests/test_engine_validation.py.
    • Engine assignments (audit A1-04, engine part of 10.67): engine.options = {"fem": {...}} stored a raw dict and the solve failed with an AttributeError (a misspelled option was never named); engine.frequencies = [] returned an empty result and [nan] solved. Tests: tests/test_engine_validation.py.
    • Result files honour the umask (audit A1-05): save_result wrote mode 0600 (tempfile.mkstemp); new files now get 0666 reduced by the umask (0644 under 022), an overwritten file keeps its mode. Tests: tests/test_result_persistence.py::test_result_file_mode_follows_the_umask, ::test_overwriting_keeps_the_mode_of_the_replaced_file.
    • ShallowSegmentWarning follows the kernel (audit F08): the advisory quantifies the bias of the point-form self image of a segment longer than four times its depth. It was also emitted for the exact line kernel (the image family's default), which has no such bias: a 10 m strip at 0.1 m depth gives 17.37863 Ω with 2 m segments and 17.37859 Ω with 0.25 m ones (point kernel 21.57 Ω and 17.23 Ω). It is now emitted for the point kernel and the mom family only, names the line kernel as the remedy and no longer claims that the mom family stays silent. Tests: tests/test_shallow_segment_advisory.py.
    • Stale docstrings (audit A1-07): create_engine (backend-specific settings such as the mom_sommerfeld accuracy controls go into options, not the deprecated flat fields), groundfield.solver (the registered backends instead of "reserved" MoM / FEM), the engine's family overview (cim / bem aliases, fem experimental), Engine.earth_inductive_model (carson_series on the anti-parallel image, sommerfeld on the image-free matrix), the consumers of the series controls, and the branch block (skin term).
    • Control characters in docstrings (10.95): \rho in a non-raw docstring of the two-layer solver started with a carriage return (a valid escape, invisible to W605). Test: tests/test_source_escapes.py.

    Security¶

    • Result files from untrusted sources (audit A1-01): the $ndarray decoder of FieldResult.metadata accepted any numpy dtype string: a 396-byte result file decoded to a 4 MiB unicode array, 413 bytes to an 8 MiB sub-array, and object arrays loaded. It now reads only what the encoder writes (bool, integer, float16/32/64, complex64/128 and unicode dtypes), checks that the shape matches the number of items and that every item is a scalar of the dtype's kind before allocating, limits string arrays to 64 bytes per character of their items (plus 4 KiB), bounds the nesting depth (MAX_METADATA_DEPTH = 100) and raises ValueError (pydantic.ValidationError through load_result) naming the metadata key for every malformed tag; load_result reports non-UTF-8 and too deeply nested JSON as ResultFormatError. The encoder applies the same limits. Tests: tests/test_result_metadata_codec.py.

    Stream fix2solver: Fixes from the second adversarial audit: solver and engines¶

    Added¶

    • groundfield.solver.PointKernelInterfaceWarning: warning category for a horizontal wire within 0.6 segment lengths of the layer interface on the point-form cross-layer path (see Fixed, point form of the cross-layer path).
    • expected_segments(...)["nodal_unknowns"] (10.06): the size of the dense augmented nodal system (current owners, nodes and branches, every conductor sub-piece included), an upper bound computed in closed form and checked against HARD_LIMIT like the segment count. Test: tests/test_diagnostics_report.py::test_nodal_unknowns_are_the_size_of_the_solved_system.

    Changed¶

    • A world without a current source raises in every backend (10.66, audit A2S-05): Engine.solve validated with World.check(require_source=False), and an earlier entry of this release said such a world "stays solvable through Engine.solve (all results zero)". Only fem did; the image and mom families and World.solve raised. Engine.solve now runs the full check, so every backend and both entry points raise the same ValueError ("has no current source"), as the 10.66 concept lists. Test: tests/test_source_validation.py::test_a_world_without_a_source_raises_in_every_backend.
    • ⚠ TwoLayerSoil(rho, rho, h) is cut nowhere (10.48, audit A2S-07): image_2layer (also reached through image and the auto dispatch) cut wires at h even when both layers have one resistivity, while mom, mom_sommerfeld and image_nlayer did not. Every backend now cuts where the resistivity changes only (the canonical stack), so such a soil solves exactly like homogeneous soil: a rod 0.3–6.3 m in TwoLayerSoil(100, 100, 2.15) had 13 segments and sat 3.8e-4 (point kernel) and 3.3e-7 (line kernel) from the homogeneous result; now 12 segments, equal to 2.2e-16 and bit for bit. Who is affected: worlds with an equal-resistivity two-layer soil and a wire through h (not AP1: its station rods end above h_1). Golden cases: none. Test: tests/test_diagnostics.py::test_equal_resistivity_two_layer_soil_is_the_homogeneous_soil.

    Fixed¶

    • ⚠ Interface contact (10.32, audit A2S-01): an interface cut that would leave a wire part shorter than the wire diameter was dropped, and the part joined its neighbour in the other layer, so the contact with that layer was ignored: a 4.5 m rod (a = 8 mm) in 1000/30 Ω·m read 195.0 Ω with its tip 15.9 mm into the lower layer and 128.6 Ω at 16.1 mm (+52 %), in image_2layer and the mom family alike, and the same at the head of a rod. Every interface cut is now kept, however short the part beyond it; the thin-wire guard concerns the chosen segment length only. The Galerkin self term (it lost every digit below L/a = 1e-8) and the line kernel's parallel-pair integral (two parts of 1e-11 m: 0 instead of 125 1/m, the rod read 72.7 instead of 133.5 Ω) are evaluated without cancellation for such parts; every regular segment keeps its arithmetic bit for bit. The impedance is now continuous in the penetration depth (line kernel 133.5 → 128.6 Ω from 10 pm to 16.1 mm, the same shape for the point kernel, mom and mom_sommerfeld); the step that remains is the onset of the contact itself between a tip that ends at the interface and one that reaches past it (195.9 → 133.5 Ω here). Who is affected: rods whose tip or head lies within 16 mm (two wire radii) of an interface: −34 % in this example. The constant geometry.electrodes.SLIVER_RADII introduced earlier in this release is gone. Golden cases: none. Tests: tests/test_interface_slivers.py (::test_every_interface_cut_is_kept, ::test_rod_resistance_is_smooth_in_the_tip_penetration, ::test_rod_resistance_is_continuous_in_h1_at_the_former_sliver_threshold, ::test_a_tip_twelve_millimetres_into_a_conductive_layer_is_felt, ::test_a_head_just_inside_a_conductive_upper_layer_is_felt, ::test_parallel_pair_integral_of_parts_much_shorter_than_their_distance).
    • ⚠ Point form of the cross-layer path (10.28, ADR-0007): with kernel="point" (and in the mom family, mom_sommerfeld with two layers and solve_mutual_* with the point kernel) the diagonal of the cross-layer reaction matrix averages the layered correction over a 3-point rule along the depth axis, ±0.387 L, whatever the segment's orientation. For a horizontal wire next to the interface the rule reached across it and returned wrong, even negative impedances (a rod through h₁ = 1.2 m with a 20 m strip at 0.8 m, 300/50 Ω·m, ds = 2 m: −19.37 Ω against 9.62 Ω with the line kernel; v0.15.0: −14.24 Ω; −14 % … −552 % over the measured cases), or reached above the soil surface and raised a bare ValueError about negative depths. Such worlds now raise BackendCapabilityError naming the line kernel (or image_2layer for the mom family) and a segment length that fits; a horizontal wire within 0.6 segment lengths of h₁ warns with PointKernelInterfaceWarning (−3 % … −17 %). Rods (cut at h₁) and every valid configuration keep their numbers; the default line kernel is unaffected. Golden cases: none. Tests: tests/test_cross_layer_point_form.py, tests/test_cross_layer_line_kernel.py::test_point_kernel_keeps_its_numbers_near_the_interface.
    • ⚠ fem below a thin top layer (10.23, audit A2E-2): the guard raised only when an interface lies between the hemisphere radius a_eq and the electrode's deepest point; a 3 m rod wholly below h₁ = 0.4 m < a_eq (1000/30 Ω·m) only warned and returned 31.1 Ω (mom: 10.5 Ω, +197 %). It now also raises when every electrode of a cluster lies below an interface between a_eq/2 and a_eq (more than half of the hemisphere in a layer the electrodes do not touch; +51 … +130 % and −81 … −98 % measured for a 3 m rod against image_2layer). Thinner top layers keep the warning (+16 … +46 %, −38 … −58 % for 100/1000 Ω·m), as do bonded rods through the top layer. Golden cases: none. Tests: tests/test_fem.py::test_fem_rejects_a_rod_below_a_top_layer_holding_most_of_its_hemisphere, ::test_fem_only_warns_below_a_thin_top_layer, ::test_fem_guard_applies_to_a_bonded_cluster_wholly_below_the_interface.
    • Resource guard (10.06, audit A2S-02): check_segment_budget counted leakage segments only, so every sub-piece of an isolated distributed conductor (a branch and an interior node of the dense nodal system and a row of the inductance matrix) counted as zero: a 100 m PEN in 2000 pieces predicted 12 segments and took 407 MiB, in millimetre pieces the solve died with a MemoryError. The guard now also predicts the unknowns of the nodal system in closed form and raises ResourceLimitError above HARD_LIMIT before anything of that size is allocated (an inductive chain before the inductance assembly); diagnose() reports it. Tests: tests/test_diagnostics_report.py::test_conductor_chain_beyond_the_limit_is_rejected, ::test_a_chain_in_millimetre_pieces_is_rejected_before_allocation, ::test_diagnose_reports_a_nodal_system_beyond_the_limit.
    • Floating clusters in solve_mutual_* (10.29, audit A2S-03): every excitation rebuilt the multi-port matrix of all floating clusters and factorised the full nodal system again, so the cost grew with the square of the number of bonded grounding nodes (256 ring-and-rod nodes: 199.9 s against 24.3 s before floating). The floating core is now assembled and factorised once with one right-hand side per anchor; an anchor outside the core enters through its coupling column. 256 nodes: 5.6 s; 100 rotated foundations of four bonded strips: 2.7 s (24.0 s); results equal to 1e-12 (golden values bit-identical). Tests: tests/test_mutual_matrix.py::test_shared_factorisation_equals_one_floating_solve_per_anchor, ::test_one_core_assembly_and_factorisation_for_all_anchors.
    • expected_segments for layer stacks (10.79, audit A2S-07): the prediction cut wires at every declared interface, the backends at the interfaces of the canonical stack only: a rod 0.3–6.3 m in MultiLayerSoil([100 Ω·m, h = 2.15 m], [100 Ω·m]) was predicted at 13 segments and solved with 12. One rule now feeds both. Test: tests/test_diagnostics.py::test_expected_segments_matches_the_solve_for_every_layer_stack.
    • fem ignored the skin effect silently (10.37, audit A2C-4): "skin" is a conductor feature of the backend registry; fem (lumped, DC) warns with BackendCapabilityWarning naming it, while image and mom raise the rod-to-rod voltage of a 300 m 150 mm² Al PEN by +54.9 % from 0 Hz to 1 kHz. A registered backend that lists its conductor features without "skin" warns too. Tests: tests/test_backend_registry.py::test_a_backend_that_ignores_the_skin_model_says_so, ::test_registered_backend_without_the_skin_feature_warns.
    • Duplicate names in a loaded or edited world (10.66, audit A2S-06): World.check() did not check that names are unique (only the add_* methods did): two electrodes of one name from JSON failed in the solver with a singular matrix, an electrode and a junction of one name were merged into one node. check() (run by every solve) now rejects a name used twice among the nodes, the conductors or the sources. Test: tests/test_source_validation.py::test_check_reports_names_used_twice.
    • convergence_study repeated show-once warnings (A1-02, audit A2S-08): it copied the engine per step with the validating Engine.model_copy, whose warning capture resets every module's registry; a shallow strip showed the same ShallowSegmentWarning once per step. It now copies internally and validates the segment lengths itself (finite and strictly positive). Tests: tests/test_convergence.py::test_convergence_study_shows_each_distinct_warning_once, ::test_convergence_study_rejects_non_finite_segment_lengths.

    Security¶

    • Crafted result files (A1-01, audit A2S-04): a $complex metadata tag holding an integer beyond the float range ({"$complex": [10**400, 0]}, also inside $tuple, as a $dict key or an $ndarray item) escaped load_result as a bare OverflowError instead of the documented ValueError; it is now a ValueError naming the metadata key. Test: tests/test_result_metadata_codec.py::test_a_complex_tag_beyond_the_float_range_is_a_value_error.

    Stream fix2coup: Fixes from the second adversarial audit: coupling¶

    Fixed¶

    • Cost of the exact touching-pair rule (10.46, audit A2C-5): the closed forms of coaxial segment pairs were evaluated with one Python call per pair, and on a straight conductor of M sub-segments all M(M−1)/2 pairs are coaxial: build_inductance_matrix took 2.4–3.2× (straight PEN, M = 100–800) and 7–26× (oblique PEN) the time of the 0.15.0 quadrature rule, and a solve of a straight 2 km PEN (400 branches, 2 frequencies) 2.2–2.6 s instead of 1.0–1.3 s CPU. The coaxial pairs of a segment are now evaluated in one vectorised call — bit-identical (the 3-element dot products go through the same BLAS routine, the logarithms through math.log): 0.9–1.6× the quadrature time on straight chains, 2.0–3.7× on oblique ones (where the quadrature rule uses the cheap parallel closed form; 0.07–0.26 s for M = 200–400), 1.1–1.4 s for the 2 km PEN solve. No number changes (golden cases bit-identical). Tests: tests/test_neumann_touching.py::test_scalar_closed_form_calls_grow_linearly, ::test_touching_batch_is_bit_identical_with_the_scalar_closed_forms, ::test_exact_rule_on_a_straight_chain_is_not_slower_than_twice_the_quadrature (benchmark).
    • ⚠ carson_series: an overhead wire over a buried one (10.20, audit A2C-1): the solver treated the buried wire of such a pair as an overhead wire at height |z| on the mirrored-endpoint image, whose image lands above ground next to the overhead wire. The mutual reactance per metre was 35.9 % low for a lead 1 m above a PEN at 0.7 m depth (1 kHz, 100 Ω·m), 13.5 % at 1 m horizontal offset, 3.4 % for a generator surface lead 0.1 m above ground, 10.3 % at 50 Hz. A perfect conductor screens the soil, so the anti-parallel baseline of a pair on opposite sides of the surface is now zero (build_inductance_matrix(image="antiparallel")), and its coupling is the field transmitted into the soil, new coupling.carson.carson_mixed_mutual_correction (Carson's J plus the bounded remainder of the buried wire's attenuation; Ametani et al. 2009): equal to an independent mpmath quadrature to 1e-9 and to the per-metre Sommerfeld kernel to 1e-3 at 1 kHz. Affects worlds with an overhead and a buried inductive conductor under carson_series; golden cases: none. Tests: tests/test_carson_validity_domain.py::test_overhead_wire_over_buried_wire_matches_the_transmitted_field, ::test_mixed_mutual_correction_matches_an_independent_quadrature, ::test_mixed_pair_agrees_with_the_sommerfeld_model_per_metre, ::test_antiparallel_baseline_of_a_pair_across_the_surface_is_zero, ::test_every_image_convention_gives_one_impedance.
    • ⚠ carson_series outside its validity domain (10.20, stream DOC-B): oblique segment pairs got sqrt(l_i l_j)·|cos φ| times the per-metre value once per segment pair, which grows with the number of segments: a probe lead turning 8.1° against the feed lead changed the measured reactance 17.6-fold, two 1 km leads at 9° gave 20× the Sommerfeld coupling. Carson's per-metre tiling is now kept for near-parallel pairs of near-horizontal segments only; oblique pairs and every pair and self term of a segment inclined by more than 8.1° get the exact Sommerfeld correction of a homogeneous earth of the same conductivity (tabulated assembly). At the boundary the result steps from the per-metre tiling to the Sommerfeld value by the model error of the tiling there: 12 % for two 1 km leads 2 m apart at 1 kHz; in the DOC-B measurement setup (93 m leads, 50 Hz, short against the 711 m skin depth) Im Z_meas goes from 0.032 Ω (6.2°) to 0.018 Ω (8.6°), which is the sommerfeld value. Affects carson_series worlds with oblique, inclined or vertical inductive conductors; golden cases: none. Tests: tests/test_carson_validity_domain.py::test_pairs_outside_the_domain_get_the_sommerfeld_correction, ::test_oblique_coupling_does_not_grow_with_the_number_of_segments, ::test_oblique_leads_couple_as_in_the_sommerfeld_model.
    • ⚠ Wires lying side by side (10.46, streams GEN-2 / DOC-B): two near-parallel segments whose axes stay closer than the sum of their radii over a common length (coincident leads, a service drop routed back along its PEN main) had no finite filament mutual inductance — the 16×16 rule stopped at its 1e-6 m floor: two coincident 50 m leads read 1.87 H of mutual inductance (+587j Ω at 50 Hz), and a two-house TN network whose drops ran back along the PEN main had a negative input reactance at 1 kHz (−0.633 Ω perfect_mirror, −0.643 Ω sommerfeld). Such pairs are now evaluated as touching wires at the axis distance r₁ + r₂ (in every image convention and in the image block), and carson_series gives two conductors overlapping on one axis the earth-return mutual of touching wires (it skipped them as "the same wire"). The two-house network now reads +0.244 / +0.233 / +0.250 Ω (perfect_mirror / sommerfeld / carson_series). Also moves the reading of the generator's in-line fall-of-potential setup, whose probe lead starts 1 cm beside the feed lead and ends on its axis: Im Z_meas at 50 Hz 0.063 → 0.054 Ω (sommerfeld). Golden cases: none. Tests: tests/test_interpenetrating_wires.py.
    • ⚠ sommerfeld: long segments (10.24, audit A2C-2): the earth-return correction of a segment pair was one 16×16 Gauss–Legendre rule over the two segments, which cannot resolve exp(−γR) along a segment much longer than the skin depth — every lumped inductive conductor is one such segment. At 1 kHz in 100 Ω·m (skin depth 159 m) the earth-return resistance of a straight buried conductor was +1.7 % (1 km), +13.4 % (3 km), +34.9 % (5 km) against the converged chain (+0.2 / +0.6 / +1.7 % at 50 Hz), and a lumped 3 km PEN between two rods read +11 % series resistance, without a warning. A segment longer than a quarter skin depth of the most conductive layer now gets a composite outer rule (16 nodes per piece, at most 512 pieces, beyond that a SommerfeldResolutionWarning) and an S(ρ) table as dense as a short pair's (24 nodes over a km-long range left 1–2e-3); frequencies with equal piece counts share one pass, so every frequency still equals a single-frequency assembly bit for bit, and the tabulated assembly splits the same way. The lumped conductors now match their chains to ≤ 1e-4 (3 km at 1 kHz: 2.78766 against 2.78773 Ω). Numbers move for every sommerfeld world with an inductive segment longer than a quarter skin depth — at 1 kHz 40 m (100 Ω·m), 22 m (30 Ω·m), 12.6 m (10 Ω·m); at 50 Hz 178 m (100 Ω·m) — by about 1e-4 just above that length up to tens of per cent for km-long lumped conductors; segments below it keep the historic rule bit for bit. Golden cases: none. Tests: tests/test_sommerfeld_long_segments.py.
    • ⚠ Neumann inductance of close, non-parallel segment pairs (10.46, found in audit round 2 while attacking the guard above): pairs that miss the closed forms — nearly parallel wires close together whose directions differ by more than 1e-9 in cos (about 0.003°), crossing and skew pairs — were integrated with the 16×16 rule, which only resolves 1/r for pairs apart by about half their length: two 5 m segments with aligned ends and 1e-5 … 1e-3 rad between them were +12 % at 10 cm and +200 … +250 % at 1 cm, −19 % at 1 mm with offset ends; crossing pairs about −1 % (−12 % when crossing near an end at 2°), skew pairs 1 cm apart −2.3 … +0.5 %, a branch starting on the interior of another segment (T-junction without a shared vertex) −3.5 … −13 %. With touching="exact" (the default) such pairs are now integrated semi-analytically (inner integral in closed form, outer Gauss–Legendre panels graded towards the near-singular points; ≤ 5e-11 against an adaptive reference); touching="quadrature" keeps the 0.15.0 numbers. Moves every inductive result with such pairs — conductors from real (non-axis-parallel) geometries running close together, the generator's in-line probe lead (starts 1 cm beside the feed lead, ends on its axis): feed–probe mutual 92.8 → 91.7 µH, the value of touching wires over the overlap plus the end-to-end coupling (91.65 µH). Golden cases: none. Tests: tests/test_neumann_close_pairs.py.

    Stream fix2sec: Fixes from the second adversarial audit: formula parser¶

    Security¶

    • Formula parser: SymPy no longer evaluates the expression being built (10.01): the second audit of the calendar release found two more evaluation paths that the parser's rules did not bound (after two rounds of rules against particular inputs): SymPy's exp asks whether the factors of its argument are comparable and expands powers of complex sums to find out, and Pow computes the real part and the argument of the inner base of (b**e)**(p/q); 67- and 75-character formulas such as exp(pi*(((pi/4 + j/2)**64 + pi/4 + j/2)**64 + pi/4 + j/2)**64/1e30) and (((((f/4 + j/2)**64/1000 + j/2)**64/1000 + j/2)**64/1000 + j/2)**63)**(1/2) ran for more than a minute. The class is fixed instead of the examples: every node is now built unevaluated (evaluate=False), and every number is computed by the parser itself in bounded floating point (128 bits, every bound checked before the operation) and stored as a SymPy float; within one product the numeric factors form one coefficient, within one sum the numbers are added and like terms collected (so 1/(f - f) and exp(f - f + 1000) stay rejected). Formulas within every limit that took 2.2 s to 3.3 s to parse before now take at most 0.04 s, every corpus string keeps its verdict, and the time of a parse grows linearly with the length of the formula. The exponents of nested powers now multiply to at most 64 (MAX_POW), the bound groundinsight's guard applies; the audit's strings are rejected within 3 ms. evaluate_spec and the exporter's passivity check evaluate the parsed expression node by node with NumPy (new formula_function) instead of sympy.lambdify, whose printer evaluates sub-expressions: 6.8 s for a 1 439-character formula within every limit, now 2 ms. tests/data/formula_corpus.json gains 16 resource-exhaustion and 2 out-of-grammar strings, all rejected by groundinsight's guard as well. Tests: tests/test_formula_security.py::test_nested_powers_of_audit_2_are_rejected_quickly, tests/test_formula_security.py::test_formulas_within_every_limit_parse_and_evaluate_quickly, tests/test_formula_security.py::test_parsing_and_evaluation_never_let_sympy_evaluate, tests/test_formula_security.py::test_evaluate_spec_does_not_use_sympy_code_generation, tests/test_formula_security.py::test_terms_that_cancel_within_one_sum_are_collected.
    • Formula parser: numeric literals outside the float range raise FormulaError (10.01): 1e- followed by 320 nines is a valid Python float literal that rounds to 0.0; the parser converted its exponent to a float and raised OverflowError, which leaked through load_bustype_json. Every literal, also in an exponent, is now bounded by its exact decimal value before any float is formed, and no arithmetic error leaves the parser as anything but FormulaError; a BusType document whose voltage_level or schema_version is outside the float range raises ValueError, and so does a crafted document that is nested too deeply for Python's JSON reader, is not a JSON object, lacks name, system_type or voltage_level, or has samples that are not numbers (it raised RecursionError, AttributeError or KeyError). Tests: tests/test_formula_security.py::test_numeric_literals_outside_the_bounds_raise_formula_error, tests/test_formula_security.py::test_load_bustype_json_reports_a_huge_literal_as_evaluate_spec_error, tests/test_formula_security.py::test_bustype_header_numbers_outside_the_float_range_raise_value_error, tests/test_io_groundinsight.py::test_crafted_bustype_documents_raise_value_error.

    Added¶

    • formula_function(expr, symbols=("f", "rho")) in groundfield.io._formula: NumPy function of a parsed impedance formula, built from the expression tree without SymPy's code generation (real arithmetic for real values, as lambdify with NumPy; SymPy's principal branch for roots and logarithms of negative numbers). Test: tests/test_formula_security.py::test_formula_function_keeps_the_real_limits_at_singular_points.

    Changed¶

    • ⚠ Form of a parsed impedance formula (10.01): parse_impedance_formula returns an unevaluated expression whose numbers are SymPy floats of 17 significant digits: 2*pi*f*j/1000 + 403191/50000 parses to (0.0062831853071795866*I)*f + 8.0638199999999998, log(2) to 0.69314718055994529; symbolic exp, log and powers keep the structure of the text. Code that compared parsed expressions with exact SymPy expressions must compare values instead. Evaluated values move by at most 4.1e-16 relative for the 103 exporter formulas of the corpus and 3.3e-15 for the hand-written ones (closer to a 50-digit evaluation than before: 1.5e-16 instead of 3.4e-15 for the worst one). Formulas that loaded before and are now rejected: nested powers whose exponents multiply to more than 64, also through sums and function calls and for powers of numbers, e.g. ((f**8)**8)**2 or (f*10**40)**2 (write 1e40), and exponent literals below 1e-300 such as f**1e-400 (which was read as f**0). evaluate_spec gives the principal-branch value where lambdify gave nan, e.g. (f - 100)**0.5 below 100 Hz. groundfield's exporters write neither nested powers nor roots, so exported formulas are not affected. Golden cases: none (bit-identical to the base of the change). Tests: tests/test_formula_security.py::test_exponents_of_nested_powers_multiply_to_at_most_max_pow, tests/test_formula_security.py::test_parsed_expression_is_unevaluated_with_numbers_as_floats, tests/test_formula_security.py::test_formula_function_takes_the_principal_branch_where_real_arithmetic_has_none.

    Fixed¶

    • Strict JSON for unknown fit quality (10.19): a fit whose rms error is unknown (NaN, e.g. a hand-built fit) exported "rms_error_Ohm": NaN, which strict JSON parsers reject, and a non-finite sample did the same; every non-finite number of a BusType document is now null, save_bustype_json serialises with allow_nan=False, the export refuses a voltage_level that is not finite, and fit_quality_summary reports an unknown rms error as rms=unknown. Tests: tests/test_io_groundinsight.py::test_unknown_fit_quality_and_samples_are_written_as_null, tests/test_io_groundinsight.py::test_export_refuses_a_voltage_level_that_is_not_finite.

    Stream fix2gen: Fixes from the second adversarial audit: generators and OSM ingest¶

    Added¶

    • Outline width of a footprint (audit A2G-06): BuildingFootprint.mean_width_m() (twice the area over the perimeter, the width of a thin rectangle), BuildingFootprint.is_degenerate(), groundfield.geo.mean_width and groundfield.geo.MIN_OUTLINE_WIDTH_M (1 mm). Test: tests/test_geo_footprint.py::test_mean_width_of_an_outline.
    • groundfield.geo.MAX_PAYLOAD_DEPTH (16): deepest container nesting of an accepted Overpass payload (an Overpass result has 7 levels). Test: tests/test_overpass_ingest.py::test_payload_nested_deeper_than_an_overpass_result_is_rejected.

    Changed¶

    • ⚠ Owners of generated mesh foundations (10.12, 10.39; audit A2G-04): the image family solves one uniform leakage current per electrode (an owner). A mesh foundation (cross-braces) is now one owner per wire line at every orientation, bare or encased, for both concrete_model values; an axis-aligned bare one was one grid_mesh owner. One owner over perimeter and braces was +1.6 / +2.5 / +3.2 % high for 2 × 2 / 3 × 3 / 4 × 4 meshes against the equipotential reference (one owner per segment, equal to mom), one owner per wire is +0.3 / +0.5 / +0.6 %. A "distributed" ring foundation is one owner per side also without a shell (it was one owner when bare and axis-aligned). Numbers move: bare axis-aligned mesh foundations −1.2 % (10 m, 2 × 2) to −2.5 % (30 m, 4 × 4); generated networks with the default mesh foundations −0.7 to −0.8 % in every node potential and up to 4.7 % in surface potentials; the largest touch voltage over the default house mesh −3.6 % (2.528 → 2.437 V per ampere, reference 2.402); bare distributed rings at most 0.06 % up to aspect ratio 4 (0.24 % at 10). A "lumped" ring keeps one grid_mesh owner when axis-aligned and one owner per side when rotated (0.24 % apart at aspect ratio 10), and polygon outlines stay one closed wire: one closed polyline would drop the interior sample of the default touch-voltage envelope, which decides it for rings of 20 m and more. AP1 (lumped polygon foundations) does not move. For a mesh foundation, electrode_currents[<anchor>] and grounding_impedance(<anchor>) now refer to its first wire, as for every rotated foundation: use cluster_impedance or the sum over <name> and <name>_w<j>. Golden cases: gen_tn_default, gen_layout_builder, gen_tn_measurement_overhead_leads.
    • ⚠ PEN cables of TnNetworkGenerator form a nearest-point tree (10.38; stream DOC-B): every cable starts at the point of the network already built nearest to its far end, inside a cable at a leakage-free tap junction (pen_tap_<k>, a plan junction): cabinet cables in the order of their distance from the substation (collinear cabinets are chained), service drops from the cable path that feeds their cabinet or radial-trunk tap (or from a drop already routed for it, closer buildings first); a building whose connection point lies on a cable splits it and gets no drop. Service drops started at their cabinet before, 60 to 160 m back along the main in the default network, and now tap the main beside each house (5 m). Numbers move for every generated TN network: default network substation potential −2.1 %, substation leakage −3.1 to −4.8 %, house potentials −0.2 to +1.0 % (the houses are tied closer to the substation); measurement network −1.1 %. Main sections are named <cable>_s<k>. Golden cases: gen_tn_default, gen_tn_measurement_overhead_leads.
    • Overpass ids and levels: element ids and member refs must be 64-bit integers (OverpassError otherwise); a non-finite or negative building:levels tag gives levels=None; outlines without area are skipped (see Fixed).

    Fixed¶

    • A shell that is electrically absent moved a mesh foundation (10.12; audit A2G-04): with ρ_c = ρ_soil (or a 0.1 mm shell) the default 10 × 10 m mesh foundation lost 1.27 % of its impedance and its largest touch voltage on the offset curve dropped from 232.26 to 221.21 V (100 A), because the shell switched it from one owner to one per wire; a distributed 20 × 2 m ring moved by 0.24 %. Tests: tests/test_foundation_owners.py::test_vanishing_shell_on_a_mesh_foundation_is_continuous, ::test_vanishing_shell_on_a_distributed_ring_is_continuous.
    • Mesh foundation impedance depended on the orientation (10.39; audit A2G-04): the same bare mesh gave 4.79618 Ω at 0° and 4.73771 Ω at 30° (100 Ω·m, line kernel). Tests: tests/test_foundation_owners.py::test_mesh_foundation_does_not_depend_on_its_orientation, ::test_distributed_ring_does_not_depend_on_its_orientation, ::test_mesh_foundation_is_close_to_the_equipotential_reference.
    • Untrusted Overpass JSON raised other exceptions than OverpassError (10.05; audit A2G-05): a body nested deeper than the decoder's limit (about 10 000 levels) raised RecursionError, also from a cache file; an unknown key nested 1 500 levels decoded, passed every check and crashed the cache write (json.dump recurses in Python); a 400-digit integer coordinate raised OverflowError; "id": null crashed the parser with TypeError. Tests: tests/test_overpass_ingest.py::test_deeply_nested_body_raises_overpass_error, ::test_payload_nested_deeper_than_an_overpass_result_is_rejected, ::test_deeply_nested_cache_file_is_refetched, ::test_vertex_with_a_huge_integer_coordinate_raises_overpass_error, ::test_huge_integer_coordinate_in_the_json_text_raises_overpass_error, ::test_ids_beyond_64_bits_are_rejected, ::test_element_with_a_null_id_is_parsed_without_an_id, ::test_non_finite_or_negative_levels_are_unknown.
    • Outlines without area (10.05, 10.54; audit A2G-06): parse_overpass_payload kept outlines of identical or collinear vertices with the default min_area_m2=0 (although documented as skipped); their oriented bounding rectangle raised IndexError, so LvNetworkLayout.to_world and TnNetworkGenerator crashed instead of widening the foundation to MIN_FOUNDATION_SIDE_M. The parser now skips outlines narrower than 1 mm on average; the bounding rectangle of such an outline is its covering segment, which the generators widen with a warning. Tests: tests/test_overpass_ingest.py::test_outline_without_area_is_skipped, ::test_thin_but_real_outline_is_kept, tests/test_geo_footprint.py::test_ombr_of_a_degenerate_outline_is_its_segment, tests/test_footprint_foundations.py::test_a_footprint_without_area_gets_a_minimum_foundation, ::test_tn_generator_widens_a_footprint_without_area.
    • Generator objects pickled by v0.15.0 (10.69; audit A2G-07): a pickled TnNetworkConfig unpickled and then failed in build() with AttributeError (renamed fields of soil specs, cabinets, PEN topology and the config itself). Pickles are now upgraded like JSON input: renamed fields and values, defaults for fields added since, and instances of the deprecated aliases (KvsConfig, StarKvsTopology, KvsPlacement, OrtsnetzLayout) load as the current classes, equal to the same object loaded from its v0.15.0 JSON. Tests: tests/test_generator_pickles.py.
    • Overlapping PEN cables made an inductive network non-passive (10.38; stream DOC-B): in a 2-house network both service drops ran from the cabinet 180 m out back along the PEN main (Im Z_in = −0.633 Ω at 1 kHz with inductance_model="neumann"); now +0.072 / +0.055 / +0.051 Ω (perfect mirror / Sommerfeld / Carson series). Tests: tests/test_pen_routing.py.
    • Integer fields of a resolved config held floats (10.53; stream DOC-B): GeneratorConfig.sample stored the float draw of a distribution in building_counts and CabinetConfig.fixed_count, and model_dump_json warned; a draw for an integer field is now int(round(x)), the value the generators build with. Test: tests/test_generators_base.py::test_resolved_integer_fields_hold_integers.

    Stream fix3solver: Fixes from the third adversarial audit: solver¶

    Added¶

    • groundfield.solver.InterfaceContactWarning and the diagnose() category interface_contact (10.32, audit A3S-05): a rod head or tip within one wire radius of a layer interface. The thin-wire model has no end face: a rod that ends exactly at the interface is a wire on one side of it, one that reaches past it by a fraction of its radius touches the other layer through a short piece only, while the solid rod is continuous in between. Against an axisymmetric finite-volume model of the solid rod (4.5 m, a = 8 mm, 1000/30 Ω·m) the line kernel reads +3.3 / +3.9 / +7.2 / +21.4 % with the tip 16 / 8 / 1 / 0 mm above the interface and −4.6 / −1.2 / +0.7 / +2.1 % 1 / 4 / 8 / 16 mm past it (point kernel and mom alike). The step at zero penetration that an earlier entry of this release called the onset of the contact is this limit of the model. No end-face term: the solid rod's 161.4 Ω at the interface lies between the two branches of the thin wire (196 Ω above, at most 149.2 Ω past it). Tests: tests/test_interface_slivers.py::test_rod_tip_within_one_wire_radius_of_the_interface_warns, ::test_rod_head_within_one_wire_radius_of_the_interface_warns, tests/test_diagnostics_report.py::test_diagnose_reports_a_rod_end_at_the_interface.
    • min_distance of two_layer_real_space_kernel, two_layer_layered_correction_real_space and two_layer_layered_correction_group (audit A3S-01): the regularisation length of the closed-form 1/R terms in m; they are clamped at min(1/lambda_clamp, min_distance) instead of 1/lambda_clamp (h_1/200 near the interface). None (default) keeps the clamp; every value at or beyond it is bit-identical. Test: tests/test_cross_layer_line_kernel.py::test_min_distance_leaves_every_farther_image_bit_for_bit.
    • groundfield.world.RESERVED_NAME_PREFIXES ("__layer_", "__cond_"), see Fixed.

    Fixed¶

    • ⚠ Cross-layer diagonal at the interface (10.28, 10.32, audit A3S-01): the diagonal of a segment is the layered correction at the wire radius with the interface image exchanged for its Galerkin integral, but the correction clamped its closed-form 1/R terms at h_1/200 (25 mm for h_1 = 5 m), so every piece within h_1/400 of an interface lost 68–84 % of its layered correction. A ring (r = 3 m, a = 5 mm) at h_1 = 5 m read −5.29 Ω with the default engine (1000/30 Ω·m; 322 Ω instead of 6.80 Ω in 30/1000 Ω·m), and the contact values of a rod tip just past the interface were 10–32 % low in every segment backend. The solver now evaluates the diagonal at the wire radius and pairs closer than h_1/200 at the 1 mm of its point forms (also mom_sommerfeld's two-layer off-diagonal kernel and two_layer_probe_matrix, which clamps at its own min_distance). The diagonal equals the image solution to 1e-13; the ring is continuous through h_1 (4.1826 Ω 1 µm above, 4.1805 Ω at h_1). Numbers move for a rod tip or head within h_1/400 of an interface (12.5 mm for h_1 = 5 m, 75 mm for 30 m; 4.5 m rod, a = 8 mm, 1000/30 Ω·m, tip 1 µm past h_1: 133.5 → 149.2 Ω, +11.7 %; 10000/10 Ω·m: 123.2 → 180.7 Ω; against the solid rod −4.6 / −1.2 / +0.7 % at 1 / 4 / 8 mm, before −14.6 / −10.7 / −6.8 %), for horizontal wires at or within h_1/400 below an interface, and for pairs of segments closer than h_1/200 (segment lengths below about h_1/100 near the interface: a 2 m rod through h_1 = 30 m, ds = 0.05 m, line kernel −0.014 %). Not affected: AP1 (station rods end 0.2 m or more above h_1). Golden cases: none. Tests: tests/test_cross_layer_line_kernel.py (::test_ring_resistance_is_continuous_through_a_deep_interface, ::test_diagonal_correction_at_the_wire_radius_matches_the_image_solution, ::test_pairs_closer_than_the_clamp_match_the_image_solution), tests/test_interface_slivers.py::test_rod_tip_contact_against_the_solid_rod_reference, ::test_rod_head_contact_against_the_solid_rod_reference, ::test_rod_tip_contact_does_not_depend_on_the_interface_clamp.
    • Point form above the interface (10.28, audit A3S-02): the point-form guard of audit round 2 ran only when a segment reaches h_1. With every wire above h_1 the point form takes the image of a segment in the interface as a point source 2(h_1 − z) away: a ring (r = 3 m, ds = 0.5 m) 1 µm / 1 mm / 1 cm / 5 cm above h_1 = 5 m read −1931 / −945 / −57.9 / 21.2 Ω (line kernel 4.18 / 4.77 / 15.4 / 28.0 Ω) with kernel="point", mom, mom_sommerfeld (two and three layers) and solve_mutual_*, also in 0.15.0; +853 % … +0.1 % in 30/1000 Ω·m. The guard now applies on both sides of the interface with the same thresholds: a horizontal wire closer than 0.387 segment lengths raises BackendCapabilityError, one within 0.6 warns with PointKernelInterfaceWarning (−2.5 % / −0.4 % at 0.2 m above h_1). Above the interface this error threshold is conservative: it also refuses coarse but accurate models (a ring r = 25 m 1.2 m above h_1 with 5 m segments, +0.9 % against the line kernel), which a finer segment_length — the message names one — or the line kernel solves. The default line kernel is unaffected. Tests: tests/test_cross_layer_point_form.py::test_point_form_rejects_a_horizontal_wire_just_above_the_interface, ::test_point_form_warns_for_a_horizontal_wire_above_the_interface, ::test_mutual_matrix_point_form_is_guarded_above_the_interface, ::test_rods_ending_just_above_the_interface_are_not_refused.
    • Reserved name prefixes (10.66, audit A3S-03): an electrode called __layer_r__L1 next to a rod r through h_1, or __cond_pen__seg_0 next to a galvanic conductor pen, took the place of the solver's internal owner or node of that name and the solve failed with numpy.linalg.LinAlgError. add_electrode, add_junction and World.check() (every solve, worlds loaded from JSON) reject node names with the prefixes __layer_ and __cond_. Tests: tests/test_source_validation.py::test_check_rejects_a_name_with_a_reserved_prefix, ::test_reserved_prefixes_are_rejected_when_a_node_is_added.
    • ⚠ Field of a soil of one resistivity declared as layers (10.48, audit A3S-04): TwoLayerSoil(rho, rho, h) solved like homogeneous soil, but FieldResult.potential and solve_mutual_field evaluated sources below h with the point form of the two-layer kernel instead of the line sources of the homogeneous result: +2.7 % at (0.2, 0, 4) m next to a 0.3–6.3 m rod, 21 % at 5 cm from it. Both now read the canonical layer stack and equal the homogeneous result bit for bit; a stored MultiLayerSoil with at most two distinct layers is evaluated as two-layer soil (it raised NotImplementedError for three declared layers). Who is affected: field values of equal-resistivity layered declarations near wires below h. fem keeps the declared interface in its mesh on purpose (mesh topology continuous over a ρ₂ sweep): 1.7e-6 / 1.0e-5 from the homogeneous solve for two / three equal layers. Golden cases: none. Tests: tests/test_layer_stack.py::test_field_of_an_equal_resistivity_stack_is_the_homogeneous_field, ::test_potential_of_a_stored_three_layer_declaration_with_two_distinct_layers.
    • Building large networks (audit round 3, generator note): since generated mesh foundations are one electrode per wire line, TnNetworkGenerator.build() was 10–80 times slower for 100–1000 houses: World.add_* scanned every list for the new name. A name index next to the world (not part of the model: equality, copies, pickles and JSON are unchanged) makes the check O(1) per add; generated worlds are bit-identical (fingerprints for 100 / 400 / 1000 houses). 1000 houses: 6.97 → 2.44 s (star) and 7.52 → 2.65 s (radial) on the measuring machine. Tests: tests/test_world_names.py::test_each_add_costs_constant_name_work, ::test_lists_edited_by_hand_are_seen_by_the_next_add, ::test_the_index_is_no_state_of_the_world.
    • Quoted near-field deviations (10.21, 10.22): UniformCurrentFieldWarning, the safety module and the known limitations quoted "+19 % / −21 %" (touch / step 1 m outside a 20 m grid corner), the point-kernel numbers, and the image engine page −28 % for a step that starts above the corner. They now give the default (line) kernel and the step: touch 1 m outside +20 %, step from 1 m to 2 m outside −21 %, from the point above the corner 1 m outwards −28 % (point kernel +19 / −21 / −28 %). The interior docstring of touch_voltage_envelope called a generated foundation a grid_mesh; it is a group of bonded wires whose cells the default samples. Tests: tests/test_touch_voltage_footprint.py::test_the_quoted_corner_deviations_are_those_of_the_default_kernel, tests/test_touch_voltage_envelope.py::test_a_generated_mesh_foundation_is_sampled_in_its_cells_by_default.

    Stream fix3coup: Fixes from the third adversarial audit: coupling¶

    Fixed¶

    • ⚠ Wires lying beside each other: order-free, pointwise rule (10.46, audit A3C-1, A3C-2): the touching-wire rule of audit round 2 switched a whole near-parallel pair to touching wires when the partner's end points lay inside a cylinder of radius r_a + r_b around the row segment, so the result depended on the order of the conductors: a V pair of 1 m and 0.5 m at 1.5° read 0.362 or 0.503 µH (+38.7 %), the generator's in-line fall-of-potential setup with lumped leads read Im Z_meas(50 Hz) = 0.134442 or 0.126676 Ω (perfect_mirror) depending on whether the feed or the probe lead came first. Tilted V and X pairs whose far ends lay just outside the cylinder kept a filament value above the self inductance: an indefinite inductance matrix (minimum eigenvalue −1.3e-8 H), a two-wire loop with Im Z_in(1 kHz) = −0.14 mΩ (−0.51 mΩ with the skin model). The rule now acts pointwise inside the Neumann integral: where a point of one wire lies beside the other wire (its foot on the other axis falls inside that wire) closer than r_a + r_b, that distance is used; both one-sided integrals (inner integral in closed form) are averaged on end points in a fixed order — bit-identical for every order and direction of the segments, continuous in the geometry, 4e-15 against an independent mpmath quadrature. The in-line reading is 0.126676 Ω in both orders (5 m lead segments: 0.126683 Ω); 2000 random close configurations × 6 conventions gave no indefinite matrix (round-2 state: 700). Numbers move for pairs that lie beside each other closer than touching except exactly coincident ones: partly overlapping parallel wires (+2.3e-4 for a 2 mm offset against the whole-pair value), V pairs leaving a junction at an acute angle, wires crossing at one depth, branches starting on another segment (crossing at 30° with r = 0.1 mm: −7.9e-5). Chain neighbours, bends turning by less than 90° and pairs farther apart keep their values bit for bit. Golden cases: none (all bit-identical). Tests: tests/test_interpenetrating_wires.py::test_beside_rule_does_not_depend_on_the_order_or_direction, ::test_beside_rule_matches_an_independent_quadrature, ::test_matrix_does_not_depend_on_the_order_of_the_conductors, ::test_inline_reading_does_not_depend_on_the_order_of_the_leads, ::test_partial_inductance_matrix_stays_positive_definite, ::test_random_close_configurations_are_positive_definite, ::test_two_wire_loop_has_a_positive_reactance, ::test_mutual_is_continuous_across_the_touching_distance, ::test_chain_neighbours_and_bends_keep_their_closed_forms.
    • ⚠ A wire inside another (10.46, audit A3C-5): a core on the axis of a cable shield was coupled like two wires touching on the outside (axis distance r_c + r_s): loop inductance +36 % against the geometric mean distance, +61 % against the analytic coax, silently. Where the thinner wire runs inside the thicker one, the thicker radius is used — a filament inside a thin tube couples to it as at the tube radius: with the internal inductance on the core only, the loop of a 100 m core (5 mm) in a shield (15 mm) is 26.966 µH against the analytic coax 26.972 µH. Equal radii never count as one wire inside the other (cables in one trench drawn on one axis stay touching wires); for unequal radii the value steps where the thinner axis leaves the thicker wire (concentric cable vs two cables side by side). Golden cases: none. Tests: tests/test_interpenetrating_wires.py::test_wire_inside_another_couples_at_the_radius_of_the_thicker_wire, ::test_wire_inside_another_uses_the_geometric_mean_distance, ::test_coincident_wires_of_equal_radius_stay_touching_wires.
    • ⚠ carson_series: segment order of near-parallel pairs (10.20, audit round 3, evidence e13): a near-parallel pair that is not exactly parallel was projected on the axis of its first segment, so reversing the segment order moved entries of the correction by up to 3.1 % of the largest one (a pair got an overlap in one order and none in the other). Such pairs are now projected on the bisector of the two axes from the mean of their mid-points (3e-17 after reversal); exactly parallel pairs keep their numbers bit for bit. Numbers move for carson_series worlds with horizontal conductors at small angles to each other: the mutual impedance of two 300 m or 1 km leads 2 m apart grows by 2e-5 at 0.5°, 4e-4 at 2°, 1.6e-3 at 4° and up to 6.4e-3 at 8° (50 Hz and 1 kHz); the step at the 8.1° boundary becomes 12.8 % for the 1 km leads at 1 kHz (12.3 %). Golden cases: none. Test: tests/test_carson_validity_domain.py::test_carson_tiling_does_not_depend_on_the_segment_order.
    • ⚠ Filaments at tiny angles (10.46, audit A3C-4): two filaments from one vertex below the coaxial tolerance (4.5e-5 rad) were taken as overlapping coaxial filaments and fell to the 16 × 16 rule: 16 % off at 1.5e-6 rad, 3.8 % at 1e-5 rad; just above it the vertex formula lost 2.7e-9. Below 0.81° the vertex formula is evaluated without cancellation: round-off on axis-parallel geometry, at most 4e-10 in a general orientation. Filaments crossing at tiny angles lost the crossing-point mark of the close-pair rule (computed from 1 − cos², cut off at 1e-14): 9.5e-4 at 1e-8 rad; below 1 − cos² = 1e-6 the mark now comes from a cross product: 1.0e-9 at 1e-8 … 3e-8 rad, 5e-11 from 1e-6 rad on. Numbers move for filaments (neumann_mutual) and segment pairs at such angles that are farther apart than r_a + r_b. Golden cases: none. Tests: tests/test_neumann_touching.py::test_filaments_from_one_vertex_at_tiny_angles, tests/test_neumann_close_pairs.py::test_crossing_filaments_at_tiny_angles.
    • Accuracy statements say where they hold (10.24, 10.46, 10.20, audit A3C-4): measured worst cases replace statements that held only where they were measured. Sommerfeld correction of a long segment (composite rule above a quarter skin depth; audit round 2 stated 7e-5 / 1e-6 and "lumped conductors match their chains to 1e-4" from buried cases): against a refined rule, 50 Hz and 1 kHz, up to 3 km, within 5.9e-4 in the earth-return resistance and 2.4e-4 in the reactance (worst: a 3 km overhead line at 10 m over 10 Ω·m at 1 kHz; a 1 km one 4.8e-4 / 1.2e-4; buried in 100 Ω·m 1.7e-4), tabulated 3.3e-4 / 9.6e-5; the historic rule below a quarter skin depth reaches 4.8e-4, so the correction steps by 3.5e-4 (resistance) at L = δ/4. Close pairs (stated "5e-11"): 5e-11 above 1e-6 rad, 1.0e-9 at 1e-8 rad; pairs inside the parallel tolerance use the parallel closed form at the start offset (4.2e-9 for wires 1 cm apart tilted by 1e-10 rad, 1.7e-5 at 1 µm). Overhead wire over a buried one (stated "1e-9"): 4.2e-9 up to x = 300 m (1 kHz, 10 Ω·m; 1e-10 at 50 Hz), 1.2e-7 at x = 1 km, inherited from Carson's J (1.2e-7 at a = 28, θ = 89.9°, QUADPACK's Fourier rule). Tests: tests/test_sommerfeld_long_segments.py::test_long_segment_rule_meets_its_stated_accuracy, ::test_the_step_at_a_quarter_skin_depth_is_the_stated_one, tests/test_neumann_close_pairs.py::test_crossing_filaments_at_tiny_angles.
    • carson_series validity domain in the documentation (10.20, audit A3C-3): the explanation page and the how-to still described the correction as "scaled by the geometric length of each segment pair" (the heuristic removed in audit round 2). They now describe the per-metre tiling of near-parallel horizontal pairs, the transmitted field across the surface, the Sommerfeld fallback with its step at 8.1°, the long-segment rule and the rule for wires beside each other; ADR-0004, -0005 and -0006 carry the corrections in their 2026.11 amendments. Test: tests/test_carson_validity_domain.py::test_docs_describe_the_carson_validity_domain.

    Stream fix3gen: Fixes from the third adversarial audit: generators and export¶

    Added¶

    • groundfield.io.groundinsight.MAX_METADATA_DEPTH (32): deepest nesting of objects and lists in the metadata of a BusType document (the exporters write four levels); a deeper document is refused on load. Test: tests/test_io_groundinsight.py::test_crafted_bustype_documents_raise_value_error.

    Changed¶

    • ⚠ PEN cables of TnNetworkGenerator (10.38; stream DOC-B; audit A3SG-01, A3SG-02, A3SG-03): every cable cabinet has its own cable, as the topology documents (star: a straight cable from the substation; radial trunk: one cable per trunk segment); cabinets on one line get separate cables in one trench. A service drop taps the cable path that feeds its own building — the cable of its cabinet, or the trunk from the substation to its tap source — at the point nearest to the building's connection point: a leakage-free tap junction pen_tap_<k> that splits the cable into sections <cable>_s<k>, or the substation / the cabinet at the end of the cable; a building in a substation slot of the radial trunk is connected to the substation. What the router guarantees: a drop never starts on another building's drop and never runs along the cable it taps; no cable section or drop is shorter than 1 cm (a tap closer to a node is that node; longer for PEN radii above 7.3 mm, so that every section keeps twice the resistance below which a distributed conductor is refused); a building whose connection point lies on its feeding cable splits it (no drop); two nodes closer than that raise ValueError. A drop may share its route with another cable (another cabinet's cable, a trunk, another drop), as cables in one trench do; the inductive coupling treats such conductors as touching wires and the networks stay passive. Against 0.15.0, where every drop ran from its cabinet (60 to 160 m back along the main in the default network, coaxial with it): the routing moves the golden networks (one cabinet west of every house) by −2.1 % in the substation potential, −3.1 to −4.8 % in the substation leakage, −0.2 to +1.0 % in the house potentials and −1.1 % in the measurement network; the input impedance of default-grid networks with ring foundations (image backend, 50 Hz, 100 Ω·m) moves by −6.4 / −1.1 / −0.2 % for 30 / 100 / 200 houses (star) and 0.0 / −0.1 % for 30 / 100 houses (radial trunk). Against the nearest-point tree of the first 2026.11 draft (chained cabinets, drops hung on drops): golden networks bit-identical; 30 / 100 / 200 houses −4.1 / −2.9 / −2.7 % (ring foundations) and −2.4 / −3.4 / −2.8 % (default mesh foundations), radial trunk −0.5 / −0.3 %. Golden cases: gen_tn_default, gen_tn_measurement_overhead_leads (moves declared in fix2gen.toml).
    • Polygon foundations of thin outlines (10.54; audit A3SG-07): with foundation_outline="polygon" an outline whose oriented bounding rectangle is narrower than MIN_FOUNDATION_SIDE_M (1 m) is replaced by that rectangle widened to 1 m, with the same UserWarning as for "ombr" (a ring along an outline 0.5 to 1 m wide ran along both faces of a wall). Outlines wider than 1 m on average are unchanged.
    • TnNetworkGenerator.build() of large networks (no finding): the materialiser hands objects instead of names to the world, so bonds and PEN sections need no search through the world: 1000 default houses 6.5 → 4.4 s (star) and 7.3 → 5.4 s (radial trunk), worlds byte-identical. The rest of the cost is the name checks of World.add_electrode / add_conductor, linear in the size of the world.

    Fixed¶

    • build() failed for 1–3 % of random TN networks (10.38; audit A3SG-01): a service drop whose tap fell 0.1 µm to 4.7 mm from a node of the cable left a section that the Conductor model refuses (series resistance below 1e-6 Ω), so build() raised pydantic.ValidationError (1 of 100 random 60-house and 2 of 60 random 200-house networks). The layout builder had the same defect for taps a few millimetres from a cable corner or from another tap; it now merges cable nodes closer than the shortest section and refuses a cable between two sites closer than it with ValueError. Tests: tests/test_pen_routing.py::test_a_building_millimetres_beside_a_node_still_builds, ::test_a_tap_millimetres_from_an_earlier_tap_snaps_to_it, ::test_random_position_network_builds, ::test_taps_on_a_thick_pen_keep_sections_the_conductor_model_accepts, ::test_random_networks_build_and_route_as_documented (400 random networks, 60 / 200 houses, both topologies), tests/test_lv_network_layout.py::test_taps_millimetres_apart_share_one_node, ::test_a_tap_millimetres_from_a_cable_corner_is_the_corner, ::test_a_cable_between_two_sites_closer_than_a_section_is_rejected.
    • Cabinets chained on one cable, drops hung on other drops (10.38; audit A3SG-02, A3SG-03): the nearest-point tree of the first 2026.11 draft fed a cabinet from the cable of a nearer one, although StarCabinetTopology and TnNetworkConfig.pen_topology document a direct cable per cabinet (input impedance +1.8 / +2.6 % for 100 / 200 houses against the 0.15.0 routing, against the documented direction), and started most drops on other houses' drops (26 of 30 in the radial default grid with ring foundations, 19 of 30 in the default star network). Tests: tests/test_pen_routing.py::test_star_topology_wires_every_cabinet_directly_to_the_substation, ::test_collinear_cabinets_have_separate_cables_in_one_trench, ::test_z_in_of_a_100_house_network_drops_against_the_old_routing, ::test_a_drop_taps_the_cable_of_its_own_cabinet, ::test_no_drop_starts_on_another_drop_in_the_radial_default_grid, ::test_overlapping_drops_and_trunks_stay_passive, ::test_routing_guarantees.
    • A loaded BusType document crashed the other entry points (10.01; audit A3SG-04): load_bustype_json accepted documents for which fit_quality_summary raised AttributeError, TypeError, ValueError or OverflowError (fit_quality null or a list; a figure that is an object, a list, a string or a 400-digit integer), to_dict() raised RecursionError (metadata nested 3 000 levels, which the JSON decoder reads) or wrote a NaN description (not strict JSON). The loader now refuses a name or system_type that is an object or a list, a description that is neither a string nor null, an impedance_formula that is an object or a list (EvaluateSpecError) and metadata deeper than MAX_METADATA_DEPTH; to_dict() writes a non-string description as null and walks the metadata without recursion; fit_quality_summary reads only finite numbers and strings. Tests: tests/test_io_groundinsight.py::test_crafted_bustype_documents_raise_value_error, ::test_a_loaded_document_never_crashes_the_other_entry_points, ::test_fit_quality_summary_reads_the_figures_it_can, ::test_to_dict_of_a_spec_built_in_memory_is_strict_json_or_refused.
    • A sum with an exact small imaginary part parsed to zero (10.01; audit A3SG-05): the formula parser drops a collected sum that cancels within its 128-bit working precision, but compared the complex magnitude of the sum with its largest term: (1 + 1e-31*j - 1)*1e31*f evaluated to 0 instead of 50j at 50 Hz. Real and imaginary parts are now judged separately; the 119 accepted formulas of the corpus evaluate as before (worst 1.9e-15 against a 60-digit mpmath evaluation). Test: tests/test_formula_security.py::test_real_and_imaginary_parts_of_a_sum_cancel_separately.
    • Polygon foundations of outlines narrower than 0.5 m (10.54; audit A3SG-07): LvNetworkLayout.to_world(foundation_outline="polygon") raised ValueError ("polygon collapses ... after removing sub-centimetre edges", although the edge limit is 0.5 m) for every such outline the Overpass parser keeps; it is widened now (see Changed), and the materialiser's last check names the real limit. Tests: tests/test_footprint_foundations.py::test_a_thin_outline_with_a_polygon_foundation_is_widened, ::test_a_polygon_foundation_of_an_ordinary_outline_is_unchanged, ::test_a_collapsing_polygon_outline_names_the_real_edge_limit.
    • Margin of generated mesh foundations against mom (10.39; audit A3SG-06): the documentation said one current per wire is +0.3 / +0.5 / +0.6 % "against mom"; that is the line kernel against a line-kernel per-segment reference. Against mom (point kernel) the default line kernel is +1.1 % and kernel="point" +0.3 … +0.5 % for 10 m 2 × 2 to 30 m 4 × 4 meshes; one current for the whole mesh was +2.4 … +3.7 % (point kernel +1.5 … +3.1 %). Test: tests/test_foundation_owners.py::test_documented_margin_of_mesh_foundations_against_mom.

    Stream dsafety: User decisions Q3 and Q4: touch points, EN 50522 beyond 10 s¶

    Added¶

    • Touch points: touch_voltage_at_part (10.21; user decision Q3): the EN 50522 touch voltage of an accessible metal part above ground — a station housing or its door, a mast, a fence post, a cable cabinet, a service connection box. The part is given by its horizontal position and the electrode or junction it is bonded to (bonded_to=), whose potential it carries; a part bonded to a PEN junction expresses a transferred potential. The feet stand on the circle of radius distance (1 m) around the part (n_angles, default 24), optionally only in given directions, and the positions inside the outlines in exclude (the housing or the building, where nobody can stand) are dropped. Returns the touch-voltage phasors and azimuths per foot position like touch_voltage_envelope (and the positions with return_points=True). Available as gf.touch_voltage_at_part and gf.safety.touch_voltage_at_part. A service connection box 40 m from a ring earth (100 A, 100 Ω·m), bonded to the PEN, has 1081 V of the 1121 V earth potential rise as touch voltage. Tests: tests/test_touch_voltage_at_part.py::test_part_on_a_wire_matches_touch_voltage_on_the_offset_curve, tests/test_touch_voltage_at_part.py::test_part_at_a_rod_head_against_the_analytic_surface_potential, tests/test_touch_voltage_at_part.py::test_part_bonded_to_a_pen_junction_carries_the_transferred_potential.
    • How-to "Assess touch voltages" (docs/how-to/assess-touch-voltages.md): touch points of a compact station, the transferred potential over the PEN, the meshes of an open-air grid, and the comparison with the permissible values; tutorial T08 adds a fence bonded to the substation grid (199.5 V at the corner post against 140.7 V 1 m outside the grid, mom, 150 A) and an earth fault that is not cleared.

    Changed¶

    • ⚠ touch_voltage_envelope samples no interior by default (10.21; user decision Q3): inside an electrode's footprint stands the station, the house, the steel structure or the mast, so the default is now interior=False for every electrode kind: the curve distance outside the wires only. The mesh sampling of the development snapshots (the centres of the meshes of grids and of generated mesh foundations, the centre of a ring) is the explicit interior="meshes", for open-air grids such as a substation yard; interior=True also samples the inside of a closed polyline. interior=None is rejected with a hint. Against 0.15.0 the default envelope is the footprint's offset curve of 10.21: a ring of 10 m radius at 0.8 m (100 A, 100 Ω·m, image, point kernel) keeps 139.4 V (the development snapshots gave 182.6 V, its centre; with the default line kernel 142.2 / 185.5 V), a 20 m perimeter loop (1 × 1 grid) 141.4 V instead of 158.3 V in its centre, and the 20 m × 20 m grid with 4 × 4 meshes 111.4 V (unchanged: the maximum is 1 m outside a corner, the mesh centres reach 63.0 V). Affected: envelopes of rings, grids and generated foundations computed with the development snapshots; pass interior="meshes" for open-air grids. Golden cases: none (no case evaluates the envelope). Tests: tests/test_touch_voltage_envelope.py::test_envelope_samples_no_interior_by_default, tests/test_touch_voltage_envelope.py::test_release_note_envelopes_with_the_new_default.
    • ⚠ EN 50522 permissible touch voltage beyond 10 s (10.42; user decision Q4): permissible_touch_voltage_en50522(t_f) follows Figure 8 / Table B.4 of EN 50522:2022 up to 10 s and returns 80 V for every t_f > 10 s, including math.inf for an earth fault that is not cleared (resonant-earthed networks). The curve of Figure 8 ends at 10 s; the note to Figure 8 (5.4.1) and the text after Table B.4 (B.3) allow 80 V for current-flow durations considerably longer than 10 s, below the 85 V end value of the table. 0.15.0 returned 85 V beyond 10 s (now −5.9 %) and rejected math.inf. permissible_prospective_touch_voltage_en50522 uses the same U_Tp: beyond 10 s 80 V + 50 mA · R_A / HF (1000 Ω shoes on 100 Ω·m soil: 137.5 V instead of 142.5 V). Affected: assessments with fault durations above 10 s, e.g. resonant-earthed networks. Golden cases: none (safety_en50522_curve covers 0.05 … 10 s). Tests: tests/test_safety.py::test_en50522_beyond_ten_seconds_is_80_volt, tests/test_safety.py::test_prospective_touch_voltage_beyond_ten_seconds_uses_80_volt.

    Removed¶

    • long_duration= of permissible_touch_voltage_en50522 and permissible_prospective_touch_voltage_en50522 (added during this release cycle, never released): 80 V beyond 10 s is the default. Test: tests/test_safety.py::test_en50522_has_no_long_duration_keyword.

    Deprecated¶

    • Nothing.

    Stream drhoeff: User decision Q6: effective resistivity¶

    Added¶

    • effective_resistivity (10.84, user decision Q6): the effective resistivity of a homogeneous or two-layer soil in Ω·m, ρ_eff = ρ₁^(1−w) · ρ₂^w with w = w0 · (h₁ / h_ref)^(−β), h_ref = 10 m and the AP1 calibration w0 = 0.13, β = 0.8 (group-specific values as w0=, beta=, h_ref=). gf.effective_resistivity(soil) (also groundfield.soil.effective_resistivity, or with the keywords rho_1, rho_2, h_1) returns ρ itself for homogeneous soil and for stacks of equal layers, without round-off (the plain formula gives 200.00000000000003 Ω·m for 200 / 200 Ω·m, h₁ = 10 m); reduces a MultiLayerSoil to its canonical stack (equal adjacent layers merged, as the layered backends do, 10.48) and raises ValueError for three or more distinct layers; limits w to [0, 1], so ρ_eff stays between ρ₁ and ρ₂ (the unlimited formula leaves that interval below h₁ = 0.78 m: 17.9 Ω·m for 100 / 30 Ω·m at h₁ = 0.5 m; with the limit such a thin upper layer gives ρ₂). Inside the calibration range of the AP1 catalogue (h₁ = 5 … 30 m, ρ₁ = 30 … 1000 Ω·m, ρ₂ = 30 … 200 Ω·m) the value is bit-identical to the AP1 / AP2 scripts' r1 ** (1 - w) * r2 ** w and agrees with a 50-digit evaluation to 1.6e-16. Test: tests/test_effective_resistivity.py.

    Changed¶

    • ⚠ The rho of a BusType in layered soil is the effective resistivity (10.84, user decision Q6): rho_at_fit of a vector fit and the rho samples of a standard form fitted in layered soil are ρ_eff of the soil they were computed in, effective_resistivity(soil), the specific_earth_resistance the AP2 network model gives a groundinsight bus. This replaces the upper-layer resistivity ρ₁ that the 10.84 entry of this release cycle had made the convention; on the AP1 grid ρ_eff differs from ρ₁ by −54.8 % (1000 over 30 Ω·m, h₁ = 5 m) to +53.6 % (30 over 200 Ω·m, h₁ = 5 m). The error message for a missing rho_at_fit, the docstrings of the four exporters, the IO and Soil references, ADR-0008 and tutorial T05 state it. Every BusType document records the convention as metadata["rho_definition"] = "effective_resistivity" (documents of 0.15.0 have no such entry). No function computes a different number: the exporter writes the rho it is given. Affected: BusTypes exported in layered soil with ρ₁ as rho. A compact electrode in the upper layer follows ρ₁ more closely than ρ_eff of the AP1 networks: the station of the IO example (2 m ring and 3 m rod) swept over ρ₁ fits the standard form to 13.4 / 8.3 / 3.6 % over ρ_eff and to 3.9 / 1.9 / 0.6 % over ρ₁ for h₁ = 5 / 10 / 30 m; check max_relative. Golden cases: none. Tests: tests/test_io_groundinsight.py::test_rho_at_fit_convention_for_layered_soil_is_the_effective_resistivity, ::test_bustype_metadata_records_the_rho_definition, ::test_layered_soil_exports_pair_with_an_effective_resistivity_bus.

    Stream ddefaults: User decisions Q15 to Q17: galerkin, tabulated Sommerfeld, resource limit¶

    Added¶

    • Per-engine resource limit Engine.max_segments (10.06, user decision Q17): the hard problem-size limit of the segment backends — the number of leakage segments and the number of unknowns of the dense nodal system — is set per engine: Engine(max_segments=...) (also a create_engine parameter) raises or lowers it for one engine, a positive integer; None (default) takes the module constant groundfield.diagnostics.HARD_LIMIT (20 000), read at solve time, so the constants stay the defaults. Every segment backend (the image family, mom, mom_sommerfeld), solve_mutual_matrix and solve_mutual_field refuse a larger world with ResourceLimitError before anything of that size is allocated; check_segment_budget, expected_segments (new key "limit"), check_segment_resolution and diagnose (report.context["max_segments"]) check against the same limit, and the messages name the setting to change. The limit is recorded in the new core metadata key FieldResult.metadata["max_segments"] (the engine block of the provenance keeps the request itself). SOFT_LIMIT (5 000, the size above which the solve reports that it may take minutes) stays a module constant. Tests: tests/test_resource_limit.py.

    Changed¶

    • ⚠ solve_mutual_matrix uses the galerkin off-diagonal for every call (10.34, user decision Q15): offdiag="galerkin" (the reaction of the leakage distributions: reciprocal, and the matrix with which (Y_L + Z_G⁻¹)u = i reproduces the solve of the clusters joined by branches) is the default whatever the call; offdiag="probe" (the potential at one probe point per cluster, the definition of releases up to 0.15.0) stays available. A call that passes probe_points without offdiag="probe" — every 0.15.0 call, the AP1 catalogue pipeline among them — returns the galerkin matrix; the points are ignored, not validated, with the new groundfield.solver.mutual.ProbePointsIgnoredWarning, which names offdiag="probe" as the way back. This supersedes the selection rule added earlier in this release cycle (offdiag=None choosing "probe" when probe points were given; None is no longer accepted). The diagonal does not move. On an AP1-like site (station ring and three rods, twelve polygon foundations, ds = 2 m, five soils) the symmetrised probe matrix missed the input impedance of the joined network by −0.15 … −0.88 % and single off-diagonal entries by up to 41 % (median 0.15 … 1.1 %); the galerkin matrix reproduces it to 7e-13. On the AP1 catalogue path (group-2 site, 7 corner soils × 4 penetrations) |Z_ons| at 50 Hz moves by −0.05 … +0.29 % at penetrations up to 20 % and by +0.25 … +2.67 % at 40 % (largest at ρ₁ = 1000 Ω·m), the ONS current share at 50 Hz by up to +2.7 %. Who is affected: every Z_G built with probe points, the AP1 catalogue. Golden cases: mutual_foundations_lumped_concrete, mutual_foundations_distributed_concrete, mutual_three_rods_crosslayer, line_mutual_two_layer (Z_G norm-wise 7.7e-4 … 8.8e-4, single off-diagonal entries 0.48 … 5.9 %). Tests: tests/test_mutual_matrix.py::test_galerkin_is_the_default_whatever_the_call, ::test_ignored_probe_points_are_not_validated, ::test_offdiag_selection_and_validation.
    • ⚠ Tabulated Sommerfeld assembly by default (10.60, user decision Q16): Engine.sommerfeld_assembly defaults to "tabulated" (one table of the spectral integral per pair of depth groups and frequency); "exact" (one quadrature per segment pair, the default up to 0.15.0) stays available. The image family, mom, mom_sommerfeld and solve_mutual_* follow the setting alike; engine JSON without the field (0.15.0) gets the new default. Blocks of fewer than 64 sub-segment pairs keep the per-pair rule, so small inductive worlds do not move. On a TN network with an inductive PEN (TnNetworkGenerator, homogeneous 100 Ω·m, ds = 1 m, four frequencies 50 … 1000 Hz) the input impedance of the tabulated solve lies within 2e-10 of two independent refined references (per-pair rule with 200 instead of 24 interpolation nodes; tables of 400 instead of 100 nodes; both with a refined λ grid), which agree with each other to 2e-10, while the per-pair rule is 1.2e-8 off (8 houses); CPU time 1.0 s instead of 16.6 s (8 houses, 78 inductive branches) and 12.2 s instead of 306 s (30 houses, 338 inductive branches; input impedance equal to 1.1e-8, the tabulated one 1.8e-11 from the 400-node tables). The correction matrices of small conductor sets in twelve critical geometries (buried, overhead, 1 cm below the surface, in the surface plane, crossing the surface or a layer interface, two- and three-layer earth, 250 m segments, 10 and 1000 Ω·m; 50 Hz and 1 kHz) are finite, symmetric and free of warnings; against a reference refined in S(ρ) and in the λ grid the tabulated rule is closer than the per-pair rule in ten of them (1.5e-7 … 1.1e-5 against 4.2e-6 … 7.6e-5 of the largest entry) and equal in two (the surface plane, where both use the per-pair rule, and a riser through h₁, 4.3e-6). Who is affected: inductive conductors under earth_inductive_model="sommerfeld" (the default) with blocks of at least 64 sub-segment pairs; numbers move by about 1e-8 … 2.4e-6. Golden case: cond_lumped_long_pen_sommerfeld (≤ 2.4e-6; against the refined per-pair reference the tabulated result is 4.2e-7 off, the per-pair rule 2.0e-6). Tests: tests/test_sommerfeld_assembly_default.py.

    Decisions on the questions left open (2026-11-08)¶

    After the third adversarial audit and the user's decisions Q1–Q19, the release preparation left 17 questions to the author, each with a recommendation. The author followed every recommendation. The table records the question, the decision and where it went; the work streams d2solver, d2gen and d2soil below implemented the decisions that change code.

    Q Question Decision Where
    20 Encase the cross-braces of mesh foundation electrodes in the concrete shell, as DIN 18014 lays the foundation earth electrode with its connections in the concrete? yes streams d2gen, f2gen
    21 Deprecate FoundationElectrodeSpec.concrete_model, which only chooses one or four current owners of a ring foundation (≤ 0.06 % apart up to aspect ratio 4)? yes; the measured difference is larger (< 1 % / < 2 % up to aspect ratio 4 / 50, see the box below; for the author's review) streams d2gen, f2gen, f3gen
    22 Keep the PEN routing of TnNetworkGenerator (an own cable per cabinet, a service drop taps the cable that feeds its building)? Place cabinets per building group in the future? routing kept; cabinets per building group moved into 2026.11.0 (user decision of 2026-11-14, stream w1gen; a wide group's cabinet on the ray through its far-end building since audit W1B-01, stream fw1gen; the 20 m step of the rule stays, user decision D7, stream fw1r2gen) changelog
    23 fem whose equivalent hemisphere puts current into a more conductive layer that the electrodes do not touch (−38 … −98 %): error instead of warning? error; extended after the audit to a more conductive layer within the electrodes' horizontal half-extent (coordinator, for the author's review) streams d2solver, f2solver, f3solver
    24 Point-form guard near a layer interface: keep the conservative thresholds (warning within 0.6 segment lengths, error within 0.387)? kept known limitations
    25 Accept the step of carson_series at the 8.1° boundary of its validity domain as a documented limit? accepted known limitations, earth return
    26 Accept that the simulated in-line fall-of-potential reading moves because the leads now couple as wires lying side by side (Im Z_meas 0.063 → 0.054 Ω at 50 Hz)? accepted release notes, 10.46
    27 Warn when perfect_mirror couples conductors on opposite sides of the soil surface through its additive image? warning streams d2solver, f2solver, f3solver
    28 Count powers of numbers (10**40) in the rule on nested exponents of the formula parser, as groundinsight does? yes; already the rule ((f*10**40)**2 is rejected, tests/test_formula_security.py::test_exponents_of_nested_powers_multiply_to_at_most_max_pow) no change
    29 Only document that printing a parsed formula or passing it to lambdify lets SymPy evaluate parts of an untrusted formula again? documented IO reference
    30 One current per wire line for grids and stars in the image family (−3 … −5 %)? moved into 2026.11.0 (user decision of 2026-11-14, stream w1sol): grids one owner per wire line (−1.2 … −5.5 %), building outlines per side (Q44); a star alone does not change (equal arm currents), its remaining error is on the roadmap changelog

    34 Tighten the rule for segments up to a quarter skin depth under sommerfeld (step of 3.5e-4 at δ/4)? a later release (2026-11-08); moved into 2026.11.0 by the user decision of 2026-11-14 done in 2026.11.0 (streams w3perf, fw3perf): exact one-dimensional self rule and graded close-pair rule; with the λ grid of audit W3B-01 the self correction is within 1.1e-9 of a refined rule from 1 m and 0.01 δ to 3 km at 50 Hz and 1 kHz for the soils tested (up to 3.4e-9 in high-contrast soils, audit W3R2B-08), no step (was 4.8e-4, step 3.5e-4); close pairs within about 4e-8 (were up to 1.5e-3); five golden cases move by up to 4.9e-7 (changelog)
    35 Radial trunk: a building in a substation slot taps its feeder's trunk instead of getting its own line from the substation? moved into 2026.11.0 together with 22 (stream w1gen) changelog
    36 Presets of the effective-resistivity weights per electrode class, with the values of the AP2 hand-over? yes streams d2soil, f2gen

    The audits of the decision streams left four more questions, answered on the same day; the author again followed the recommendations and chose the next release for 37 and 39, this release for 38, and for 40 a quantification only once a 3-D volume reference exists. The earlier wording of 37 pointed to a "conforming axisymmetric" roadmap item that did not exist yet. With the user decision of 2026-11-14, 37 and 39 moved into 2026.11.0, and the 3-D model of wave 4 gave the reference that 40 waited for; the rows say where each was done.

    Q Question Decision Where
