    • Left open (before the stream as well): in point form a ring at the interface differs by up to 2.96e-4 between the series and the cross-layer path (the default by 1.3e-6), and solve_mutual_matrix(symmetrize=False) of a cross-layer world is asymmetric by 2.3e-8 in both forms.
    • Golden: 563 of 563 quantities identical; 29 new tests.

    Stream fw3r2b: fixes of audit W3R2B¶

    • Rows under jitter (W3R2B-01): two drops of one node belong to one row when their start points lie within the touching distance d_t of each other and the shorter drop's end lies within d_t of the longer drop's line; drops that are not exactly parallel lie along one common normal (PlannedBranch.axis_offset_normal_xy), each at least d_t from the others, and a drop that would overlap a drop of a row joins it, also a drop of a tap 1 … 4 cm away on the same trunk (from 2 cm of jitter). The 10-house radial network at 1 nm of jitter: −8.5e-10 / −1.1e-9 instead of +2.4 % / +2.9 %; no generated network reports an overlap with jitter_m 0 … 0.1 m (240 networks, star and radial, 5 … 200 houses); every network without jitter is bit-identical.
    • Projected coordinates (W3R2B-02): the tolerance at the touching distance is the larger of 1e-9 of it and 16 units in the last place of the pair's largest coordinate; local worlds keep their findings bit for bit.
    • Values and documents (W3R2B-05, -06): a PenetrationDepth built directly is validated and its hash agrees with ==; metadata["rho_preset"] must be a known preset name or null, and only exact preset lines of a description are removed or replaced.
    • plan() and build(): build() right after plan() on the same generator gives the world of that plan; successive calls still draw successive realisations.
    • Golden: 563 of 563 quantities identical.

    Wave 4: a conforming fem (Q37, Q40, D6)¶

    Wave 4 replaced the equivalent-hemisphere reduction of fem by conforming finite-element models (user decision Q37): an axisymmetric model (stream w4axi, part A) and a three-dimensional model for every other world (stream w4fem3d, part B), which also quantified the overlap of concrete shells at junctions (Q40). Its dependencies follow decision D6.

    Stream w4axi: the conforming axisymmetric model (part A)¶

    Rods on one vertical axis and horizontal rings centred on it are solved as the solid bodies they are (a cylinder with flat end faces, a torus), in any number of horizontal layers, bare or with a concrete shell, each electrode one port: linear triangles on a graded tensor mesh conforming to every electrode surface and layer interface, an O-grid around the ring wire, the monopole Dirichlet-to-Neumann condition on a box 1000 lengths away, and the port conductance matrix extrapolated from two nested levels; no new dependency. The network of the shared pipeline runs on top. In short:

    • Accuracy: within about 1e-4 of independent references (thin-wire limits, elliptic and Hankel rings, finite volumes of the solid rod) at 0.2 … 2 s per solve; the limit cases of the reduction — a ring 1 cm above an interface (+191 … +308 % without a message), a rod reaching 1 µm … 8 cm into a more conductive top layer (−44 … −78 %, warning only), a ring exactly on an interface, rods through one, clusters of several electrodes and several clusters — are solved within 1e-4 (within 2e-3 by the 3-D model of part B where they are not axisymmetric; audit W4A-01 found the exception of a ring wire at an interface, see below).
    • Numbers: every fem result moves: the stored 3 m rods −4.65 % / −4.57 % (Dwight's rod ignored the head depth), the B2 rod / B3 ring −0.70 % / −0.35 %, a ring with a bonded central rod +20 %, a ring 1 cm above a conductive layer −67 %, a rod 2 cm into a conductive top layer +164 %. Every other world was refused until part B.
    • Options: FemOptions.n_radial, n_axial and z_far_factor are gone (2026.11 names); the point sources of fem count against Engine.max_segments (found in the stream's own attack); benchmark B1 checks fem against Weber's disc.
    • For image_2layer: the stream's references found a ring 1 cm above an interface 1.7 % above the exact thin ring, a jump of 0.03 … 0.38 % between the two sides for a ring on an interface, and a truncated series in 30/10 000 Ω·m (−1.0 %); wave 3 resolved the jump, and audit W3A-02 found the cause of the first.
    • Golden: fem_rod_hom −4.65 % and fem_rod_two_layer_deep −4.57 % (w4fem.toml); the other 47 cases are bit-identical.

    Stream w4fem3d: the three-dimensional conforming model (part B; Q40, D6)¶

    Every world that is not axisymmetric — strips, polylines, stars, meshes, grids, rods and rings side by side, several clusters with their transfer resistances, galvanic distributed conductors with every leaking piece a port — is solved with smoothed line sources and an analytic radius correction in an isotropic tetrahedral mesh refined towards them, partially penalised immersed layer interfaces, conjugate gradients with an algebraic multigrid, and two nested levels extrapolated. It needs the optional extra fem3d (scikit-fem, BSD-3-Clause; pyamg, MIT; decision D6) and raises ImportError naming it otherwise. In short:

    • Against part A: within 2.0e-3 for rods and rings in one to three layers, inside its own error estimate (away from interfaces and other wires since audits W4B-01, W4B-02, see below).
    • The AP1 station in the corner soils of the AP1 grid lies between the thin-wire engines: image_2layer +0.7 … +3.0 % above it, mom −0.4 … −0.9 % below; 1.5 … 3 minutes and 3.6 … 4.4 GB per solve.
    • Brace effect (the open point of w2mom): +49.09 / +317.99 % at ρ_c = 1000 / 5000 Ω·m, which confirms the per-segment value of the concrete-shell page (+49.2 / +318.5 %) to 0.1 / 0.5 points.
    • Q40: against the concrete resolved as a volume, each junction of encased wires acts like at most about one shell radius of wire (a corner −1.5 cm, a crossing −3 … −6 cm, a T-joint within ±5 cm; 5 mm bar, 5 cm cover, ρ_c 1 000 / 5 000 Ω·m); the generators' house mesh changes by less than 0.6 %, so no correction rule is applied (audit W4B-05 found these numbers not converged, see below).
    • Golden: nothing moves (every golden fem case is axisymmetric and stays on part A bit for bit); the 3-D model has no golden case.

    Audit W4A: the axisymmetric model and the shared network¶

    The first wave-4 audit checked part A and the network on top of it (snapshot 4e37760) against references without groundfield code: axisymmetric boundary elements for the solid cylinder and torus with the Tagg/Sunde images as rings, and cell-centred finite volumes of a rod with a shell. Findings:

    • W4A-01 (medium): a ring wire cut by an interface to a more conductive layer, or within 0.05 wire radii of it, is off by up to 6.0e-3 at the default; the error estimate lies below the error in 7 of 14 cases, and the levels are not monotone.
    • W4A-02, W4A-03 (low): extrapolate=False reports an error estimate of 0 for a level-0 ring 6.2e-3 low; model="auto" ends in a bare ValueError for a ring diagonal to a rod's end edge, without falling back to the 3-D model or naming a remedy.
    • W4A-04 (low): tutorial T09 still says that fem refuses every other world.
    • W4A-05 (medium): the node limit of 4 M admits about 16 GB (3.1 / 3.8 / 12.6 GB at 0.80 / 0.91 / 3.21 M nodes), and no memory figure is documented.
    • W4A-06 (low): the provenance line of the validation page names Linux and 2026-11-14 for fem rows measured on macOS on 2026-11-15.
    • W4A-07 (medium): the known limitation on rings near an interface quotes +3.6 % at 1 cm only; closer in, image_2layer reads +8.6 … +93 % (at 1.5 … 0.95 wire radii) without a warning, and diagnose() is silent.
    • W4A-08, W4A-09 (low): the dispatch threshold of 3 nm sends a rod 1 µm off the ring axis to the 3-D model (40.7 s / 2.35 GB instead of 1.8 s / 0.61 GB, −1.43e-3, and it needs the extra); stale or overstated texts ("every other world is refused", "lower bound / monotone", "within 5 %").
    • W4A-10, W4A-11 (info): predicted_nodes is 1.5 … 2.3 % low for rings; the second methods of the validation table are thin-wire models or none (the solid-body boundary elements: −1.07e-5 … −3.0e-5).

    Confirmed: the default fem agrees with the boundary elements to 1.0e-5 … 1.5e-4 for rods, rings, rings near and on interfaces, rods through and near interfaces and two-port worlds, with an error estimate of 10 … 130 times the error; three layers and concrete shells against further references; the network (U = R·I to 8e-17, one branch matrix for fem and image_2layer, round trips, a UTM world and the D5 warning); the refusals, the light import and the extra; golden 559 of 563 quantities identical, the 4 that move in the two declared fem cases. Taken up by the wave-4 fixes below.

    Audit W4B: the three-dimensional model¶

    The second wave-4 audit checked part B, decision Q40 and the extra fem3d (snapshot 4e37760) against the axisymmetric model, the exact thin rings and closed forms of the smoothed line source. Findings:

    • W4B-01 (medium): the radius correction added terms for the surface image and for every wire within 20 R, where the mesh already gives the exact field: shallow wires up to +1.9e-3 high, two wires 10 cm apart +4.6e-3, above the error estimate.
    • W4B-02 (medium): neither "within 2e-3 of the axisymmetric model" nor "the estimate bounds the error" held for every world the guards accept: a ring 1.5 cm above a 1000 / 30 Ω·m interface read +9.1e-3 against an estimate of 5.4e-3, its two levels on either side of the reference.
    • W4B-03 (low–medium): at a layer contrast of 1000 the conjugate gradients did not converge in 3000 iterations and ended after about a minute in a bare RuntimeError; the pages said "any contrast".
    • W4B-04, W4B-05 (low–medium): the square concrete prism keeps the shell's outer conformal radius, but its inner one is 0.914 r_b, so its concrete leaks 3.9 % per metre more than the shell, which the texts blamed on the discretisation; the Q40 junction numbers were not converged (one face refinement moved the corner by −4.9 / −7.1 cm), although "no correction rule" stays justified.
    • W4B-06, W4B-07 (low–medium): the face key of the immersed elements aliased above 2²¹ nodes (the default limit of 2 million lies 4.6 % below) and coupled unrelated elements; results depended on numpy's global random state (1e-12 … 1.9e-6 apart), and every solve advanced the user's stream.
    • W4B-08, W4B-09, W4B-10 (low): Engine.max_segments was checked only after both levels were solved, and the number of ports had no limit; fem_peak_memory_gb reported the peak of the whole process; about 30 stale texts.

    Confirmed: no stored golden number changes (563 of 563 quantities against the merge parent); the AP1 station, the brace effect (+49.09 / +317.99 %) and the Q40 numbers reproduce to the last digit; the light import, the message without the extra, the lock file, the licences and the ci: commit are correct; keeping fem experimental is justified. Taken up by the wave-4 fixes below.

    Streams fw4axi and fw4fem3d: fixes of audits W4A and W4B¶

    • fw4axi, a ring next to a more conductive layer (W4A-01): a ring wire within 1.05 radii of an interface gets 64 instead of 8 tensor intervals per side of its mesh block. The auditor's 14 cases are within 3.3e-4 of the boundary elements (were 6.0e-3), and the error estimate (7.5e-4 … 1.9e-3) is at least the error in each of them (was below it in 7); such a ring takes about four times the nodes (0.37 → 2.3 … 2.7 s).
    • fw4axi (W4A-02, -03, -05, -08, -10): a single level reports an error estimate of NaN; auto solves a ring without room for its mesh block with the 3-D model, and model="axisymmetric" refuses it with a remedy; the common axis has a tolerance of 1e-3 of the smallest wire radius or gap (the axisymmetric reading then errs by less than 1e-6), so a rod 1 µm off a ring's axis stays axisymmetric; FemOptions.max_nodes defaults to 2 million (about 4 GB per million nodes, re-measured 3.92 … 4.21), and the node prediction errs high (+0.67 … +10.8 %).
    • fw4axi, the thin wire next to a contrasting interface (W4A-07, W4B-02): InterfaceContactWarning and the diagnose() category interface_contact for horizontal wires within the reach where the thin wire deviates from the solid wire by more than about 1 %, measured against the axisymmetric torus and the Hankel thin ring with an exact 2-D proximity correction at contrasts 1.5 … 1000 in both directions: 1.0 / 1.5 / 2.9 / 3.5 / 3.8 / 3.9 wire radii over a layer 2 / 3 / 10 / 33 / 100 / 1000 times more conductive, 1.0 … 1.25 radii over a more resistive one, nothing below a contrast of 2. Emitted by the image family, mom, mom_sommerfeld, solve_mutual_* and the 3-D fem; no generated network, no world of the AP1 soils and no golden case is reported.
    • fw4axi, texts (W4A-04, -09, W4B-10): tutorial T09 describes both models; the stale texts of the auditors' lists in the reference pages, the docstrings and ADR-0002.
    • fw4fem3d, the radius correction (W4B-01): it counts the observer's own wire whole and, of every other wire and surface image, only the part within 1.5 R (a clip at 2 R would still keep 2 % of the self term at 1.5 R). A ring r = 1 m at 0.06 … 0.8 m depth lies −1.62e-3 … −1.83e-3 from the axisymmetric model (spread 2.1e-4, was 2.1e-3); two coaxial rings 10 cm apart −1.19e-3 / −1.48e-3, inside the estimate of 3.87e-3 (were +4.60e-3 / +3.67e-3); the AP1 station moves by about −1e-4, the brace effect to +49.14 / +318.23 %.
    • fw4fem3d, the accuracy texts (W4B-02): "within 2e-3 of the axisymmetric model" holds away from interfaces and other wires; the error estimate bounds the error while the ratio of the level errors is at least 2.5 (2.1 … 2.7 times the error for rings), and not for resolved concrete. The ring 1.5 cm above a 1000 / 30 Ω·m interface still reads +9.2e-3 against 5.4e-3: the thin-wire error, which the new warning reports.
    • fw4fem3d (W4B-03 … -09): a conjugate-gradient solve that does not converge (contrast above the tested 300) raises BackendCapabilityError naming the contrast, the axisymmetric model and the integral engines (the auditor's preconditioner gave no gain); the texts say that the square prism's concrete is 3.8 % less resistive (inner conformal radius 0.914 r_b, two methods) and mark the junction numbers not converged — a few cm of wire per junction, at most about 2 r_b, sign not settled, the house mesh 0.35 … 0.85 % re-measured; faces are paired with np.lexsort; numpy's global random state is saved, seeded and restored around the multigrid set-up (results bit-identical whatever the user's seed, the user's stream unchanged); Engine.max_segments and the number of ports (100; about 0.95 s per port and level) are checked before meshing; fem_peak_memory_gb is what the solve added (about 2.9 GB per million nodes of the finer level), fem_process_peak_memory_gb the process peak.
    • Golden: 563 of 563 quantities identical in both streams.

    Stream fw4int: the loose ends of the wave-4 fixes¶

    • The texts of fw4axi on the fem page and in ADR-0016, fitted to those of fw4fem3d; the validation rows next to and on an interface re-measured: image_2layer reads a ring 1 cm above a 1000 / 30 Ω·m interface +3.46 … +3.57 % above fem (the page said +5.2 … +5.4 %, from before wave 3), the ring on an interface within 1.2e-5 of the solid-torus boundary elements.
    • FemOptions.max_ports_3d (default 100): the port limit of the 3-D model is an option, and its refusal names it.
    • Benchmarks B8 and B10 exclude fem for its cost; measured once with the 3-D model, both are reciprocal to round-off, B10 +0.6 / +0.8 % above mom. The transfer impedance of B8 lies 0.40 … 0.96 % below mom, more than the error estimate; handed to the second audit round.
    • A test that the 3-D model warns once for a wire next to a contrasting interface; the call was already in place.
    • Golden: 563 of 563 quantities identical.

    Audits W4R2A and W4R2B: round 2 on the wave-4 fixes¶

    The second round tried to break the wave-4 fixes (snapshot 4eb1c2c) with the boundary elements of round 1, the axisymmetric model, mom refined and closed forms of the smoothed line source. Findings:

    • W4R2A-01, W4R2B-03 (medium): the 3-D model next to another wire. The fallback of W4A-03 sent a ring without room for its mesh block to the 3-D model, which read two diagonal rings 1.3 … 3.8 mm apart +11.4 … +14.3 % high against an estimate of 0.42 %, without a message; coaxial rings 7.5 cm apart, where the texts promised 2e-3, read +4.35e-3, 6 cm apart +1.0e-2, and below one source radius the port entries were off by up to 4.3 %; the radius correction counted other ports' wires with the observer's current.
    • W4R2A-02 (medium): the refined ring block held up to a contrast of 33; a ring touching a layer 1000 times more conductive read −1.61e-2 against an estimate of 1.07e-2, and at refinement=1 the estimate fell below the error at contrasts 100 and 1000.
    • W4R2A-03 (medium): the 3-D model neither warned nor refused a rod end at an interface (+43 % for a tip on a 1000 / 30 Ω·m interface).
    • W4R2B-01, W4R2B-02 (low–medium): on straight pieces longer than about 7 m off the coordinate axes the observer's own piece was clipped by round-off (a 20 m oblique strip −3.8e-4); an arm of the same polyline folded back at any angle but exactly parallel counted whole (a hairpin tilted by 1e-4 rad +1.41e-3, a 10° V +8.5e-4 against the same V as two strips).
    • W4R2A-04, W4R2A-05 (low): the reach of the near-interface warning lay 0.2 … 0.4 wire radii short for a thick wire on a small ring, and cut wires below a contrast of 2 read up to ±1.5 % without a warning; diagnose() with a fem engine reported a ring that the axisymmetric model solves.
    • W4R2B-04 … W4R2B-07, W4R2A-06, W4R2A-07 (low, info): resolved concrete with a thin cover ended in a bare ValueError after meshing; stale texts (level ratios, station memory, the junction table, the refusal of W4B-03); tests that failed on the base for the wrong reason.
    • B8 (open point of stream fw4int): the 3-D model is right; mom's floating port at the benchmark's 0.5 m is not converged. Refined to 1/32 m it falls by 0.20 … 0.75 % and ends 0.15 … 0.21 % above fem, and on a coaxial rod and ring the axisymmetric model confirms the 3-D transfer resistance to 1.3e-3.

    Confirmed: golden 563 of 563 quantities identical in both audits; one near-interface warning per solve in every backend that should warn and none from the axisymmetric model; no false positive over 868 tests and the tutorials; the depth residual, the coaxial rings at 10 cm and the brace effect reproduce to the digit; the refusals of W4B-03 and W4B-08, the determinism of W4B-07 and the memory keys hold.

    Streams fw4r2a and fw4r2b: fixes of audits W4R2A and W4R2B¶

    • fw4r2b, wires beside each other (W4R2A-01, W4R2B-03): two wires run beside each other where a point of one lies within 1.5 R of the side of the other and the two run within 30° of parallel, or where the whole of one lies within 1.5 R of another it does not meet; junctions, crossings at more than 30°, a rod on a ring and collinear pieces that meet are left alone. The model keeps such wires 1.5 R apart by a smaller source radius for the whole world and refuses them below 2.2 radii of the thickest bare wire of the world (3.3 such radii, axis to axis) before meshing, naming the wires, their distance, mom and image. Coaxial rings 4.5 … 10 cm apart lie within 1.72e-3 of the solid tori in every port entry and the bonded value, inside the estimate; 3 cm is refused by the node limit, the diagonal rings of W4R2A-01 and the ring at a rod head before meshing. The window term of another port counts only where the two wires meet; the coordinator kept this against the literal brief, since dropping it would put an L of two bonded strips 6.3e-4 below the same L as one polyline (1.0e-4 with it) without fixing any audited case.
    • fw4r2b, the own wire (W4R2B-01, -02): the axis distance comes from the cross product (the 20 m oblique strip matches its closed form to 4e-16); the own polyline counts whole only where it has turned by less than 120° (the tilted hairpin −1.9e-4, mom −2.0e-4; the jump at the threshold 1.5e-4 for 4 m arms, −1.26e-3 for 0.5 m arms, replaced in round 3 by a blend in the accumulated turn; the auditor's axis-line rule moved rings away from the solid torus and was not taken).
    • fw4r2b, the rest (W4R2B-04 … -07, W4R2A-07): resolved concrete with a thin cover is refused before meshing; the new fem_error_estimate_transfer (1.06 … 6.3 times the transfer error where the model holds; 6.3 on the coaxial analogue of B8); the B8 note says that mom at 0.5 m is not converged; the level ratios (rods 3.3 … 4.4, rings 3.0 … 3.4) and the CG refusal corrected; the W4B-01 tests fail on their base by assertion; solid_earth defaults to 2 million nodes. The AP1 station and the house mesh are unchanged to 7 digits.
    • fw4r2a, the ring block (W4R2A-02): graded towards the points where the interface cuts the wire at a narrow angle or comes closest to it, in angle and radius, from cells of 1e-3 wire radii, with 32 instead of 64 intervals. The estimate bounds the error in all 113 cases of the auditor's sweep at the default and at refinement=1; the default lies within 5.1e-5 of the boundary elements up to a contrast of 1000 (3.2e-5 at 100), at fewer nodes than before (a touching ring 151 807 nodes, 1.8 s, 0.76 GB).
    • fw4r2a, the warnings (W4R2A-03 … -05): the 3-D dispatch warns for rod ends at an interface; the reach table is re-measured on six ring geometries and covers all 2348 measured points that are 1 % off (34 lay outside the old one); cut wires are reported from a contrast of 1.4; no false positive over 741 tests, T01–T10 and 48 golden cases; diagnose() with a fem engine is silent for a world the axisymmetric model takes.
    • fw4r2a, the rest (W4R2A-06, -07): texts; a ring of radius 1.2 wire radii is solved (−7e-5 against the boundary elements) instead of refused, since no backend could otherwise.
    • New, found by fw4r2a: two mesh lines 5.6e-17 m apart (an interface at 0.3 m and a ring or rod tip at 0.1 + 0.2 m) gave a singular factor or, silently, 6809 Ω instead of 647 Ω; features within 1e-12 were one line, since audit W4R3A-01 within 1e-9 of max(1 m, x).
    • Golden: 563 of 563 quantities identical in both streams; full suite after the merge: 6137 passed, 0 failed.

    Audits W4R3A and W4R3B: round 3 on the wave-4 fixes¶

    A short round on the round-2 fixes (snapshot b727b0b), with the boundary elements of rounds 1 and 2, the axisymmetric model, mom refined, image and closed forms. Findings:

    • W4R3B-01 (medium): two collinear wires of different ports with a small end gap ("stacked rods") were left alone, and an end gap up to the wire radius counted as meeting: rods 2 mm … 3 cm apart read +5.1e-2 … +6.4e-3 in a port entry and −20 % … −2.9 % in the transfer entry, far outside both estimates, without a message.
    • W4R3B-02, W4R3B-03 (low–medium): wires that meet at less than 30° (a V, a branch) were refused with the distance of a sample point; the 120° rule tested a piece's direction, not the turn along the chain (an arm turned by 360° counted whole, a spiral as one polyline +2.08e-3 against its windings, the jump at 120° up to −1.26e-3 for short arms).
    • W4R3B-04, W4R3B-05 (low): wires met at an axis distance of the smaller radius, not where their surfaces touch (a 2 m cross jumped by −7.5e-4); the refusal radius is that of the thickest bare wire of the world.
    • W4R3A-01 (low): mesh features merged only within the snap tolerance, so a ring axis a few picometres off an interface still read +35 … +44 % or failed, silently.
    • W4R3A-02 (low): the resistive side of the reach table had been measured above the wire for a thin ring only; a 55 mm wire under a 33 times more resistive layer read +1.72 % at 1.3 radii, unreported.

    Confirmed: the graded ring block (the 113-case sweep bit for bit, 26 more worlds against the boundary elements, the estimate above every port entry), the rod-end warning once per solve, diagnose(), the costs, the cross-product axis distance, the 1.5 R and 30° switches for wires that do not meet, the thin-cover limit, the transfer estimate wherever the model holds (1.06 … 6.3 times the error), and the AP1 station and house mesh bit-identical in their radius correction.

    Streams fw4r3a and fw4r3b: fixes of audits W4R3A and W4R3B¶

    • fw4r3b, wires end to end (W4R3B-01): pieces in line with a gap below 5 R run beside each other with the gap as their distance: a smaller R (gap / 5), or a refusal before meshing (stacked 8 mm rods up to 8.8 cm apart). Collinear pieces meet only without a gap, and a collinear piece of another port counts only where the wires meet. The coordinator first took the stream's factor 3; at 10 … 18 cm it left the transfer entry 1.15 … 1.18 times its estimate, so the factor is 5: 10 … 30 cm apart every entry lies within its estimate (R12 −2.6e-3 … −3.7e-3, 10 cm 0.47 million nodes). A rod cut into touching pieces is bit-identical.
    • fw4r3b, wires that meet (W4R3B-02, -04): wires meet where their surfaces touch, with flat ends (crossings within a1 + a2; an encased wire by its shell radius), and the window fades out to a surface gap of a1 + a2 (the cross continuous, −2.4e-5 against mom −1.6e-5). Wires that meet at 10° or more are left alone (a V of 10 … 35° on the trend of mom, polyline and two strips alike to 2e-5). The literal rule of the brief would have made wires touching along their length one conductor, with port entries that are not physical, and would have reached the D9 service drops of the generators; the coordinator set a minimum angle of 10°, below which such wires are refused as before.
    • fw4r3b, the own chain (W4R3B-03): counted whole up to a turn of 90° accumulated along it, fading linearly to 0 at 150° (the meander −1.24e-4, the spiral +5.9e-5, the 0.5 m V across 120° +3.30e-4, each next to mom; rings move by −6.9e-6 / −4.4e-7).
    • fw4r3a, mesh features (W4R3A-01): within 1e-9 of max(1 m, x) they are one line, keeping the coordinate of the physical feature (a ring touching a 1000 times more conductive layer moves by 2.6e-3 per nanometre of the interface); interfaces are cut at their own line; a zero-area triangle is an internal error. 276 gap solves lie within 1.7e-6; the 113-case sweep is bit-identical.
    • fw4r3a, the reach (W4R3A-02): the resistive side re-measured above and below the wire of five geometries with the converged thin wire (1.3 / 1.9 / 2.2 / 2.2 / 2.3 a at 3 / 10 / 33 / 100 / 1000); the conductive node at 1000 is 4.3 a; cut wires are reported from 1.3; no false positive over 727 tests, T01–T10 and 48 golden cases. The conductive nodes stay as measured at one segment length; converged, every distance lies inside them (by 0.004 … 0.17 a), so the coordinator kept the table.
    • Golden: 563 of 563 quantities identical in both streams; the AP1 station, the house mesh and the bonded L / T bit-identical.

    Final documentation pass (author's request of 2026-11-16)¶

    Before the release the author asked for a documentation that reads well, renders every formula on GitHub Pages, documents the whole public API, has getting-started examples, explains the physics, mathematics and numerics behind the methods, says when which engine is the better choice and where its limits are, and carries three case studies. Six streams worked on separate files (docstart, doctheory, docapi, docex1 … docex3), the coordinator integrated them and checked the formulas.

    • docstart: the home page, getting started (rod, ring, grid in homogeneous and two-layer soil, a fault with touch and step voltage, a profile, plots, every number asserted), concepts, glossary, units, and the decision guide Choosing an engine (decision path, accuracy against the benchmarks, cost measured on this machine, one table per engine of where it must not be used). It fixed the stale "uniform per electrode" of the engine table (one current per straight wire since Q30).
    • doctheory: one skeleton for the five engine pages (what it solves, method, numerics, accuracy, limits, the audit trail collapsed), the theory pages derived step by step, known limitations and warnings as one short entry per limit with the remedy. It found and fixed four errors (the skin-depth range, fem's support for return_to, junctions and voltage sources, a stale size notice, the quadrupole bound) and reported four outside its files (ADR-0005's skin depths, a code comment, the cim / bem names in an error message, the fem3d extra on the how-to index), which the coordinator fixed.
    • docapi: every name of the public __all__ lists (92 modules, 864 entries) is rendered with a docstring summary; 62 were on no page and 77 had no summary (constants and type aliases documented only by #: comments). tests/test_docs_api_coverage.py keeps it so.
    • docex1 … docex3: the case studies E01 (secondary substation and its LV network: 9.9 Ω alone, 0.29 Ω with 30 houses), E02 (single-family house: concrete moisture, rod, outer ring, image / mom / fem) and E03 (60 m × 40 m substation grid in two-layer soil against EN 50522, with the measures that make it pass). E02 found that a bare ring bonded 1 m outside a foundation mesh reads the touch voltage +5 … +16 % high with the image family (now in the known limitations and the safety docstring); E03 found the corner gap of the touch-voltage envelope below and the larger field errors of image_2layer over a resistive bottom (+45 % at mesh centres).
    • The envelope at the corners (author's decision): the 24 equally spaced directions of touch_voltage_envelope hit the rounded corners of the offset curve only for a square; the 60 m × 40 m grid read 12 % / 28 % low, on the unsafe side. The envelope now also stands on the arc around every corner of the convex hull of the wires (nine feet per corner, corners=True); on that grid it equals the maximum over 3600 directions to 1e-4. Square grids, rings and rods keep their maximum; corners=False reproduces the directions alone; no golden case uses the envelope.
    • Review W5E of the envelope fix: the collinearity tolerance of the hull grew with the absolute coordinates; at UTM coordinates a 20 m × 1 m outline collapsed to one straight wire and the maximum read 1.2 % low (W5E-01; the hull is built relative to the lower-left corner of the footprint). Two text corrections (W5E-02, -03). Checked correct: every corner foot at the distance to 1e-15 over many shapes and distances, the default within −0.07 % of dense sampling at the origin-scale coordinates.
    • Round 2, W5R2 (author's request): the fix holds — no maximum changes with the coordinates, no arc is lost, no sweep flips (425 705 hull vertices). Two low findings, both fixed: at projected coordinates the tolerance lay below the coordinates' round-off, so edge nodes of rotated outlines (also generated foundations) became corners and the number of feet depended on the coordinate system (64 instead of 44 at UTM; the maximum unchanged; W5R2-01: the tolerance includes 16 ulp of the largest coordinate). Five feet per 90° arc read a narrow outline 0.22 % low between two feet (W5R2-02: nine per corner, every probe within 0.001 %).
    • Formulas: a headless-Chromium render of the built site under the mike version path with the vendored MathJax found three formulas left raw (two docstrings split by a line that Markdown read as a list item), raw TeX in the table of contents of four headings with math, and a \texttt with an underscore. All fixed: 99 pages, 4540 formulas typeset, none with an error; tests/test_docs_math.py guards the three classes.
    • On the way: ruff format --check, a blocking CI step, failed on T08 and two test files on the base; fixed. The golden rebase (D8) had removed the v0.15.0 world JSON that tests/test_soil_models.py read; it is a fixture now. DIN 18014 is cited in its edition 2023-06.
